
### Paper [1]
Title: Quantitative Gaussian limits of periodic O(N) models at and below the critical temperature
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14570
Authors: Weiqi Jiang, Pengcheng Hou, Zhiyi Li
Abstract: We prove a volume-uniform Gaussian approximation for local spin coordinates in the zero-field nearest-neighbour $O(N)$ model on periodic boxes of side $M$ in $d\ge3$, at and below the critical temperature of the limiting spherical model. On a fixed spatial window, for sufficiently large $M,N$, the unnormalized $1$-Wasserstein error for the first $p\le N$ coordinates is at most $C\sqrt p (N^{-1/2}+s_M)$, where $s_M\to0$ is the spectral shift of the finite-volume mean spherical model. The estimate is uniform on compact ranges of positive directional couplings and inverse temperatures. The target has independent coordinate fields, each a scaled anisotropic Gaussian free field with an independent spatially constant Gaussian term below criticality.
  On a window containing a nearest-neighbour star (a site and its $d$ positive-direction neighbours), the error tends to zero as $M,N\to\infty$ exactly when $p=o(N)$ and $\sqrt p s_M\to0$. For any choice of periodic infinite-volume accumulation states, the error on any nonempty fixed window tends to zero as $N\to\infty$ exactly when $p=o(N)$. In $d\ge5$, when the inverse temperature exceeds its critical value by at least a fixed positive amount, the bound improves to $C(p/N+\sqrt p M^{-d})$. For fixed $p$, the sharp order is $N^{-1}+M^{-d}$ on star-containing windows and $N^{-1}$ on every nonempty fixed window in periodic accumulation states.

### Paper [2]
Title: Rational layers in the low temperature expansion of the three-dimensional Ising model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14577
Authors: Ran Huang
Abstract: A low-temperature expansion orders Ising droplets by wall area, but droplets with different transverse spans can occur at the same order. We instead organize the zero-field simple-cubic model by the excess above the minimum wall area compatible with two prescribed spans. We prove that every fixed excess layer, including all logarithmic cover corrections and Euler inversion, has a rational bivariate generating function with denominator factors restricted to 1 - x, 1 - y and 1 - x - y. The proof compresses repeated coordinate cuts into bounded decorations and assigns each unbounded interval to one monotone chain. A finite controller then enforces induced contacts and arbitrary cover-color reuse. We explicitly assemble the complete layers of excess zero through three over all widths, attributing their known geometric constituents to earlier polyomino enumeration. Exact lattice checks corroborate the formulas. A certified low-temperature comparison shows a modest hybrid accuracy gain at one sampling point and higher evaluation cost. The result establishes an exact layer structure; summation over unbounded excess remains a separate problem.

### Paper [3]
Title: Little-Parks Oscillations and Parity Effects in Mesoscopic Superconducting Rings
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14597
Authors: Iogann Tolbatov, Luca Salasnich
Abstract: We analytically investigate the Little-Parks (LP) effect, the periodic oscillation of the superconducting transition temperature $T_c$ driven by an applied magnetic flux $\Phi$, from the weak-coupling Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer (BCS) limit to the intermediate-coupling regime in a mesoscopic, quasi-one-dimensional ring. Utilizing a Ginzburg-Landau (GL) expansion with twisted boundary conditions, we correctly incorporate both the discrete finite-size topological parity interference and the continuous center-of-mass (CM) kinetic energy shift induced by the supercurrent. We derive a closed-form expression for $T_c(\Phi)$ valid within the GL framework across variable interaction strengths. We demonstrate that the LP response comprises two distinct physical contributions: a discrete topological parity effect governed by the correlation length ratio $\xi/R$, and a continuous kinetic energy term scaling as $1/R^2$. As the coupling increases and the spatial correlation length of the pairs contracts ($\xi \ll R$), the constituent pairs become geometrically blind to the global boundary constraint, leading to an exponential suppression of the discrete parity modulation. However, the requirement that the condensate acquires a flux-dependent macroscopic CM momentum persists. This ensures that the critical temperature $T_c$ maintains an algebraic LP modulation driven predominantly by the macroscopic kinetic energy of the supercurrent. We bound our theoretical approach by discussing the breakdown of mean-field GL validity before phase fluctuations dominate near the strong-coupling limit.

### Paper [4]
Title: Carbon-made Lobachevsky surfaces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14602
Authors: R. Gabbrielli, P. Pais, P. Castorina, T. P. C. Klaver, D. Legut, O. De Luca, R. G. Agostino, A. Iorio
Abstract: Carbon-made surfaces of constant negative Gaussian curvature realize portions of the Lobachevsky plane in the laboratory, with motivations ranging from analogue gravity, where the elliptic, Beltrami and hyperbolic pseudospheres correspond to the three static phases of the Ba\~{n}ados--Teitelboim--Zanelli black hole, to water desalination. We review and extend their construction around a single principle, the balanced Gauss--Bonnet theorem: the curvature fixed by the topology is shared among disclination defects, elastically deformed hexagons and boundaries. Curvature without defects cannot change the topology: a sphere made of hexagons only is a punctured sphere, as an explicit polyhedral construction shows. The arrangements of the defects on the three pseudospheres stem from the elliptic, parabolic and hyperbolic one-parameter subgroups of $SL(2,\mathbb{R})$: we construct them for the elliptic family, recover the known one of the Beltrami pseudosphere, and propose one for the hyperbolic pseudosphere. We compare these ideal arrangements with carbon structures obtained by design and by simulated nano-extrusion, and suggest that the infinitely many Fuchsian groups may correspond to the infinitely many Lobachevsky surfaces.

### Paper [5]
Title: Singlet-triplet superconductivity from repulsive interactions in a $p$-wave magnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14609
Authors: Hanbit Oh, Laura Classen, Daniel F. Agterberg
Abstract: Motivated by recent proposals of $p$-wave magnets, which host odd-parity spin splitting, we study electrons near Van Hove singularities with local repulsive interactions. We demonstrate how the symmetry of the $p$-wave spin splitting governs the competition among instabilities. Using a parquet renormalization group (pRG) analysis of a simple one-sublattice model of a $p$-wave magnet, we find that superconductivity is the leading instability over the entire phase diagram for purely repulsive interactions. The resulting superconducting state is generically a mixture of spin-singlet and spin-triplet pairing and approaches an equal-weight mixture when $T_c$ is small compared with the energy offset between each Van Hove electron and its inversion partner. Extending the pRG analysis to a more realistic two-sublattice model, we find that superconductivity remains the leading instability, while the sublattice degree of freedom leads to a competition among various singlet-triplet pairing channels and even a finite-momentum equal-spin pair-density wave. Our mechanism applies broadly to odd-parity magnets and opens a natural route to nodal topological superconductivity.

### Paper [6]
Title: Symmetry-Enabled Rolling of Composite Bosons: A Route to Superconductivity from Strong Repulsion with $T_c$ Proportional to the Electron Bandwidth
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14615
Authors: Zhiyu Dong
Abstract: Motivated by superconductors discovered in systems with a strong Coulomb repulsion, we ask whether such a repulsion can give rise to superconductivity with a transition temperature $T_c$ bounded only by the electron bandwidth. Coulomb repulsion can produce pairing as it binds electrons to holes. If each hole binds three electrons into a three-electron-one-hole (3e1h) composite, the condensation of these bosonic composites can give rise to superconductivity. However, the resulting $T_c$ falls far below the electron bandwidth in the controlled regime where the binding is much stronger than the carriers' kinetic energy. This is because, on a lattice, a tightly bound composite usually has to move through a higher-order process where the intermediate state costs an energy of order the binding energy, so that the strong binding makes the composite heavy, limiting the superfluid stiffness and hence $T_c$. Here, we show that symmetry offers a way around this tradeoff. In lattices satisfying certain symmetry requirements, a composite can move by single-particle hops without any costly intermediate state, a motion we call rolling. Such rolling keeps the mass of the composite finite even for an infinitely strong binding, so that a $T_c$ proportional to the electron bandwidth can be achieved in the controlled regime. We derive a criterion for rolling and apply it to construct all fourteen minimal lattice models that support such rolling. We show that rollable 3e1h composites can indeed be stabilized in the ground state. We then propose to realize such superconductivity in a dual-gate bilayer, and use exact numerics to show that $T_c$ can reach a few percent of the electron hopping. These results establish rollability as a guiding principle in the search for and design of superconductors in strongly repelling electron systems, with the minimal models constructed here as prototypes.

### Paper [7]
Title: Hidden universality of rare fluctuations and a central-limit structure at criticality
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14621
Authors: Nikolaos G. Fytas, V\'ictor Mart\'in-Mayor, Attilio L. Stella, Gianluca Teza, Alexandros Vasilopoulos, David Yllanes
Abstract: The central limit theorem is among the most fundamental principles in the natural sciences, governing the fluctuations of sums of weakly correlated random variables through Gaussian statistics. At critical points, however, long-range correlations invalidate this paradigm, raising a fundamental question: does any general limit theorem survive at criticality? Although scale invariance has long been recognised as the defining feature of typical critical fluctuations, the behaviour of rare fluctuations has remained largely unexplored, as their extraordinarily small probabilities place them beyond the reach of conventional numerical methods. Recent theoretical advances suggest that universal asymptotic laws {may govern large deviations in systems at criticality}, yet direct numerical evidence has been lacking. Here we provide the first such evidence in the two-dimensional Ising universality class. Using large-scale Tethered Monte Carlo simulations, we probe order-parameter fluctuations with probabilities as small as $10^{-200}$ and show that, once normalised by their characteristic width, the far tails collapse onto a single asymptotic law shared by distinct microscopic models in the same universality class. This collapse is governed by a universal amplitude, whose numerical extraction is compatible with the conjectured exact result we derive from conformal field theory, and points towards a generalised central limit theorem for critical fluctuations.

### Paper [8]
Title: Quantum Hall Fabry-P\'erot Interferometry in Monolayer WSe$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14633
Authors: Himadri Chakraborti, Charles L. Tschirhart, James Fang, Saisab Bhowmik, Chengrui Yu, Shujian Cao, William Caro, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Jie Shan, Kin Fai Mak
Abstract: Electronic Fabry-P\'erot interferometers provide a powerful probe of coherent quantum Hall edge transport, yet the presence of multiple spin and valley degrees of freedom in existing van der Waals material platforms can complicate their interpretation. Monolayer WSe$_2$ offers a distinct opportunity because strong spin-orbit coupling and spin-valley locking lift these degeneracies, producing intrinsically single-component Landau levels. Here we realize the first gate-defined quantum Hall Fabry-P\'erot interferometer in monolayer WSe$_2$. By continuously tuning the cavity size in a single device, we observe robust Aharonov-Bohm (AB) interference of quantum Hall edge channels across the interferometer; the observed predominance in the AB regime is consistent with a weak bulk-edge coupling ($\Delta \approx 0.034$) estimated by electrostatics. Moreover, temperature and bias dependence measurements consistently yield an edge velocity $v \approx (4.9 \pm 0.2) \times 10^4\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-1}}$. Our results establish monolayer WSe$_2$ as a clean, flavor-resolved platform for quantum Hall interferometry and provide a foundation for future studies of single-component fractional quantum Hall states and interferometric probes of anyonic statistics in transition-metal dichalcogenides.

### Paper [9]
Title: A discrete realization of the boundary sine-Gordon model and the Schmid transition
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14637
Authors: Nicolas Paris, Oscar Bouverot-Dupuis, Christophe Mora
Abstract: The phase diagram of the boundary sine-Gordon model (bsG), or equivalently of the resistively shunted Josephson junction, has been the subject of recent debate. We introduce a discrete model that reproduces exactly the properties of the bsG model and can be simulated extremely efficiently using cluster Monte Carlo algorithms. This enables us to explore a broad range of parameters and compute the complete universal crossover between the weak- and strong-tunneling regimes. We establish the self-duality of the model and unambiguously identify the vertical line of critical fixed points characteristic of the Schmid transition, recovering the conformal field theory predictions along this line with high accuracy. We further clarify the essential distinctions between the Schmid and Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless transitions. Our results definitively establish the existence and nature of the Schmid transition and demonstrate the validity of the Schmid-Bulgadaev scenario.

### Paper [10]
Title: Superlattice Stabilization of the Topological Quantum Hall Ferromagnet in Graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14679
Authors: Tiago Cecchi, Ahmed Khalifa, Shubhayu Chatterjee
Abstract: Competition between spin and valley orders makes graphene a versatile platform for engineering correlated topological phases in the quantum Hall regime. We show that a tunable superlattice potential reorganizes this competition, and stabilizes the ferromagnetic quantum Hall topological insulator at charge neutrality without requiring a large in-plane magnetic field or strong dielectric suppression of Coulomb interactions. The interaction anisotropies responsible for choosing between different competing states are first computed by a generalized renormalization group procedure that accounts for the superlattice potential. We find that the potential suppresses anisotropies that compete with spin-polarization. At the same time, the potential introduces dispersion which prefers a partly spin-polarized compensated semi-metal. Therefore, we carry out a self-consistent momentum-space Hartree-Fock computation with renormalized aniostropies to establish the regime of stability of the insulating spin-polarized ferromagnet. Finally, we verify that this ferromagnet is adiabatically connected to the quantum Hall ferromagnet, thereby confirming its topological character. Our results establish superlattice potentials as a means of controlling the phase diagram of interacting graphene and accessing its helical quantum Hall regime.

### Paper [11]
Title: High-Fidelity In Silico Dataset of Ordered Binary and Ternary MXenes with Predictive Synthesis Guidance
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14687
Authors: Ahmed H. Biby, Charles B. Musgrave
Abstract: Despite their promise, the targeted synthesis of novel MXenes remains challenging due to their vast structural and compositional space and the lack of reliable stability descriptors that account for synthesis conditions. To address this gap, we utilized density functional theory (DFT) with the r$^2$SCAN functional to develop a high-fidelity dataset of 1,040 ordered binary and ternary bare delaminated MXenes, including mono-transition metal MXenes, in-plane ordered double-transition metal MXenes, in-plane ordered metal-divacancy MXenes, and out-of-plane ordered double-transition-metal MXenes. For each structure, we calculated formation and decomposition Gibbs energies, decomposition products, layer symmetry groups, and energies above the hull. We find that all MXenes are thermodynamically inaccessible under equilibrium conditions. However, 115 predominantly carbide MXenes become thermodynamically stabilized at elevated temperatures (500--2000 K), and 23 are stabilized under high pressure (10 GPa). Additionally, three MXenes in the Mn--Fe--C chemical space are stabilized within an experimentally feasible range of carbon chemical potentials. These findings reveal stability trends across the MXene space and identify synthesis strategies that can guide experimental efforts. Our dataset provides a robust foundation for developing machine-learned stability descriptors and for guiding experimental efforts toward novel MXene discovery. By identifying synthesizable candidates along with their synthesis conditions, this work bridges a critical gap in data-driven approaches to MXene discovery and lays the foundation for future autonomous experimental workflows.

### Paper [12]
Title: Force-Dependent Change of Structural Behavior Upon Pulling a Grafted Flexible Polymer
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14720
Authors: J. Matthew Smith, Michael Bachmann
Abstract: Manipulating polymer chains by means of experimental instruments such as optical tweezers has helped understand better the effects of molecular forces in macromolecular structures. In this study, we simulate the influence of a pulling force on structural phases of a coarse-grained model for flexible polymers using generalized-ensemble Monte Carlo methodologies. The recently introduced microcanonical inflection-point analysis method is employed to identify and classify structural phases of the system. Specific order parameters help characterize the transition behavior in response to the present pulling force. We show that, upon increasing the pulling strength, the coil-globule transition turns from second to first order, then merges with the liquid-solid transition. An intermediate phase, separating the coil and globule phases, is identified for certain force strengths. Clear evidence of the Gough-Joule effect is found as well. Transitions do not occur at all if the pulling force exceeds a certain threshold value.

### Paper [13]
Title: Fatigue crack growth without plasticity under cyclically reversed shear
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14722
Authors: E. A. Jagla
Abstract: We show that cyclic fatigue can occur in a system whose elements behave elastically up to an irreversible failure threshold, without plasticity, friction, or any time-dependent process. The model represents a weak interface between two elastic bodies loaded in shear. Under sufficiently large cyclic loading below the static failure threshold, a pre-existing flaw advances progressively, by a few lattice spacings per cycle. Two conditions are needed for this behavior: each bond fails more easily under one sense of the shear than the opposite, and the shear must be reversed during the cycle by an amount large enough to break bonds that have not yet failed. The model thus identifies the minimal conditions for an intrinsic, plasticity-free fatigue mechanism. Such conditions may be met at interfaces and weak planes subjected to strongly reversed shear, as in fretting, rolling contact, and reversed torsion, or in cyclic shear of cemented granular materials.

### Paper [14]
Title: Susceptibility Bottlenecks in Equilibrium Propagation Using Oscillator Ising Machines
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14727
Authors: Malihe Farasat, Oshada Rathnayake, Nikhil Shukla
Abstract: Equilibrium propagation (EP) enables learning in physical systems by exploiting the displacement between free and weakly perturbed equilibrium states. Here, we investigate how the underlying oscillator dynamics govern the transmission of learning signals in oscillator Ising machines (OIMs). We show that the hidden-layer response can be decomposed into two distinct factors: the nudging force applied at the output and the susceptibility governing its transmission to the hidden layer. Using a bipartite OIM with interactions interpolating between phase-difference and phase-sum coupling, we investigate these factors while ensuring that all coupling forms realize the same Ising Hamiltonian at binary phase-locked states. We demonstrate that second-harmonic injection primarily influences the nudging force through phase binarization, whereas the coupling form controls the cross-layer susceptibility through the mixed curvature of the energy landscape. At equal mixing, the mixed curvature vanishes on the binary manifold, strongly suppressing hidden-layer learning signals. Numerical simulations validate these mechanisms and reveal their consequences for classification performance. Our findings establish that realizing a desired energy landscape alone is insufficient to ensure effective physical learning; the system must also support the transmission of perturbations required for learning.

### Paper [15]
Title: Beyond Ground States: Learning Many-Body Hamiltonians for p-Bit Invertible Arithmetic
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14744
Authors: Tian Feng, Xutang Fan, Yiyun Chen
Abstract: Probabilistic-bit (p-bit) invertible logic encodes a Boolean relation as an energy whose ground states are its legal assignments, computing in any direction by sampling. Traditional constructions target ground-state membership and a wide minimum energy gap, yet finite-time samplers traverse excited states. We cast Hamiltonian design as inverse statistical mechanics: fit an energy with interactions up to order four whose Gibbs law approaches uniformity on each clamp case's legal states, under a gap floor keeping every legal state below every illegal one. Constrained maximum likelihood with pruning and refitting fits terms from a fixed pool, and every delivered energy is audited exactly on all cases. Each energy is scored by time to a verified solution under restarts with its best of nine tuned samplers. In underdetermined modes, learned multipliers factor in 0.55, 0.44, 0.52 and 0.46 [0.38, 0.56] of the hand-written encodings' updates from 4x4 to 7x7, faster within every family below 7x7 at no resolved work penalty; learned adders solve the reverse mode in 0.71 of the updates at 8 bits and about a quarter at 24 and 32 bits, at 0.55 of the work at 32 bits. At 5x5 under equal pool, budget and floor, a gap-maximizing fit reaches 0.79 [0.73, 0.88] in updates and 1.26 in work against the hand-written encoding, while likelihood fitting takes 0.55 [0.46, 0.58] and 0.58 [0.48, 0.61] of the gap fit's updates and work. Rounding coefficients to the narrowest exact integer words tested, we run both energies at each pair's common word on one FPGA color-sweep SSA core, bit-exact with software: learned multipliers take 0.41 of the hand-written encoding's cycles at 4x4 and 0.057 [0.045, 0.068] at 7x7, at up to 2.4 times its lookup tables. The method requires only a circuit's legal assignments; in determined modes, hand-written encodings take less work, and from 12 bits on the adder fewer updates.

### Paper [16]
Title: Zeroth persistent homology of ground-state correlations at clean and disordered quantum transitions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14797
Authors: Francisco Belch\'i, Nahuel Statuto
Abstract: The antiferromagnetic Ising chain in transverse and longitudinal fields exhibits a continuous critical point, a mixed-order transition line and, with quenched disorder, an infinite-disorder fixed point and reentrant order. Using exact free-fermion solutions and the density-matrix renormalization group, we examine what the zeroth persistent homology of staggered ground-state correlations adds to conventional responses. Its barcode records the merge scales of the single-linkage hierarchy, and the maximum of its persistence-landscape velocity defines a threshold-free pseudocritical point. This point locates the clean critical point and the mixed-order line with an accuracy comparable to conventional responses. In the reentrant regime, the global velocity misplaces the entry, which is recovered only by restricting the filtration to the ordering scale, and none of the tested estimators locates the exit. A domain wall forced by the boundary conditions suppresses the disorder-averaged reentrant lobe. For individual disordered realizations, the labelled merge tree recovers most strong-disorder renormalization-group clusters, including noncontiguous ones missed by lattice-restricted hierarchies, with increasing agreement at stronger disorder. This information resides in the full correlation matrix, while zeroth homology provides a stable, threshold-free hierarchy.

### Paper [17]
Title: Controlling the Vacuum: Tunable Magnonic Casimir Effect in Magnetic Moir\'e Systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14809
Authors: Alejandro Roldan, Dante Navarrete, Mario A. Castro, Sebasti\'an Allende, Roberto E. Troncoso, Alvaro S. Nunez
Abstract: We prove that the quantum fluctuations of the magnetic degrees of freedom of moir\'e structures can be tuned by adjusting the moir\'e lattice. Using the Bistritzer-MacDonald approach, we explore the consequences of this fact for the magnonic vacuum energy and the magnonic Casimir force, which become functions of the moir\'e pattern parameters. This allows a prototypical engine to be run with the vacuum fluctuations as a working agent and, in that way, to furnish a novel type of magneto-mechanical effect. Our calculations focus on the moir\'e Ladder as an example but can be generalized to any moir\'e structure in a straightforward fashion. We anticipate that our results belong to a generic property of moir\'e structures, regardless of the physical nature of the excitations at hand.

### Paper [18]
Title: Direct imaging of Su-Schrieffer-Heeger edge modes in a disordered suspended mechanical oscillator chain
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14826
Authors: Defeng Lu, Rohan Modi, Nicolas Delnour, Michael Hilke
Abstract: We report a reconfigurable macroscopic mechanical realization of the Su-Schrieffer-Heeger (SSH) model: a suspended chain of alternating masses coupled by identical compression springs. The relevant SSH degree of freedom is the relative longitudinal displacement between neighboring masses. In this bond-coordinate basis, applying a constant shift and rescaling of the mechanical dynamical matrix maps a chain of $N$ blocks onto an (N-1)-site SSH chain. Using video tracking, we reconstruct bond-resolved spectra and mode profiles and compare them with predictions for an ideal finite SSH chain. The topological termination supports mid-gap modes localized near the boundaries, with the alternating-sublattice structure expected for an effective even-length SSH chain. Mass variations split the edge-mode frequencies and produce asymmetric mode profiles relative to the non-disordered predictions. This directly imageable platform enables studies of topological normal modes and disorder effects in a classical mechanical system.

### Paper [19]
Title: Soft material mechanical property degradation at high strain rates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14872
Authors: Victor Sanchez, Joseph Beckett, Aryan Shah, Sicong Wang, Jin Yang, Jonathan B. Estrada, Mauro Rodriguez Jr
Abstract: The rheometry of soft, tissue-like materials at ultra-high strain rates is critical to biomechanics, materials science, and medical engineering. One approach for characterizing high-rate material response is Inertial Microcavitation Rheometry (IMR), which infers viscoelastic constitutive behavior from the measured radius-time history of a laser-induced cavitation bubble. At these rates, materials may exhibit history-dependent behavior, including damage, fracture, and plasticity. However, conventional IMR analyses typically assume constant material properties throughout the cavitation event, limiting their ability to capture such changes. We present an IMR framework for identifying effective time varying material properties from measured cavitation radius histories. The framework allows the shear modulus, viscosity, or both to evolve during the event using normalized sigmoid histories. Synthetic tests recover prescribed constant and time varying material histories. Application to polyacrylamide and gelatin datasets yields effective material-property histories across formulations and experimental conditions. Viscosity decreased across the datasets, while the extent of shear modulus reduction varied with material and formulation. When both properties decreased, their reductions could occur at different stages of cavitation. This distinction in mechanical property degradation depends on both the property and its evolution during high-strain events.

### Paper [20]
Title: Optimal Driving between Nonequilibrium Steady States under a Dissipation Budget
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14891
Authors: Keisuke Sugie, Dimitri Loutchko, Tetsuya J. Kobayashi
Abstract: Driving between nonequilibrium steady states incurs two thermodynamic costs: excess entropy production (EP) resulting from changing control parameters, and housekeeping EP required to sustain steady currents. Conventional fixed-duration optimization minimizes the former while neglecting the latter. Here, we instead optimize excess EP over both the protocol and its duration subject to a fixed leading order of housekeeping-EP budget, thereby minimizing total EP for that budget constraint. In the slow-driving regime, accumulated housekeeping EP acts as an entropic time, mapping the problem to a geodesic optimization under a friction metric weighted by the steady-state EP rate. This resource-based geometry determines optimal paths and schedules for systems that continue to dissipate even in steady states, establishing an intrinsic trade-off between housekeeping and excess EPs. Near equilibrium, this framework further yields geometric lower bounds on total EP, attained with equipartition at the optimal budget given by a modified thermodynamic length. Numerical simulations of a three-state model for an ATP-driven calcium pump reveal distinct optimal protocols compared to fixed-duration driving, confirming the total-EP bounds.

### Paper [21]
Title: Higher-Order Exceptional Point Sensing with Rydberg Micro-Ensemble Arrays
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14893
Authors: Jun Zhang, Ya-Jun Wang, Zi-Han Li, Jin-Hang Fang, Luan-Yi Wei, Qing Li, Guang-Can Guo, Bang Liu, Li-Hua Zhang, Dong-Sheng Ding, Bao-Sen Shi
Abstract: Higher-order exceptional points arise from the coalescence of multiple eigenvalues and eigenstates, leading to enhanced spectral responses to weak perturbations. However, realizing and controlling such higher-order degeneracies in interacting many-body systems remains challenging due to the complex coupling among multiple interacting eigenstates. Here, we construct spatially structured microensemble arrays in cold Rydberg gas, where long-range interaction and spatial connectivity enlarge the effective non-Hermitian dimensionality, enabling higher-order exceptional points. By varying the spatial arrangement of these sites, we realize different orders of non-Hermitian exceptional points. We further show that interactions between Rydberg excitation sites can enhance the quantum Fisher information, thereby improving sensing sensitivity. The resulting higher-order exceptional points exhibit increasingly nonlinear phase-boundary responses, which can be further tuned by varying the measurement time and atom number. By tracking shifts of these phase boundaries, we achieve a sensitivity of 0.47(10) nV cm-1 Hz-1/2, surpassing the standard quantum limit. Our results establish Rydberg microensemble arrays as a platform connecting higher-order non-Hermitian criticality, many-body dynamics, and microwave sensing.

### Paper [22]
Title: Structural Relaxation in Supercooled Water: Hydrogen-Bond Exchange, Defect Propagation, and Power-Law Scaling
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14915
Authors: Biman Bagchi
Abstract: Molecular simulations of deeply supercooled water reveal an intriguing power-law relationship between structural relaxation time and the concentration of a specific three-coordinated hydrogen-bond defect, $3'=H^1O^2$. We develop a reaction--transport theory in which active $3'$ defects propagate through concerted hydrogen-bond rearrangements. An unsatisfied donor hydrogen forms a new bond, creating a transient five-coordinated intermediate; subsequent breaking of another bond transfers the defect to a neighboring molecule. The dynamics includes propagation, self-decay, and encounter-induced annihilation with five-coordinated defects. Independently acting defects yield the mean-field result $\tau_\alpha\propto c_{3'}^{-1}$. By contrast, concentration-dependent propagation, defect interactions, and correlations between successive network rearrangements can generate a larger, noninteger effective exponent $\alpha_D$. Analytical results and illustrative calculations on a four-connected network show how elementary exchange kinetics, finite defect lifetimes, and repeated exploration of local environments modify the relaxation efficiency and hence the observed power law.

### Paper [23]
Title: The metastability of icosahedral quasicrystals assembled from one-component systems with isotropic interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14921
Authors: Cosmin A. Dicu-Gohoreanu, Jonathan P. K. Doye, Eva G. Noya
Abstract: Icosahedral quasicrystals (IQCs) are long-range ordered materials that lack translational periodicity. While they have only been observed in multi-component metallic systems, simulations have shown that one-component IQCs can robustly self-assemble using oscillatory potentials. However, their thermodynamic stability remains an open question. Here, we employ direct coexistence simulations to estimate the melting line of the competing solids for one such family of potentials. We find that the IQCs are never thermodynamically stable within the explored parameter space, being consistently outcompeted by periodic crystalline phases. This metastability is primarily driven by the higher internal energy of the IQC relative to the competing solids. Our findings suggest that the observed formation of these single-component IQCs in simulations is likely a kinetic phenomenon rather than a thermodynamic one.

### Paper [24]
Title: Improving Nonequilibrium Thermal Conduction in CaF2 by Breaking the Phonon Bottleneck Effect
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14957
Authors: Mingde Qu, Minghuan Song, Yuxing Wang, Wudi Wang, Chenbo Zhang, Jiamin Quan, Jun Xu, Zhongwei Zhang, Jie Chen
Abstract: Heat dissipation in laser optical materials depends not only on their thermal conductivity but also on how absorbed energy is redistributed among various phonon modes. In calcium fluoride (CaF2), rapid energy transfer from hot electrons to weakly propagating longitudinal optical (LO) phonons can create a nonequilibrium phonon bottleneck effect that limits efficient heat removal. Here, we propose selective phonon excitation as an effective strategy to break this bottleneck. Combining electron-phonon scattering calculations, phonon Boltzmann transport analysis, and nonequilibrium molecular dynamics simulations, we identify LO phonons as the primary modes that preferentially receive energy from hot carriers, leading to energy accumulation due to their inefficient transport and relaxation. Guided by three-phonon scattering phase-space analysis, selective excitation of transverse acoustic (TA) modes can accelerate LO relaxation and promote energy transfer toward heat-carrying acoustic modes. While sustained LO-phonon excitation reduces the thermal conductivity by approximately 12%, additional TA-mode excitation improves heat conduction by approximately 21% compared to the state limited by the LO-phonon bottleneck, surpassing the unexcited value. These findings demonstrate that controlling interbranch energy redistribution can counteract phonon-bottleneck-induced heat accumulation and reveal a working route for active thermal management in laser optical materials through selective control of phonon populations.

### Paper [25]
Title: Nitride crystalline barrier epitaxial SIS Josephson junctions by MBE
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14968
Authors: Manas Verma, Anand Ithepalli, Amit Rohan Rajapurohita, Eegene Clara Chung, David A. Garcia-Wetten, Naomi Pieczulewski, Danqing Wang, John G. Wright, Archit Shah, Jimy Encomendero, Rishabh Singh, Chandrashekar Savant, Hangwang Yang, Anthony Hyatt, Jaehong Choi, Thomas Varghese, Kushagra Aggarwal, Paul B. Welander, Apurva Mehta, David Schuster, Valla Fatemi, Gregory D. Fuchs, Michael C. Hamilton, Hong X. Tang, David A. Muller, Huili Grace Xing, Debdeep Jena
Abstract: Superconductor-insulator-superconductor Josephson junctions with crystalline tunnel barriers have remained elusive by epitaxial techniques such as MOCVD or MBE that produce the junction materials and devices powering today's electronic and photonic systems like transistors and lasers. Here, we overcome the key materials hurdle to realize the first functional nitride crystalline barrier Josephson junctions by MBE. Despite an AlN barrier thickness of approximately 4.5 nm, the NbN/AlN/NbN trilayers exhibit coherent Josephson tunneling with critical current densities of approximately 17 A/cm2, an IcRn product of approximately 2.5 mV, and a superconducting gap voltage of approximately 3.7 mV with a BCS-like gap evolution with temperature. Hysteretic current-voltage characteristics and Fraunhofer current modulation with magnetic field establish underdamped SIS Josephson behavior. The realization of crystalline barrier Josephson junctions by MBE opens the doors to a richer materials palette with alloys and superlattice electrodes and tunnel barriers, as well as potential monolithic integration of superconducting quantum circuits with active electronic and photonic devices such as HEMTs and lasers.

### Paper [26]
Title: Reversal of the Anomalous Hall Effect by Directional In-Plane Electric Field in Strained Bilayer MoO
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14982
Authors: Wenpeng Wang, hang Shi, Xiangru Kong
Abstract: Layered magnetic materials with tunable valley and spin degrees of freedom hold promise for multifunctional spintronic devices. Here, we demonstrate that the orientation of an in-plane electric field enables selective switching of single-valley spin splitting and reversal of the anomalous Hall conductivity in strained bilayer MoO. In particular, electric fields along the a and b directions selectively lift the spin degeneracy at the X and Y valleys, respectively, while preserving it at the other valley. This valley-selective switching is accompanied by a redistribution of Berry curvature with opposite signs, giving rise to a reversible anomalous Hall response. These effects are realized in the energetically and dynamically stable AC2 stacking configuration, where biaxial strain induces Dirac-like band crossings that enable subsequent electric-field control. Meanwhile, the AC1 stacking configuration, which is also energetically and dynamically stable, exhibits rich strain-driven phenomena, including a transition from metal to topological insulator with quantized spin Hall conductivity, as well as valley polarization and piezomagnetism. Our findings reveal a combined effect of strain and electric fields for controlling valley-selective spin splitting and anomalous Hall responses, highlighting bilayer MoO as a promising platform for multifunctional spintronic and valleytronic devices.

### Paper [27]
Title: Interplay of the Fano-Andreev Effect and Nonlinear Screening in Electric and Thermoelectric Transport through a T-Shaped Double Quantum Dot
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14991
Authors: Sachin Verma
Abstract: We present a theoretical study of weakly nonlinear electric and thermoelectric transport through a T-shaped double quantum dot coupled to normal and superconducting leads. Nonlinear screening is treated within the characteristic-potential formalism, in which an internal electrostatic potential accounts for nonequilibrium charge redistribution on the lead-coupled central dot. The side-coupled dot induces the Fano-Andreev effect, which, together with nonlinear screening, significantly modifies the transport properties. Under voltage bias, the screening-induced level renormalization produces a pronounced bias asymmetry in the Andreev differential conductance. In response to a pure thermal bias, increasing the interdot coupling weakens the level renormalization and suppresses the quasiparticle thermoelectric response, leading to reduced thermovoltage and nonlinear Seebeck coefficient. When voltage and thermal biases are applied simultaneously, a finite subgap cross thermoelectric response emerges beyond the linear regime. The sign of this response is governed by the competition between the energy-dependent transport contribution, modified by the Fano-Andreev effect, and the nonlinear screening contribution, thereby determining whether a thermal bias enhances or suppresses the low-bias Andreev current.

### Paper [28]
Title: Observing Autler--Townes splitting of an rf-dressed Feshbach resonance
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15016
Authors: Samyajit Gayen, Matthew Chilcott, Chongwu Xie, Niels Kj{\ae}rgaard
Abstract: We experimentally implement the scheme introduced by Ding, D'Incao, and Greene [Phys. Rev. A {\bf 95}, 022709 (2017)] for controlling the scattering length near a magnetic Feshbach resonance using an rf dressing field. Specifically, we observe Autler--Townes splitting of the 1007 G Feshbach resonance in dipole-trapped $^{87}\mathrm{Rb}$ by coupling the associated quasibound molecular state to a deeper-lying bound state with a different spin projection. At a fixed magnetic field away from the bare resonance, we further demonstrate rf switching of the collisional response. Our experimental observable is atom loss, which is consistent with enhanced three-body recombination arising from an increase in the magnitude of the real part of the scattering length. Our observation of Autler--Townes splitting establishes the feasibility of generating the strong rf magnetic fields required for sufficient molecular dressing and provides an important basis for further experimental tests of the predicted elastic nature of the rf-controlled interactions.

### Paper [29]
Title: Termination-selective Kondo hybridization of topological surface states in CeCuAs$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15022
Authors: Bo Chen, Chen Zhang, Hao Liu, Qi-Yi Wu, Zhong-Tuo Fu, Ying Zhou, Chun-Hui Lyu, Yang Luo, Zi-Jie Xu, Hai-Long Deng, Zheng-Tai Liu, Mao Ye, Zhen-Hua Chen, Yao-Bo Huang, Sheng-Tao Cui, Zhe Sun, Da-Wei Shen, Jun He, Yu-Xia Duan, Jian-Qiao Meng
Abstract: Strongly correlated topological states in Kondo lattices represent a frontier in condensed matter physics, where the coupling between boundary electrons and localized f states can reshape the low-energy electronic structure. Here, we combine termination-resolved angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES), Ce 4$d \rightarrow 4f$ resonant photoemission, and surface electronic structure calculations to investigate the Kondo lattice compound CeCuAs$_2$. Two distinct surface electronic structures are assigned to the As1 and CeAs2 terminations through their characteristic Fermi-surface geometries and termination-specific calculations. On CeAs2, a Dirac surface state associated with the calculated nontrivial bulk topology coexists with a weakly dispersive Ce 4$f$ band near $E_F$. Resonant photoemission identifies the low-energy 4$f$ character and reveals crystal-field-related fine structure in the energy range traversed by the Dirac branch. On cooling, the 4$f$ and Dirac regions both gain spectral weight, whereas a reference conduction-band region changes much less. Together, these observations provide spectroscopic evidence for termination-selective $f$-Dirac hybridization and the development of 4$f$ character in a topological surface state. Our results identify surface termination as a microscopic means of controlling the interplay between Kondo correlations and topological boundary electrons.

### Paper [30]
Title: Momentum-Space Mismatch Enables Non-Hermitian Valley Filtering
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15036
Authors: Kai Shao
Abstract: Valley-selective transport is central to valleytronics, but existing valley filters are largely tailored to specific materials or lattice geometries, motivating the search for a common microscopic ingredient. Here we identify the momentum-space mismatch between the band-touching nodes of a system and its dissipative reservoir as a route to node-selective non-Hermitian skin effects (NHSE). When the two are coupled, the mismatch is encoded in the retarded self-energy, producing node-dependent dissipation and contrasting quasiparticle lifetimes. The resulting effective non-Hermitian Hamiltonian exhibits opposite point-gap spectral winding at the two nodes, giving rise to a node-resolved NHSE with strongly asymmetric dissipation rates: one node decays rapidly into the reservoir while the other survives over the device, yielding a nearly valley-polarized outgoing current. We demonstrate this mechanism in a minimal one-dimensional dimer chain and in two-dimensional systems hosting genuine Dirac valleys, including $\pi$-flux rectangular and honeycomb lattices. In all cases, transport calculations yield nearly 100\% valley polarization, while remaining gate-tunable and robust against disorder and edge imperfections. Our results show that momentum-space mismatch provides a route to non-Hermitian valley filtering.

### Paper [31]
Title: Depth determination from core-loss 4D-STEM
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15037
Authors: Michael Deimetry
Abstract: Moving beyond specimen-projected micrographs in scanning transmission electron microscopy to determine the three-dimensional distribution of dopants is of vital importance for materials characterization and development. We present an approach for dopant depth determination using momentum-resolved core-loss four-dimensional scanning transmission electron microscopy data. Within the Born approximation, Fourier transformation over probe position reveals fringes whose frequency encodes the dopant's distance from the focal plane. Two principal challenges arise in extending this approach to realistic experimental conditions. First, dynamical scattering degrades fringe contrast which can be partially mitigated by acquiring off major zone axes. Second, because core-loss doses are punishing, we use a Bayesian framework which, in simulation, achieves nanometre to sub-nanometre depth precision from only 100 to 1000 core-loss counts, depending on the signal-to-background.

### Paper [32]
Title: Defective $\alpha$-In$_2$Se$_3$ from first principles
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15060
Authors: Jorge Vega Bazantes, Timo Lebeda, Zhiqiang Mao, Jianwei Sun
Abstract: $\alpha$-In$_2$Se$_3$ is a two-dimensional van der Waals material with promising applications involving ferroelectricity. However, the presence of native defects can significantly influence its structural and electronic properties. In this work, we employ density functional theory with a state-of-the-art meta-generalized gradient approximation (meta-GGA) exchange-correlation functional to investigate the electronic structure of bulk $\alpha$-In$_2$Se$_3$ and the formation of neutral point defects, including vacancies and antisites, under Se-rich and In-rich growth conditions. Our calculations reproduce the experimental structural parameters of the pristine material and reveal that the Se vacancy in the middle layer exhibits an unexpectedly low formation energy under both chemical environments. Structural analysis shows that the neighboring tetrahedrally coordinated In atom undergoes a substantial local relaxation along the three-fold axis, which preserves its $C_{3v}$ site symmetry even as its coordination is reduced from four to three, providing a microscopic explanation for the remarkable stability of this defect.

### Paper [33]
Title: Maximally Chiral Eigenstate from Inverse Thermodynamic Bethe Ansatz
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15081
Authors: Tigran A. Sedrakyan
Abstract: We show that an ordered quantum state can support waves with a finite speed in one direction and exponentially small energies in the other. We prescribe this spectrum for a spin-$1/2$ Heisenberg eigenstate and reconstruct a nonlocal Hamiltonian. The thermodynamic construction selects the ordered, current-carrying state as a ground state. Its magnetic moment remains finite while its response to a weak field has a divergent slope. The entropy per spin vanishes only inversely with a logarithm of temperature, with an exact coefficient containing the golden ratio. Our spin-response calculations reveal a bound pair whose energy can be tuned without changing its intensity. Added exchange and smoother mode selection restore quadratic excitations at the lowest energies. These results connect the design of interactions to directional dynamics and unusual thermodynamics. We propose momentum-resolved spin spectroscopy, weak-field magnetometry, and low-temperature entropy measurements to probe these counterintuitive signatures.

### Paper [34]
Title: Magnon Chirality Selection at Equal Linewidths in Open Collinear Magnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15113
Authors: Dinghui Wang, Lulu Liu
Abstract: Loss can select one magnon chirality even when all resonances have the same linewidth. We construct a passive collinear mode network in which spatially correlated loss and coherent exchange give opposite angular momenta different survival probabilities. An initially unpolarized mixture reaches 26.9\% polarization with 29.9\% of the population remaining; an isospectral normal control gives none. A combined spin and crystal rotation reverses the signal and exchanges the exceptional contours reached at stronger loss. We organize the allowed pole pairings by the action of a non-Hermitian collinear spin-space little group on chirality and complex frequency. A second realization separates conjugate chirality sectors at opposite diagonal edges despite zero total winding. The two readouts distinguish constraints on poles from constraints on preparations, eigenvectors, and physical reservoirs.

### Paper [35]
Title: Magnetically Compensated Spin-Polarized Edge States in Coplanar Magnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15134
Authors: Dinghui Wang, Lulu Liu
Abstract: A boundary can release spin-polarized electronic states from a magnetically compensated, spin-degenerate layer. We establish the symmetry criterion governing this release in coplanar exchange magnets: the allowed even-parity edge-spin space equals the allowed uniform-magnetization space. Symmetry-protected compensation therefore selects an odd spin texture perpendicular to the moment plane. Six maximal sectors organize the possible edge textures. Orthogonal cuts of one exchange model realize odd and even sectors, while FeSe spin-vortex and NiI$_2$ period-four spiral Wannier models provide material realizations. Their semi-infinite boundaries exhibit complementary electrical responses: perpendicular spin accumulation at the odd edge and in-plane spin-current response at the even edge. Boundary symmetry thus connects magnetic compensation to equilibrium spin textures and electrical spin readout.

### Paper [36]
Title: High-Throughput Surface Screening Reveals Energetically Competitive Low-Symmetry Facets in Wurtzite Semiconductors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15138
Authors: Gustavo Madrigal, Cibr\'an L\'opez, Jordi Llorca, Claudio Cazorla
Abstract: Surfaces and interfaces govern the performance of wurtzite semiconductors in electronics, sensing, and solar-fuel conversion. To date, first-principles studies have focused mostly on high-symmetry low-index facets, owing to the computational cost and complexity of modelling low-symmetry high-index surfaces. In practice, however, surface formation energy is determined by the planar atomic density and the strength of the bonds severed upon cleavage, neither of which inherently excludes high-Miller-index surfaces from being energetically competitive. Here, we combine automated surface generation, machine-learning interatomic potential screening, and density functional theory to explore 226 clean, stoichiometric surface models of wurtzite GaN, LaN, and ZnO, spanning all Miller orientations with $|h|,|k|,|l| \le 3$ and their inequivalent terminations. We find that low-symmetry high-index prismatic facets are energetically competitive with respect to the conventional $m$- and $a$-planes: nine of the fifteen lowest-energy nonpolar surfaces are high-index, and the five best candidates of each compound lie within only 2.5-5.3% of the minimum surface energy. Hybrid-functional calculations reveal, however, that this energetic proximity does not translate into similar electronic alignment. In GaN, the two most stable surfaces differ by just 0.08 J m$^{-2}$ in formation energy but by almost 0.8 eV in vacuum-referenced band-edge positions, whereas in LaN the nearly degenerate $m$- and $a$-planes are separated by 0.16 eV. Wurtzite facets with similar formation energies may therefore exhibit very different ionization potentials and electron affinities. Our results enable the joint, systematic assessment of stability and band alignment across the facets that can actually occur in wurtzites, and provide a map of candidate surfaces for the rational design of related nanostructure and heterointerfaces.

### Paper [37]
Title: Higher-order topology induced by altermagnetism in quasicrystals
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15139
Authors: Xingmin Huo, Xingchuan Zhu, Xingyu Ma, Shiping Feng, Huaiming Guo
Abstract: Altermagnetism (AM), a novel magnetic phase featuring zero net magnetization and momentum-dependent spin splitting, provides a compelling platform for exploring exotic quantum phenomena. While recent studies have extended the concept of AM to quasicrystals, the momentum-dependent spin splitting in the absence of translational symmetry remains inadequately understood. Here, we investigate the emergence of higher-order topological phases (HOTPs) in Thue-Morse and Ammann-Beenker quasicrystals, driven by the coupling between $d$-wave quasicrystal AM orders and a two-dimensional topological insulator. We reveal that the $d_{x^2-y^2}$-wave AM exhibits a ferromagnetism-like aspect in opening edge gaps and inducing zero-energy higher-order topological corner states. In contrast, conventional antiferromagnetic and $d_{xy}$-wave AM configurations are unable to induce the higher-order topological gaps. These findings highlight the pivotal role of altermagnetic symmetry and its alignment with lattice corners in the realization of HOTPs and establish that the emergence of HOTPs can serve as a signature for identifying altermagnetic orders in aperiodic systems.

### Paper [38]
Title: Magnetism on emergent molecular-orbital lattices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15141
Authors: Zhan Liu, Chenrui Wang, Chen Fang
Abstract: In solids, strong hybridization between nearby atomic orbitals forms molecular orbitals (MOs), the range of which covers a cluster of atoms. The centers of molecular orbitals form an emergent lattice generally different from the atomic lattice. For partially filled MOs, electron interaction may induce a new type of magnetism on this emergent lattice where the local moments come from the molecular orbitals instead of single atoms. In this work, we show in a ruby-lattice tight-binding Hubbard model that frustrated and unfrustrated antiferromagnetism may appear on the same atomic lattice and at the same filling, as the emergent molecular-orbital lattice changes from triangular to honeycomb upon changing the hopping parameters. Moreover, we show that general molecular orbital magnetism may be experimentally verified in the low-energy dynamic structure factor: the molecular form factors, as the Fourier transforms of the molecular spin-density, modulate the low-energy dynamic structure factor across different Brillouin zones. Zeros of these form factors suppress low energy spectral weight irrespective of the magnetic order formed by the molecular moments. This is exemplified by random phase approximation calculations for the ruby-lattice model: the structure factor integrated over low energies exhibits minima around molecular form-factor zeros, while its ratios between selected equivalent momenta in different Brillouin zones agree with ratios of squared form factors.

### Paper [39]
Title: Quantum-Metric-Induced Distinct Optoelectronic Responses in Triple $M$ Valleys
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15145
Authors: Yongliang Song, Shuxian Sun, Zhichao Zhou, Xiao Li
Abstract: While $K_{\pm}$ valleys with contrasting optoelectronic properties have been the main focus of valleytronics, the lack of reliable manipulation methods for $M$ valleys leaves numerous $M$-valley materials untapped. Here, we discover that three flavors of $M$ valleys become distinguishable via quantum-metric-induced optoelectronic responses. The tight-binding calculations demonstrate that by tuning the electric-field orientation, opposite nonlinear Hall currents from any pair of $M$ valleys can be chosen to realize the nonlinear valley Hall effect. Moreover, linearly polarized light allows for preferential excitation of individual $M$ valleys, driving photoexcited valley-polarized Hall transport. Using first-principles calculations, we further validate the quantum-metric-induced optoelectronic responses in antiferromagnetic $\mathrm{MoI}_3$ bilayers, which exhibit a significant nonlinear valley Hall conductivity amenable to transport measurements. Our findings extend the scope of valleytronics from conventional binary $K_{\pm}$ valleys to triple $M$ valleys and pave the way for designing advanced optoelectronic devices with multiple quantum states of high tunability.

### Paper [40]
Title: Kitaev Exchange and Altermagnetism in the Double Perovskite Sr$_2$CoTeO$_6$: An Ab Initio Study
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15152
Authors: Alexey V. Ushakov, Sergey V. Strelsov
Abstract: The Co$^{2+}$-based double perovskite Sr$_2$CoTeO$_6$ attracts attention due to its nontrivial magnetic properties. \textit{Ab initio} density functional theory calculations reveal a substantial ferromagnetic Kitaev exchange $|K|\sim J/3$ between nearest neighbors that, combined with the face-centered cubic lattice, leads to pronounced exchange frustration and places Sr$_2$CoTeO$_6$ among Kitaev materials with moderate Kitaev interaction strength. In addition, Sr$_2$CoTeO$_6$ is found to be altermagnetic with a sizable non-relativistic spin splitting, which can give rise to a transverse piezomagnetic effect and a nontrivial magneto-optical response.

### Paper [41]
Title: Polymer rigidity controls conformational response to deformation-dependent nonreciprocal interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15183
Authors: Shalabh K. Anand
Abstract: Nonreciprocal interactions, which violate action-reaction symmetry, constitute a broadly applicable mechanism for generating nonequilibrium forces in active and driven systems. Here, we explore how such interactions remodel polymer conformations using a minimal model in which local strain along the backbone gives rise to nonreciprocal forces. We find that bending rigidity is the decisive control parameter: flexible polymers contract under activity, whereas semiflexible polymers display a non-monotonic response that ultimately yields extension. These large-scale conformational changes arise despite only weak shifts in local structural statistics. Instead, activity reassigns bond-orientational correlations along the contour, damping orientational memory in flexible chains while amplifying it in semiflexible ones. Collectively, our results show that bending rigidity governs how deformation-dependent nonreciprocal activity transmits across length scales, offering a mechanism to regulate the structure and mechanics of active filamentous matter.

### Paper [42]
Title: Probing Unknown Luttinger-Like Parameters and Band Structure in BAs: A 24-Band k.p and DFT Approach
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15186
Authors: Mohsen Jemai, Nouha Mastour, Omar Zitouni, Tarek Barhoumi, Said Ridene
Abstract: In this work, the electronic band structure and the unknown Luttinger-like parameters associated with the p-type conduction bands (CBs) of boron arsenide (BAs) are calculated and analyzed using both 24-band $k.p$ and density functional (DFT) theories. For the DFT study, Gaussian and plane-wave basis sets implemented in the CP2K code were used. Comparison with DFT and available experimental results shows that the 24-band k.p model accurately reproduces the known electronic properties of BAs. These results demonstrate that the 24-band k.p model accurately reproduces the electronic band structure and key band parameters over the investigated energy range. In addition, the 24-band $k.p$ Hamiltonian parameters are adjusted and we show that the effective masses and the band parameters are validated against previously published theoretical studies. These findings suggest that the $k.p$ method is computationally more efficient than DFT, making it suitable for modeling complex nanostructures and quantum wells based on BAs semiconductor.

### Paper [43]
Title: Interface-Driven Remote-Surface States in Rhombohedral Graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15192
Authors: Pierre A. Pantaleon, Alejandro Jimeno-Pozo, Francisco Guinea
Abstract: Rhombohedral multilayer graphene hosts low-energy states on its two outer surfaces. We identify a layer-by-layer mechanism through which a low-energy state generated at one surface acquires weight on successive layers up to the opposite surface. For an outer-layer vacancy, the wavefunction spreads over a larger area on successive layers, shifting normalized weight toward the remote surface as the system size increases. The same relation governs the extension of sublattice-polarized zero modes bound to circular voids and straight zigzag terminations. At periodic moire interfaces, it acts separately on each momentum harmonic, making the layer polarization of the low-energy bands depend on Bloch momentum and modulation period. This provides an intrinsic single-particle explanation for the remote-surface modulations observed in recent multilayer graphene experiments.

### Paper [44]
Title: Competing Octahedral Tilting and $B$-Site Off-Centering Govern the Phase Diagram of CsGe$_{1-x}$Sn$_x$Br$_3$ Perovskite Alloys
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15200
Authors: Apinya Ngoipala, Erik Fransson, Paul Erhart, Julia Wiktor
Abstract: Lead-free halide perovskites based on Ge and Sn avoid the toxicity of Pb, but the two cations favor different structural distortions. The stereochemically active Ge $4s^2$ lone pair drives polar $B$-site off-centering, whereas the larger Sn$^{2+}$ cation favors octahedral tilting. Here, we map how these two instabilities compete across the full composition range of CsGe$_{1-x}$Sn$_x$Br$_3$ using large-scale molecular dynamics simulations with a charge-aware machine-learned interatomic potential trained on hybrid-functional data. At low temperature the alloy exhibits a morphotropic phase boundary between $x=0.60$ and $0.65$ that separates a ferroelectric monoclinic $Cc$ phase from a monoclinic $P2_1$ phase with mixed antipolar and polar off-centering, with both the tilt pattern and the off-centering order changing discontinuously. The composition-temperature phase diagram reproduces the known transition sequences and temperatures of both end members and reveals an intermediate tetragonal $I4/mcm$ phase near the boundary that has no counterpart in either parent compound. Above the transition temperatures the $B$ cations remain locally off-centered but orientationally disordered. The structural transition at the boundary causes a jump in the band gap at low temperature, whereas at $350$ K the gap decreases smoothly with Sn content. These results establish $B$-site mixing as a means to tune polar and rotational order in lead-free halide perovskites.

### Paper [45]
Title: Chirality-Odd Readout of Coherence-Mediated Magnon Spin Transport
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15214
Authors: Yanmeng Lei, Ji Zou, Sanchar Sharma, Tao Yu
Abstract: Structural chirality is ubiquitous in magnets, yet its impact on spin transport in insulators remains largely unexplored, with only a few recent experiments offering the first glimpses. Here we predict the chiral response in the electric spin injection-detection configuration in both ferromagnetic and antiferromagnetic spirals. The screw symmetry of the spiral forbids any nonzero diagonal element of the magnon spin matrix: every eigenmode is linearly polarized and carries no spin, so spin transport is mediated entirely by superposition between distinct modes. Reversing chirality flips the magnon spin along the spiral axis and the injected quantum coherence in lockstep, leaving the associated injected spin current invariant. Spiral chirality acts instead at the detection interface, where electron--magnon scattering amplitude is reversed and causes the magnon accumulation to be read out as a voltage of opposite sign. This interfacial readout is consistent with reversed first-harmonic voltage under electric-field switching in multiferroic BiFeO$_3$, establishing structural chirality as an intrinsic control parameter for magnon coherence and its electrical readout.

### Paper [46]
Title: Steering Skyrmion Diffusion with Chiral Molecules
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15253
Authors: Palina Shaban, Ulrich Pototschnig, Gulnaz Rakhmanova, Mikhail Lemeshko, Ragheed Alhyder
Abstract: Chiral molecules adsorbed on a magnetic film interact with its magnetization and can thereby alter the thermal motion of magnetic skyrmions. Here we develop a theoretical model in which an aligned layer of tilted molecules modifies the interfacial magnetic interactions according to its handedness. Within this model a skyrmion responds only to spatial variations of the coupling, so a uniform layer leaves its position unaffected, whereas boundaries and coverage gradients exert forces on it. Near a chiral region, these forces deflect the skyrmion transversely in a direction that reverses between enantiomers, which provides a direct signature of the chiral interaction. In extended periodic patterns, the same forces reshape the long-time diffusion, suppressing it in the direction in which the potential varies and enhancing it in the perpendicular direction. The net effect on the mean diffusion is set by the balance between the gyrotropic and dissipative responses of the skyrmion, as we show exactly for one-dimensional landscapes. Stochastic micromagnetic simulations and collective-coordinate calculations confirm these predictions and show how molecular orientation, spatial patterning, and skyrmion properties together determine the motion.

### Paper [47]
Title: Periodic Trends in Oxygen Vacancy Formation Energies Across Binary Main-Group Metal Oxides
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15297
Authors: Manish Kumar, Bridget S. Ferris, Avital Isakov, Robert B. Wexler
Abstract: The function of oxide materials, from solid-oxide fuel cells to solar thermochemical fuel production, depends on their oxygen vacancies. Their formation energetics have been mapped systematically for ternary perovskites, but reported values for the binary oxides, their conceptual and thermochemical building blocks, remain scattered across studies with differing computational methods. Here we report a systematic r$^2$SCAN density functional theory survey of neutral oxygen vacancy formation energies across 39 binary main-group metal oxide polymorphs spanning groups 1, 2, 13, 14, and 15, using the ShakeNBreak method to locate reconstructed defect ground states. The formation energies span 8 eV and decrease down each group and, for most multivalent cations, with increasing oxidation state. Both trends follow the reducibility of the cation. An interpretable crystal feature model is fit to these energies; its inputs are properties of the pristine bulk, with the reduction potential alternatively taken from tabulated thermochemistry. Retaining only its two dominant descriptors, the crystal reduction potential and the band gap, the model reproduces the formation energies with a held-out mean absolute error of 0.31 eV, lower than that of its four-descriptor parent model for transition-metal perovskites. The bond-breaking and stability terms of the full Born-Haber fit prove statistically null for the binary oxides, and the largest residuals identify the hosts where electron accommodation departs from the cation-reduction picture that the descriptors encode. The dataset provides a benchmark for machine-learning interatomic potentials, and the model estimates vacancy formation energies of main-group oxides from bulk properties alone.

### Paper [48]
Title: Directed Domain Wall Motion by Radio-Frequency Currents
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15305
Authors: Lukas Fischer, Rouven Dreyer, Simon Arnold, Jae-Chun Jeon, Georg Woltersdorf, Stuart S. P. Parkin
Abstract: Magnetic domain walls (DWs) are central to emerging spintronic technologies for memory, logic and neuromorphic computing, where their controlled displacement is a key functionality. While current-induced DW motion driven by spin-orbit torques is well established under direct currents, it remains unclear how radio-frequency (RF) currents couple to DWs and whether they can generate directed motion under a symmetric periodic drive. Here we show that RF currents drive DW oscillations, which can be rectified into directed motion through symmetry breaking. Using time-resolved and static magneto-optical Kerr microscopy, we demonstrate that RF spin-orbit torques efficiently excite low-frequency DW oscillations well below the ferromagnetic resonance frequency. Whereas these oscillations remain symmetric in the absence of perturbations, symmetry breaking by a transverse magnetic field produces self-propulsion, while symmetry breaking by an initial current pulse gives rise to sustained DW motion. We further show that in initially uniform magnetic states, RF-driven expansion of a pulse-generated edge domain ultimately results in efficient RF-assisted magnetic switching. Together, these results establish a general framework for understanding how RF-driven DW oscillations are rectified into directed motion, providing new opportunities for their high-frequency control.

### Paper [49]
Title: Matrix-aware 2D field-effect biosensing of cardiac troponin I using complementary ion-sensitive transduction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15347
Authors: Subhabrata Das,  Bharti, Babulu Pradhan, Abhishek R Nath, Kashish Sapra, Jaydeep sharma, Vijayender Bhalla, Satyaprakash Sahoo, Manish Mathew, Md. Ehesan Ali, Kaushik Ghosh
Abstract: Cardiovascular diseases remain a leading cause of mortality worldwide, highlighting the need for rapid, sensitive and reliable detection of cardiac injury at the point of care. Here, we develop a dual-sensor platform that combines an aptamer-functionalized molybdenum disulfide (MoS2) field-effect transistor (2D-FET) with a graphene-based ion-sensitive field-effect transistor (ISFET) for the detection of cardiac troponin I (Trop-I) in complex biological matrices. The atomically thin MoS2 channel enables highly sensitive detection of Trop-I, achieving a limit of detection of 0.001 ng mL-1. However, the high ionic strength of serum substantially attenuates the field-effect response, resulting in approximately 80% degradation of the sensor response. To address this matrix effect, the ISFET is employed as a secondary sensor to characterize the ionic environment of the sample. Machine-learning-assisted integration of the 2D-FET and ISFET responses is investigated as a strategy for compensating serum-induced signal distortion and recovering key electrical figures of merit extracted from the 2D-FET transfer characteristics, including maximum on-state current (Ids_max), subthreshold slope (SS) and transconductance (gm). This dual-sensor architecture combines molecular recognition, high analytical sensitivity and matrix-aware electrical signal correction, providing a potential pathway towards more reliable FET-based biomarker detection in complex biological fluids. The approach may further enable multiplexed detection of cardiac biomarkers through incorporation of target-specific aptamers, offering potential for broader assessment of cardiac stress and myocardial injury risk.

### Paper [50]
Title: Interplay of Pair Density Wave and Uniform Superconductivity on the Kagome Lattice
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15354
Authors: Ruiqing Fu, Xianxin Wu, Ziqiang Wang, Sen Zhou
Abstract: We study the interplay between pair density wave (PDW) and uniform superconductivity on the kagome lattice within a single-orbital $t$-$W$-$J$ model near the $p$-type van Hove filling, where $W$ and $J$ denote effective pairing attractions on lattice sites and nearest-neighbor bonds, respectively. While onsite attraction favors zero-momentum pairing through the conventional BCS instability, nearest-neighbor attraction naturally promotes finite-momentum pairing due to the kagome geometry and sublattice interference. The model therefore provides a framework for exploring the competition and coexistence between uniform superconductivity and PDW order. Self-consistent mean-field calculations in a $2\times2$ supercell reveal that the uniform component reorganizes both the amplitudes and phase structure of the three $F_1$ PDW components, giving rise to superconducting states absent in the pure bond-pairing limit. A Ginzburg--Landau analysis identifies symmetry-allowed couplings that can favor this reorganization. The resulting states exhibit distinct charge modulations and quasiparticle spectra, while the chiral PDW undergoes topological transitions as the onsite attraction increases. Our results demonstrate how uniform superconductivity reshapes the order-parameter landscape and quasiparticle topology of a PDW state.

### Paper [51]
Title: Anisotropy driven critical phenomena in a van der Waals ferromagnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15380
Authors: Neesha Yadav, Harish Chandr Chauhan, Pintu Das
Abstract: We report a detailed investigation into the field-dependent critical behavior of the van der Waals ferromagnet $\mathrm{Fe_3GeTe_2}$ to elucidate how applied magnetic fields ($B$), strong magnetocrystalline anisotropy, and emerging other energy scales modulate its critical behavior. Utilizing the recently developed method, viz., the average of normalized slope (ANS), for analysis of critical behavior, we evaluate the critical exponents ($\beta$, $\gamma$, and $\delta$) across distinct field windows up to $\rm{7\,T}$. For $B$ applied along the crystal's easy axis ($B\parallel c$), the system exhibits long-range interactions dominated by competing double-exchange and itinerant Ruderman-Kittel-Kasuya-Yosida (RKKY) mechanisms, which is systematically suppressed with increasing $B$ to converge toward the long-range 3D Ising universality class ($\beta\sim0.330$, $\gamma\sim1.00$). Conversely, under an in-plane hard-axis configuration ($B\perp c$), the critical behavior study exhibits a sub-unity regime ($\gamma<1$) across the measured range of $B$. This behavior is attributed to the intense competitive interplay between the external Zeeman energy and strong out-of-plane magnetocrystalline anisotropy. Our findings demonstrate that critical behavior in nontrivial van der Waals magnets is fundamentally modulated by the orientation of $B$ and the strength of magnetocrystalline anisotropy, demonstrating the necessity of $B$-dependent critical behavior investigation over conventional frameworks.

### Paper [52]
Title: Going beyond the standard quasiparticle picture: Entanglement spreading after quenching from highly entangled states
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15409
Authors: Elisa Sabadini, Vincenzo Alba
Abstract: The quasiparticle picture provides a quantitative description of entanglement spreading after quantum quenches in integrable systems. In interacting models, however, it relies on the assumption that the quench creates entangled pairs of quasiparticles, which is justified only for the so-called integrable initial states. Here we go beyond this setting by studying quenches in the XXZ spin chain from the $n$-rainbow states, a family of macroscopically homogeneous states formed by increasingly long-range Bell pairs, which are not integrable for $n>1$. The local stationary state after the quench approaches the infinite-temperature state as $n\to\infty$, with corrections of order $1/n$, as we show analytically. Nevertheless, in the noninteracting XX chain the quench produces entangled multiplets of $2n$ species of quasiparticles. Although interspecies correlations vanish as $1/n$, they strongly suppress the growth of the entanglement entropy, whose rate vanishes as $n\to\infty$. We capture this behaviour within a generalized quasiparticle picture. In the interacting XXZ chain, instead, time-dependent density matrix renormalization group (tDMRG) simulations show that the entanglement entropy grows at the rate at which thermodynamic entropy is exchanged across the cut. Within the time scales accessible to tDMRG, the data show no trace of the multiplet suppression. This holds both after homogeneous quenches and after joining the $n$-rainbow state with an infinite-temperature state, for which we conjecture a formula for the von Neumann entropy.

### Paper [53]
Title: Nonlocal multipolar spin accumulation in altermagnets beyond d-wave
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15438
Authors: J. W. Gonzalez
Abstract: In spin-conserving models of $g$- and $i$-wave altermagnets, the uniform longitudinal conductivity has no spin-odd component. A point injector reveals higher moments of the Fermi velocity through the nonlocal spin accumulation. Boltzmann theory predicts that the rank $m=2,4,6$ of the first nonzero spin-odd velocity moment sets the leading angular harmonic, its asymptotic radial dependence $r^{2-m}$, and its amplitude scaling $\tau^{m-3}$ at fixed distance and injected current. For $g$-wave order, the fourth moment produces a fourth angular harmonic with an asymptotic $r^{-2}$ tail. Quantum transport simulations support the radial profiles predicted by finite-geometry Boltzmann theory without an amplitude fit. Across the three symmetries, the quantum amplitudes exceed the Boltzmann predictions based on the Born scattering time by $15-65$\% in the windows analyzed. In a two-contact ribbon, we detect the $g$-wave fourth harmonic at $6.8\,\sigma$ in an independent set of 1800 disorder realizations using a preselected radial window. Its amplitude decreases when the altermagnetic splitting is reduced.

### Paper [54]
