Title: Multifractal collective order : Application to multifractal superconductivity in dodecagonal quasicrystals
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15446
Authors: SeHyun Park, Junmo Jeon, SungBin Lee
Abstract: Conventional descriptions of collective ordering often assume spatially uniform order parameters, although the underlying electronic states can exhibit strong spatial inhomogeneity or multifractality. Here, we demonstrate that deterministic aperiodic geometry can instead stabilize multifractal collective order. We formulate a general diagnostic applicable to superconducting, magnetic, and charge channels, combining direct scaling analysis of the order parameter with three criteria: its spatial hierarchy must be dictated by the underlying geometry, emerge in the leading ordering instability, and persist in the self-consistent ordered state below a single mean-field ordering onset. We demonstrate that superconductivity on the experimentally resolved Ti network of a dodecagonal oxide quasicrystal satisfies all three criteria. The resulting condensate exhibits multifractal scaling over the resolved range of spatial scales, retaining its hierarchical structure throughout the ordered phase and channeling flux-induced equilibrium currents along geometry-locked rings.

### Paper [55]
Title: Materials research with a general-purpose agent
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15451
Authors: Paulo R. Pires, Th\'eo Cavignac, Tiago F. T. Cerqueira, Hai-Chen Wang, Silvana Botti, Miguel A. L. Marques
Abstract: Over the last decade we have built Alexandria, an open database of about 15 million density-functional calculations of crystals. It includes PBE, PBEsol, SCAN and TB-mBJ results, structures relaxed at pressures up to 150 GPa, dielectric tensors and about 145,000 electron-phonon calculations, together with more than 400 million structures relaxed with machine-learned potentials. On these data we trained a generative model for crystal structures, a universal interatomic potential and graph neural networks for more than ten properties, from band gaps to superconducting critical temperatures. From the convex hull we computed a reaction graph of the 17.4 billion reactions between its 193,416 stable compounds. Here we give all of this to a general-purpose language-model agent. Working unattended, the agent screens the database for a solar absorber, drops a candidate whose synthesis releases too little energy, and writes a synthesis plan with precursors, crucible and atmosphere. Asked about the well-studied Ba-Zr-S system, it finds an unreported sulfide and designs the experiment that would confirm or rule it out. Asked for an air-stable alternative, it designs one and tests it against new structures from the generative model. With web search alone, the same agent proposes what the literature already discusses. Put together, the data, the models and the reaction graph let a general agent do what none of them does alone: go from a question in plain words to an experiment that a laboratory can run and test.

### Paper [56]
Title: Defect Topology Governs the Optical Response of Doped Amorphous Silicon
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15478
Authors: Ridwan Hussein, Chinonso Ugwumadu, Louise A. M. Rosset, Roxanne M. Tutchton
Abstract: The influence of defect topology and chemical doping on the optical response of amorphous silicon (a-Si) remains unresolved. In this study, hybrid-functional density functional theory and linear-response calculations are employed to examine four structurally distinct continuous random networks with substitutional boron and phosphorus doping. The calculated optical properties reproduce key experimental trends and demonstrate that dopant sensitivity is determined not only by defect concentration, but also by the character and spatial organization of defects. A configuration with fully extended 4-fold coordination shows the strongest dielectric perturbation to doping, while dopant effects in networks with pre-existing defect states are more varied. For most networks, the dielectric response is more strongly perturbed by boron than phosphorus. Notably, a network having both 3- and 5-fold defects with a total defect fraction of 7.4% reverses the typical boron-dominant response of other cases, becoming more sensitive to phosphorus. Tauc and optical sum-rule analyses further indicate that network topology defines the broader optical landscape, whereas doping primarily affects states near the gap and redistributes spectral weight. These findings establish defect topology as a microscopic control parameter that links amorphous structure, dopant activity, and optical response in amorphous silicon.

### Paper [57]
Title: Cumulant dynamics of the ferromagnetic dipolar XY model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15485
Authors: Jinxin Yang, Alberto R. Cavallar, Lode Pollet
Abstract: We study the second-order truncated cumulant dynamics for the preparation of dynamical states in the dipolar ferromagnetic XY model on the square lattice for system sizes up to linear size L = 60. The observations lead to a transparent picture in which a coherent region of finite extent and finite magnetization forms, which is possible even for diabatic but sufficiently slow ramps. These two scales are functions of the ramp constant, the initial entropy of the system, and time. Our findings put strong requirements on future experiments addressing dipolar XY equilibrium physics in bigger system sizes.

### Paper [58]
Title: Symmetry-Enabled Ferroelectric Valley Transduction in Rhombohedral Bilayer WSe$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15494
Authors: Katsunori Wakabayashi, Souren Adhikary, Tomoaki Kameda, Kazuhito Tsukagoshi
Abstract: Ferroelectric polarization couples to the layer degree of freedom in a bilayer, but a valley-selective energy response requires the valleys to carry different interlayer character. Rhombohedral bilayer WSe$_2$ meets that condition by symmetry: three-fold rotation forbids interlayer tunneling at the $K$ valley, while strong hybridization remains at $\Gamma$. The resulting contrast converts the band-effective ferroelectric layer asymmetry into a shift of the $K$ edge, and so displaces the $K$--$\Gamma$ landscape: a finite-field fit that does not impose the rule leaves the $K$ coupling consistent with zero, and the shift reaches \polshiftdft~meV, the most that asymmetry can give a single valley. Reversing the polarization mirrors the valley landscape in displacement field, so the two registries have equal and opposite differential transconductance $g_d$ at zero field. In the ideal symmetric limit a second parity holds about each registry's own compensation field and places a zero of $g_d$ there. The valley-dependent screening calculated here displaces that zero by only \gdnulllow\% at the read point, so device asymmetry limits the accuracy. The result links a known stacking selection rule to ferroelectric valley transduction, with a quantitative valley shift and a directly testable transport signature.

### Paper [59]
Title: Engineering many-body tunneling dynamics of one-dimensional bosons with finite-core power-law interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15504
Authors: Abhik Kumar Saha, Cesare Vianello, Andrea Trombettoni, Barnali Chakrabarti
Abstract: We investigate the many-body dynamics of a one-dimensional Bose-Josephson junction of ultracold bosons in a box-like potential with a central barrier, interacting via finite-core power-law interactions $V_\alpha(r)=g/(r^\alpha+d^\alpha)$, where $d$ sets the short-distance scale and $\alpha$ controls the spatial dependence. Using the multiconfigurational time-dependent Hartree method for bosons (MCTDHB), we explore how the interaction profile and strength, barrier, and initial population imbalance shape tunneling dynamics. At weak interaction, increasing $\alpha$ drives a crossover from Josephson oscillations to dephasing and recurrent rephasing, demonstrating the importance of the interaction kernel's spatial structure. A higher barrier suppresses recurrences, whereas a larger initial imbalance promotes relaxation toward nearly vanishing population imbalance. Increasing interaction strength progressively suppresses population-transfer fluctuations, particularly at larger $\alpha$. The two-mode extended Bose-Hubbard model captures the dynamics when the lowest orbital in each well dominates, but loses accuracy as additional orbitals become populated. In the strongly interacting regime, many-body spatial structures develop and several natural orbitals are needed for converged dynamics, placing the system beyond a two-mode description. Our results establish the interaction profile and strength, barrier, and initial state as distinct control parameters for coherent, dephasing, recurrent, and suppressed many-body tunneling of one-dimensional bosons. The ability to tailor soft-core interactions via Rydberg dressing offers a promising route to exploring interaction-engineered tunneling in ultracold atoms.

### Paper [60]
Title: Probe Dynamics in Active Baths via Trajectory-Response Relations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15518
Authors: Chul-Ung Woo, Jiwon Choi, Heiko Rieger
Abstract: Passive probes in active baths can undergo spontaneous motion driven by nonequilibrium forces that depend on probe velocity and interaction geometry. Using trajectory-response relations that express the bath response to a probe in terms of free-particle displacement statistics, we investigate the dynamics of freely moving probes weakly coupled to ideal active baths. The mean force exerted by the bath defines a velocity-space potential whose minima describe the stable states. By analyzing how the mean force depends on the probe velocity, we identify stable resting and moving states and determine the parameter regime in which they coexist. For elliptical interactions, the resting state becomes unstable first along the narrow axis, resulting in direction selection already at the linear-response level. For fourfold interactions, linear friction remains isotropic, while the nonlinear dependence of the mean force on probe velocity selects axial or diagonal motion and produces directional multistability. We further show that the trajectory-response relations extend to higher orders in the probe strength, where multi-interval free-particle trajectory statistics become relevant. For a Gaussian probe, these higher-order terms shift the speed of the stable moving state.

### Paper [61]
Title: Competing lattice instabilities in epitaxially strained BaSnO3
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15550
Authors: Benyao Sun, Anna Gr\"unebohm
Abstract: We use density functional theory to map the lattice-dynamical landscape of BaSnO3 under biaxial compressive strain and uncover the hierarchy of structural instabilities. Our results demonstrate that the phase stability of strained BaSnO3 cannot be inferred from the softening of the polar mode alone. Instead it is governed by the competition among lattice instabilities and their anharmonic coupling to structural relaxation. Two-mode energy-surface analyses show that the polar and rotational order parameters interact predominantly competitively. While A- and M-point octahedral rotations become unstable before the zone-center polar mode and dominate the harmonic response for moderate values of strain, they are related to a continuous transition without anharmonic lattice coupling and a moderate gain of energy with strain. Large strain may induce a first order transition to a supertetragonal polar state. Once this state is stabilized, the magnitude of polarization and ferroelectric energy well depth are comparable to BaTiO3 under compressive strain. Furthermore, we discuss how an electric field or electron doping may tip the balance between the different modes and favor of the supertetragonal structure over antipolar and rotational alternatives at a reduced critical strain.

### Paper [62]
Title: Filling and Sealing Carbon Nanotubes by Local Anodic Oxidation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15567
Authors: Ruiqing Zang, Jianan Xiao, Jacob Bair, Ian Wang, Chuyue Peng, Matthias Kuehne
Abstract: Carbon nanotubes (CNTs) are prime nanofluidic conduits and nanopores for studying matter under extreme confinement. Since most synthesis routes yield CNTs with closed ends, opening and filling strategies are required to access their interior for fluid and ion transport, whereas studies of the encapsulated species themselves may additionally require sealing to prevent their escape in dry or vacuum environments. Capacitively coupled local anodic oxidation (LAO) in an atomic force microscope (AFM) has been shown to cut individual CNTs at predefined positions, but whether cutting induces opening, filling, or sealing has remained unclear. Using spatially resolved micro-Raman spectroscopy, we find that LAO cutting surprisingly produces filled CNT segments that remain sealed under dry Ar atmosphere for weeks. The magnitudes of the blueshift and broadening of the innermost-wall radial breathing-like mode are consistent with water filling, pointing to the water meniscus condensed between the AFM tip and the substrate as the source of CNT filling during cutting. High-vacuum annealing at 140 {\deg}C releases the encapsulated species from some, but not all, segments, indicating a thermally surmountable barrier rather than re-formed end caps. This one-step cut-fill-seal capability offers a route to diameter-resolved studies of nanoconfined water and aqueous solutions, and to zeptoliter-scale sample delivery for vacuum-based analytical techniques.

### Paper [63]
Title: Single Spin Hall Conversion in the Hall Response of Magnetic-Insulator/Heavy-Metal Bilayers Identified by Odd Spin-Hall-Angle Dependence
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15571
Authors: Akashdeep Akashdeep, Ewiese Mohammad Ababneh, Botros Gerges Mikhail Fenhas, Duc Minh Tran, Mathias Kl\"aui, Steven S. -L. Zhang, Vivek Amin, Gerhard Jakob, Timo Kuschel
Abstract: The Hall response of magnetic-insulator/heavy-metal bilayers is usually ascribed to two successive spin Hall conversions and is therefore expected to be even in the spin Hall angle. Using distinct magnetic-field windows, we separate the Hall voltage of yttrium iron garnet bilayers with Ta and Pt of opposite spin Hall angle into contributions from magnetization- and magnetic-field-driven precession as well as from ordinary Hall effect. The field-driven component reverses sign between Ta and Pt, revealing a Hall response odd-in-spin-Hall-angle. The magnetization-driven component also has opposite signs for Ta and Pt, except for the thinnest heavy-metal layers. A drift-diffusion model with interface-generated spin currents describes this behavior: the field-driven component is predominantly carried by a single spin Hall conversion, and the magnetization-driven component is consistent with one as well. Other potential explanations, based on a description with a finite imaginary spin-mixing conductance rather than interface-generated spin currents, require unusually large values of this conductance, making this mechanism unlikely. Our findings advance the understanding of Hall responses in magnetic-insulator/heavy-metal bilayers toward a more general description.

### Paper [64]
Title: States, localization, and oxidation dynamics in gene segments with CAG repeats
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15594
Authors: Gerasimos Papadopoulos, Constantinos Simserides
Abstract: We study coherent quantum-mechanical properties of gene segments with CAG repeats: eigenstates including density of states, mean over time probabilities of finding the hole at each site after initial oxidation at a particular site, mean transfer rates between sites, participation ratios, and inverse participation ratios. The genes are analyzed at the base-pair level within the framework of the Tight-Binding Wire Model. All selected genes share a common feature: a region consisting of multiple repeats of the CAG triplet (referring to a strand in the 5'-3' direction). For each gene, there exists a range of CAG repeat numbers for which the gene remains functional, while values outside this range are associated with pathogenicity. We examine the genes associated with (a) Huntington's disease, (b) Kennedy's disease, (c) Spinocerebellar Ataxia 6, and (d) Spinocerebellar Ataxia 7. These genes differ in the sequences flanking the CAG repeats, as well as in the thresholds separating non-pathogenic and pathogenic repeat lengths. In the literature, the underlying cause of these disorders is attributed to errors in DNA replication, repair and transcription leading to expansion of the triplet repeats. We model both pathogenic and non-pathogenic sequences. We find that periodic CAG regions promote hole delocalization, while primers break periodicity, enhancing localization. This is of purely coherent quantum-mechanical origin, arising from the periodic structure of the CAG repeats and the disruption of periodicity at the primers, and cannot be explained by thermally activated hopping. Finally, transfer depends on oxidation site, e.g., a hole generated at a C-G or G-C site is mainly transferred through other C-G and G-C base pairs, while transfer through A-T sites is much less probable.

### Paper [65]
Title: Machine-accelerated extraction of experimental superconducting parameters for physical insight and prediction across thousands of materials
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15627
Authors: Mihir Manium, Benjamin Geisler, Peter J. Hirschfeld, Amalia I. Coldea
Abstract: New superconductors could unlock next-generation technologies from nuclear fusion to lossless electronics. Machine learning can predict candidate superconductors but lacks validated experimental training data beyond the critical temperature. Here we present AutoSuperCon, a database of eight superconducting parameters, containing 20354 entries for 6793 superconductors. The data were extracted from over 47000 arXiv preprints using a large language model and validated against reference datasets ($R^2 = 0.96$ with NIMS SuperCon). Using AutoSuperCon, we show that median gap ratios rise from 3.6 in conventional superconductors to 6.4 in cuprates, 28% of upper critical fields exceed the Pauli limit, and critical temperatures lie below a ceiling proportional to the superfluid density. Further, we train composition-based predictors for the critical temperature ($R^2_{\log}=0.83$) and the upper critical field ($R^2_{\log}=0.69$), boosted by transfer learning. Within known superconducting families, composition alone is sufficient to screen candidates for both parameters. AutoSuperCon thus enables physical insight and data-driven discovery across thousands of superconductors.

### Paper [66]
Title: Cation-intermixing driven evolution from a narrow avoided crossing to an enlarged inverted gap in Ge$_2$Bi$_2$Te$_5$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15631
Authors: Alexander S. Frolov, Alexander V. Fedorov, Nadezhda V. Vladimirova, Sergey M. Aksenov, Mark A. Naumov, Savely V. Domenko, Artem A. Minaev, Vasily S. Stolyarov, Denis V. Vyalikh, Lada V. Yashina
Abstract: Combining the building blocks and structural motifs of the topological insulator Bi$_2$Te$_3$ and the ferroelectric Rashba semiconductor GeTe, we synthesized and comprehensively characterized the resulting Ge$_2$Bi$_2$Te$_5$ compound, which exhibits pronounced cation intermixing. Using momentum-resolved photoemission spectroscopy and ab initio calculations, we demonstrate how cation intermixing drives the evolution of its electronic structure from a narrow avoided crossing to a substantially enlarged inverted band gap. We establish the microscopic origin of this behavior in the redistribution of Ge and Bi atoms within the cation sublattice, which increases the contribution of Bi-derived states near the valence-band maximum and enhances the effective contribution of spin-orbit coupling, thereby stabilizing the topological electronic structure. Our findings demonstrate that cation disorder, which can be controlled during crystal growth, provides an additional degree of freedom for engineering topological electronic structures, opening new opportunities for the development of functional quantum materials.

### Paper [67]
Title: Brute-force molecular dynamics simulation of aging in amorphous Ge$_2$Sb$_2$Te$_5$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15664
Authors: Elisa Petroni, Dario Baratella, Omar Abou El Kheir, Andrea Redaelli, Marco Bernasconi
Abstract: Chalcogenide alloys such as the prototypical Ge$_2$Sb$_2$Te$_5$ compound are used in phase change electronic memories due to their ability to undergo a fast and reversible transition between the amorphous and crystalline phases upon heating. The amorphous phase is subject to aging that leads to an increase in the electrical resistance, which is detrimental to the operation of the devices. Using a machine learning interatomic potential, we have investigated the structural relaxations responsible for aging in amorphous Ge$_2$Sb$_2$Te$_5$ in molecular dynamics simulations at temperatures slightly below the glass transition. The aging simulation, lasting about 100 ns, follows an equilibration of 24 ns at 400 K. During aging we observed an enhancement of the Peierls distortion in axial bonds which is a typical feature of these alloys that we relate to the widening of the electronic band gap computed within Density Functional Theory in smaller aged amorphous models. We also observed a marked reduction of the atomic mobility with an increase of the bond lifetimes during aging. However, we do not see a reduction in homopolar bonds, which are expected to be absent in the ideal aged glass and which should be removed on a much longer time scale.

### Paper [68]
Title: Spin Structure Factor of the classical Ising Model on the Centered Pyrochlore Lattice
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15677
Authors: L. Xu, T. Krei{\ss}el, N. Davier, L. D. C. Jaubert, L. Pollet
Abstract: We present the spin structure factor of the Ising model on the centered pyrochlore lattice. Generalizing Henley's method [Phys. Rev. B 71, 014424] to the case of inequivalent center and vertex spins, we obtain spin correlation functions that are in excellent agreement with Monte Carlo results and can capture sharp and broadened pinch points, also at finite temperature. Our approach can be extended to other centered lattice structures in classical frustrated magnetism.

### Paper [69]
Title: Toward Self-Driving Multi-reference Calculations: Belief-Aware Orbital Selection
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15682
Authors: Chen Li, Kaifan Pan, Xinyu Sun, Yinxuan Song, Lianhua He, Teng Lu, Haibo Ma, Zhenggang Lan, Yingjin Ma
Abstract: We reformulate active-orbital selection as a partially observable Markov decision process (POMDP), analogous to decision-making in autonomous driving and robot control, and couple it with quadratic unconstrained binary optimization (QUBO) to emulate expert-like selection of active orbitals. The three popular types of orbital information, i.e. occupation numbers, orbital composition, and orbital entanglements, can be used separately or jointly. QUBO constructs complete candidate spaces under fixed electron and orbital counts, while the POMDP selects successive calculations, updates its belief from each completed result, and thereby corrects and refines the active orbitals. This belief-aware sequential procedure enables continuous active-space selection rather than one-shot threshold-based filtering, so the space can adapt as the calculation proceeds. We demonstrate the approach for valence-space construction in small molecules, spectral calculation of metal compound TiF$_3$, and active-space refinement of 1,2-dioxetanone around conical intersections. Because selection learns from calculations on the molecule of interest without requiring a pre-existing training set, it is suited to following the entire course of complex chemical reactions.

### Paper [70]
Title: $^{55}$Mn-NMR/NQR study on the kagome metal Sc$_3$Mn$_3$Al$_7$Si$_5$: Re-examining the possibility of spin-singlet paired state
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15693
Authors: Qing-Ping Ding, Yongbin Lee, Charuni Dissanayake, Yasuyuki Nakajima, Yuji Furukawa
Abstract: Sc$_3$Mn$_3$Al$_7$Si$_5$ is a Mn-based kagome metal that has been proposed to be close to a ferromagnetic instability. Recent $^{55}$Mn nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) study suggested the emergence of a spin-singlet paired state below $\sim$10 K. To clarify its low-energy magnetic and electronic properties, we performed $^{55}$Mn NMR and nuclear quadrupole resonance (NQR) measurements on single-crystalline Sc$_3$Mn$_3$Al$_7$Si$_5$. From the detailed NMR/NQR spectrum measurements and the DFT calculations, a quadrupolar frequency of $\nu_{\rm Q}$ = 4.6$\sim$4.8 MHz and an asymmetry parameter of $\eta$ = 0.45$\sim$0.48 as well as the principal axes of electric field gradient EFG for the Mn site were determined. The temperature dependence of Knight shift ($K$) shows a similar temperature dependence of the DC magnetic susceptibility $\chi$ except for the low-temperature region below $\sim$ 50 K where $K$ is almost constant while $\chi$ keeps increasing, which suggests that the increase in $\chi$ at low temperatures is not intrinsic. $^{55}$Mn spin-lattice relaxation rate divided by temperature ($1/T_1T$) is found to be nearly constant, confirming the metallic state of Sc$_3$Mn$_3$Al$_7$Si$_5$ from a microscopic point of view. Furthermore, no peak in $1/T_1T$ is observed near 10 K, and neither the Knight shift nor the linewidth shows any reductions at low temperatures. These results suggest no clear signature for the spin-singlet pairing around 10 K in Sc$_3$Mn$_3$Al$_7$Si$_5$.

### Paper [71]
Title: Zero-Field Optical Readout of Quintet States in Through-Space Coupled Tetracene Oligomers at Room Temperature
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15713
Authors: Jeannine Gr\"une, Maciej Majdecki, William K. Myers, Przemys{\l}aw Gawe{\l}, Hannah L. Stern
Abstract: Optically addressable electron spins are promising platforms for quantum technologies, with molecular systems offering chemical control over their spin and optical properties. Achieving optical readout of molecular spin states at room temperature and without an applied magnetic field is an important step towards their practical implementation. Quintet states generated via singlet fission are promising optically addressable multilevel qudits, but their intrinsic spin transitions at zero and weak magnetic fields remain largely unexplored, while substantial room-temperature quintet coherence has predominantly been demonstrated in structurally constrained molecular architectures. Here, we combine zero-field optically detected magnetic resonance (zero-field ODMR) with transient and pulsed electron paramagnetic resonance (EPR) to investigate the magnetic-field dependence and coherent spin properties of quintet states in conformationally flexible, through-space coupled tetracene oligomers. We demonstrate zero-field optical readout of quintet states at room temperature in both tetracene dimers and hexamers, and map the response of optically detected transitions to weak applied magnetic fields. Complementary EPR measurements support quintet formation through stochastic exchange fluctuations and demonstrate a room-temperature phase-memory time of 446 ns, showing that the conformational dynamics enabling high-spin formation remain compatible with substantial spin coherence. Together, these results integrate field-free optical readout, weak-field response and room-temperature spin coherence within a chemically modular, through-space coupled molecular architecture, expanding the design space for optically addressable molecular qudits and quantum sensors.

### Paper [72]
Title: Large deviations of spatial moments of the Kipnis-Marchioro-Presutti lattice gas following a localized energy release
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15739
Authors: Timo Schorlepp, Baruch Meerson, Pavel Sasorov
Abstract: We study the long-time statistics of spatial moments of the time-dependent energy distribution in a one-dimensional Kipnis-Marchioro-Presutti model following an instantaneous release of energy on a single lattice site. The moments are $M_n(T)=\sum_i x_i^n u_i(T)$, where $u_i(T)$ is the energy at site $i$ at the observation time $T$. Employing macroscopic fluctuation theory (MFT), we calculate the variance of $M_n$ for every positive integer $n$ and determine the non-Gaussian distribution tails for the representative cases $n=1$ and $2$. For the first moment, we establish an exact duality, within MFT, to integrated-current statistics for a deterministic flat initial density. For both $n=1$ and $n=2$, the large-moment tails are dominated by a single adiabatically evolving doublon: a rapidly propagating pair of narrow solitary pulses of the energy density and of the gradient of the conjugate field. The small-second-moment tail is associated with concentration of the energy near the origin at time $T$. Comparing single- and two-doublon histories, we predict a dynamical phase transition at a critical value of $M_2$ at which the optimal path breaks reflection symmetry $x\to -x$. Numerical solutions obtained by direct minimization of the MFT action establish the second-order character of this transition and support our analytical predictions. Finally, we extend calculations of the large- and small-moment tails to arbitrary $n$ and numerically find a transition of the same type for $n=4$.

### Paper [73]
Title: Chiral-phonon-induced anomalous Hall effect in bilayer MoSe$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15761
Authors: Omer Yaniv, Swati Chaudhary, Carolina Paiva, Dominik M. Juraschek
Abstract: We demonstrate that optically driven chiral phonons induce an anomalous Hall response in a nonmagnetic, centrosymmetric material. Using first-principles calculations to parameterize an effective $\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}$ Hamiltonian for bilayer MoSe$_2$, we show that circular excitation of an $E_u$ phonon generates a circular dynamical Rashba spin-orbit coupling. The resulting Floquet bands carry same-sign Berry curvature at the two valleys, producing a Hall response whose sign follows the phonon handedness. We obtain a range of $\sigma_{xy}\sim10^{-2}-0.21$ $\frac{e^2}{2\pi\hbar}$ for experimentally accessible light pulses. Our results show that chiral phonons provide a route to controlling electronic Berry curvature and transport on ultrafast time scales.

### Paper [74]
Title: A Hierarchical Harmonic-to-Anharmonic Framework for ab initio Screening and Mechanistic Analysis of Negative Thermal Expansion in ZrV2O7
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15767
Authors: J. Bustamante, A. Miliute, N. Jaykhedkar, A. A. Naik, J. Grandel, F. Emmerling, T. M. Stawski, J. George
Abstract: Negative thermal expansion (NTE) in framework materials arises from complex lattice dynamics that are challenging to capture using conventional theoretical approaches. Here, we establish a hierarchical computational strategy for investigating NTE in ZrV2O7, integrating the quasiharmonic approximation (QHA), temperature-dependent effective potentials (TDEP), and machine-learning-accelerated ab initio molecular dynamics (ML-MD). Quasi-harmonic lattice-dynamical calculations identify low-frequency phonon modes with negative Gr\"uneisen parameters as inexpensive screening descriptors, while TDEP incorporates finite-temperature anharmonicity and ML-MD further reveals temperature-dependent structural distortions that drive the lattice contraction. The observations are further corroborated with X-ray diffraction (XRD) measurements. The concurrent increase in transverse displacement (uT) provides microscopic evidence for a quasi-rigid-unit-mode (QRUM) mechanism underlying the NTE behaviour at elevated temperatures. This is consistent with previous reports on other framework materials, including ZrW2O7, where collective polyhedral rotations and transverse atomic motions have been linked to NTE. Overall, our results demonstrate how relatively inexpensive lattice-dynamical descriptors can identify potential NTE materials, while higher-level anharmonic simulations can then resolve the underlying structural mechanisms at the atomic scale.

### Paper [75]
Title: Evidential Deep Regression for Universal Density Functional Theory Hamiltonian Models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15786
Authors: Jiaxuan Liu, Yan Zhang, Caiyuan Ye, Tiannian Zhu, Zhong Fang, Hongming Weng, Quansheng Wu
Abstract: Universal density functional theory (DFT) Hamiltonian models trained across broad chemical and structural spaces offer an efficient route from atomic structure to electronic properties. However, estimating prediction uncertainty alongside fast Hamiltonian predictions remains a challenge. In this work, we present EviDTB, an evidential deep regression approach for tight-binding DFT Hamiltonians that effectively estimates prediction uncertainty in a single forward pass. Compared with deep ensembles, Monte Carlo dropout, and mean-variance estimation (MVE), EviDTB achieves a better balance between computational cost and uncertainty-quantification performance. On 5,478 held-out test structures, EviDTB achieves a full spin--orbit-coupled (SOC) Hamiltonian matrix-element mean absolute error of 0.622meV. Its mean uncertainty per atom correlates with the mean local squared prediction error (Spearman $\rho=0.812$); removing the 15% most uncertain structures reduces the 99th percentile of this error by 78%, from 16.63 to 3.72eV$^2$. This score offers a practical way to prioritize candidates for first-principles verification. We hope that combining fast Hamiltonian prediction with uncertainty-guided screening will accelerate the search for materials with desirable electronic properties.

### Paper [76]
Title: Heterogeneous ice nucleation with TIP4P/Ice: the role of lattice mismatch
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15806
Authors: M. Camarillo, J. Oller-Iscar, M. M. Conde, J. Ram\'irez, S. Blazquez, E. Sanz
Abstract: Ice formation, a crucial phase transition for life on Earth, mainly occurs through heterogeneous nucleation on a solid substrate. Despite the importance of this phenomenon, the correlation between the structure of the substrate and its ability to induce ice nucleation remains poorly understood. In this work, we use molecular dynamics simulations with an atomistic model of water to investigate how the lattice mismatch affects the ability of the substrate to nucleate ice. To unambiguously quantify the mismatch, we use substrates with a stretched ice structure. We put the substrate in contact with supercooled water and determine the temperature at which ice spontaneously nucleates during the course of the simulations. We find that the nucleation temperature decreases by $\sim$ 5-6 K for each percent by which the substrate lattice is stretched relative to that of ice. This trend is similar to that previously obtained with a coarse-grained water model [Camarillo et al., J. Chem. Phys. 160, 134505 (2024)]. \textcolor{black}{The substrate induces a fluctuating network of subcritical ice-like clusters, from which a spherical-cap-shaped supercritical nucleus eventually emerges stochastically.} Using Classical Nucleation Theory, combine previously reported homogeneous nucleation rates with heterogeneous nucleation rates computed in this work to estimate key heterogeneous nucleation parameters, including the nucleation barrier, the kinetic pre-factor, and the contact angle. The latter is consistent with a microscopic visualization of nucleation trajectories, supporting the applicability of Classical Nucleation Theory to describe a microscopic phenomenon such as heterogeneous ice nucleation.

### Paper [77]
Title: Lithium redistribution and electrode degradation in commercial NMC/Graphite and LCO/Graphite cells under high-rate cycling conditions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15818
Authors: Naisargi Kanabar, Nicholas Fusco, Seiichiro Higashiya, Devendra Sadana, Stephen Bedell, Haralabos Efstathiadis
Abstract: Fast charging accelerates degradation in lithium-ion batteries, but the effects of cathode chemistry and charging protocol remain difficult to separate. Here, commercial NMC811/graphite (200 mAh) and LCO/graphite (45 mAh) coin cells were cycled for 100 cycles at 0.3C, 1C, 2C, and 3C, followed by scanning electron microscopy (SEM), X-ray diffraction (XRD), and nuclear reaction analysis (NRA). Both cell types exhibited a transition in cycling stability between 1C and 2C. NMC811/graphite retained approximately 99.7% of its initial discharge capacity at 0.3C and 1C, decreasing to 72.3% at 2C and 63.5% at 3C, while LCO/graphite retained only 11.1% at 2C. NRA revealed maximum lithium accumulation in graphite anodes at 2C for both chemistries, supported by SEM observations of granular surface deposits. XRD showed rate-dependent shifts in cathode (003) and graphite (002) reflections, indicating structural changes during cycling. The lower lithium accumulation at 3C was associated with reduced charge throughput, while the LCO/graphite 3C condition was limited by its charging protocol. These findings highlight the importance of anode-side degradation and charging protocols in interpreting high-rate cycling performance of commercial lithium-ion batteries.

### Paper [78]
Title: Potts Order Preempts Emergence of 2-Form U(1) Spin Liquidity in Three-Dimensional Spin Vorticity Models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15845
Authors: Michael D. Burke, Mohid U. Farooqi, Kristian Tyn Kai Chung, Michel J. P. Gingras
Abstract: Three-dimensional (3D) spin vorticity models (SVMs) have been proposed to realize an exotic 2-form U(1) classical spin liquid (CSL), characterized by a zero-curl constraint, string excitations, and deconfined fluctuating membranes. A previous numerical study [PRB 111, 064417 (2025)] uncovered a puzzle in the pyrochlore lattice SVM: despite exhibiting pinch points characteristic of a U(1) spin liquid, it also develops weak all-in--all-out (AIAO) order. Here, we show that the emergence of the 2-form CSL in 3D SVMs is preempted by an intrinsic instability towards entropy-driven height locking. We establish this by resolving the ground state constraints using a height mapping, which we further relate to a 3-state Potts antiferromagnet. This identifies the pyrochlore's weak AIAO order as permutationally symmetric sublattice (PSS) order, albeit with an exceptionally weak locking strength. We extend the SVM to lattices of corner-sharing octahedra and cubes, where we identify the alternative broken-sublattice-symmetry (BSS) order, a hidden order without magnetic Bragg peaks coexisting with collective paramagnetism. All three models undergo a 3D Ising transition consistent with a symmetry-allowed locking potential in the coarse-grained height theory. The spin liquid persists over intermediate length and temperature scales, but a critical Coulomb ground state ensemble fails to materialize, its correlations cut off by the finite locking length. These findings illustrate how microscopic constraints can determine the ultimate stability of emergent gauge theories in frustrated spin systems.

### Paper [79]
Title: Universal Theory of Strange and Bad Metals from Spatially Random Interactions with Underdamped Quantum-Critical Bosons
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15851
Authors: Aaron Kleger, Rufus Boyack
Abstract: One of the most important challenges in condensed matter physics is understanding the macroscopic properties of strange metals, particularly why their resistivity remains linear in temperature over several decades. We propose that a (2+1)d theory of fermions coupled to underdamped quantum-critical bosons through a spatially disordered interaction provides this long-sought-after theoretical explanation. The key novelty of our work is to establish the appropriate separation of scales so that the bosons are underdamped. Using this framework, we obtain two distinct forms for the fermionic self-energy: a conformal-limit solution, $\Sigma\sim\sqrt{\omega}$, and a marginal Fermi-liquid solution, $\Sigma\sim\omega\log(1/\omega)$, corresponding to regimes above and below the Mott-Ioffe-Regel limit, respectively. These solutions produce Planckian scattering, exhibit exact $T$-linear resistivity, $\omega/T$ scaling in the optical conductivity, as well as $\sim T\log(1/T)$ heat capacity at low temperatures. Moreover, our theory naturally unifies strange- and bad-metal phenomena within a single framework, suggesting that we are now approaching a new epoch in understanding the physics of strongly correlated matter.

### Paper [80]
Title: Crystalline Equivalence Principle for Anomalies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15857
Authors: Ching-Yu Yao, Ryohei Kobayashi
Abstract: We develop a microscopic characterization of crystalline symmetry anomalies in bosonic lattice systems in (1+1) and (2+1) dimensions with a combination of crystalline and finite unitary internal symmetry. We construct anomaly indices of symmetry operators and derive their cocycle conditions and coboundary ambiguities. For generalized crystalline symmetries combining permutations of onsite Hilbert spaces with quantum cellular automata, anomaly indices take values in $H^3(B\widetilde \Gamma,U(1)_r)$ and $H^4(B\widetilde \Gamma,U(1)_r)$ respectively, where $\widetilde \Gamma$ is the total group of crystalline and internal symmetries, and the coefficient twist $r$ accounts for spatial orientation reversal. When crystalline symmetries act solely by permuting onsite Hilbert spaces, the anomaly classes restrict trivially to the spatial subgroup. These results establish a crystalline equivalence principle for bosonic anomalies at the microscopic level, without assuming a continuum description or emergent Lorentz invariance. We prove that a nontrivial total anomaly index obstructs any symmetric invertible phase. The framework incorporates modulated symmetries where spatial symmetries permute internal symmetries. Our results reproduce LSM and SPT--LSM constraints, with the latter illustrating how spectral-sequence differentials can trivialize candidate LSM anomaly indices.

### Paper [81]
Title: Learning Quantum Hamiltonians from the Dynamical Structure Factor
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15859
Authors: Nisarga Paul, Thomas Schuster, Jason Alicea
Abstract: Identifying the microscopic interactions governing a quantum system is essential for developing predictive theories of materials and validating quantum technologies. Despite considerable progress, learning Hamiltonians from realistic experimental data remains a challenge. Motivated by neutron scattering and quantum simulations, we study the problem of learning a quantum Hamiltonian from the dynamical structure factor (DSF), which captures two-point correlation data in quantum magnets. We show that the DSF encodes sufficient information to efficiently reconstruct broad classes of quantum Hamiltonians. Our approach exploits simple relations between low-order spectral moments of the DSF and microscopic couplings, giving rise to three complementary Hamiltonian tomography protocols based on the high-temperature expansion, strong-field polarization, and the fluctuation-dissipation theorem, respectively. The framework we introduce establishes practical and robust Hamiltonian learning from common experimental data, with potentially immediate application to neutron scattering experiments.

### Paper [82]
Title: Non-Bloch Topology of Frustrated Non-Hermitian Flat Bands
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15863
Authors: Fan Yang, Jiong-Hao Wang, Hui Liu, Emil J. Bergholtz
Abstract: Frustrated lattices with exactly flat bands and compact localized states provide an ideal setting to uncover the topology of flat bands, leading to intriguing physics in two dimensions. Extending from Hermitian to non-Hermitian systems, however, band structures are drastically changed by the non-Hermitian skin effect, which is less well understood beyond one dimension. Here, we construct a broad class of two-dimensional frustrated non-Hermitian lattice models with exactly flat bands which can be naturally described in a non-Bloch band theory. Using non-Hermitian breathing Kagome and checkerboard models as illustrations, we reveal how flat bands acquire a basis-independent skin effect induced by the other bands; in the breathing Kagome model it appears exactly when the flat band enters the area spanned by the periodic-boundary spectra of the dispersive bands. A flat band becomes singular when its eigenvectors hold immovable discontinuities at band crossings. Such singularity in non-Hermitian systems is sensitive to boundary conditions and unprotected by band flatness. Non-Bloch topology underlying is uncovered in a bulk-boundary correspondence with the formation of a singular band touching in the bulk and a robust flat band skin mode on the boundary. We present a geometric approach to construct this boundary skin mode from a macroscopic sum of compact localized skin modes and trace its topological origin to two linearly independent noncontractible loop states on a non-Bloch torus. To characterize the correspondence, an invariant is proposed based on biorthogonal polarization of the zero-energy corner mode. Breaking exact flatness, we further realize nearly flat non-Bloch Chern bands.

### Paper [83]
Title: Band geometry, Anderson's theorem, and vortex nucleation in unconventional 2D superconductors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15872
Authors: Vladislav Poliakov, Inti Sodemann Villadiego, T. Senthil
Abstract: Motivated by the recent discovery of chiral superconductors in 2d materials, we study the effects of disorder on superconductivity in materials with non-trivial band geometry. First, we show how Anderson's theorem and the conventional Abrikosov-Gorkov description of disorder-induced pair breaking are modified by the structure of the normal-state Bloch functions. We apply these results to twisted MoTe2 and show that a BCS pairing mechanism for the observed superconductor is in tension with the measured normal state residual resistivity. Second, we study whether random potential disorder can nucleate superconducting vortices in a chiral time- reversal-breaking superconductor. We show that, for a superconductor stabilized by the usual BCS mechanism, weak disorder does not induce a vortex glass phase, in contrast to superconductivity formed from an anyon fluid.

### Paper [84]
Title: U(1)-Symmetric Spin SYK Models: Ordering, Chaos, and Transport
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15896
Authors: Shiyong Guo, Enrico Rinaldi, Jad C. Halimeh, Brian Swingle
Abstract: The Sachdev--Ye--Kitaev (SYK) model provides an analytically controlled setting for strongly interacting quantum dynamics and holography, but its fermionic formulation leads to nonlocal encoding overheads on qubit-based quantum processors. We study a U(1)-symmetric spin analogue of the complex SYK model, constructed from random charge-conserving $q$-spin interactions that are native to a qubit Hilbert space. In the large-$N$, large-$q$ limit, the model reproduces the leading saddle-point structure of complex fermionic SYK under the identification of chemical potential with magnetic field and charge density with magnetization. We investigate the finite-$N$, finite-$q$ regime using exact diagonalization. A U(1)-invariant ordering susceptibility increases with system size for $q = 2, 4$ but decreases for $q = 6$, while bulk-spectrum level statistics are consistent with the Gaussian orthogonal ensemble at half filling and the Gaussian unitary ensemble in the adjacent magnetization sector. We further construct coupled-cluster chains and obtain domain-wall dynamics consistent with diffusive magnetization transport in a small-cluster regime accessible to matrix-product-state simulations. These results establish analytical benchmarks and finite-size signatures of ordering, quantum chaos, and magnetization transport in U(1)-symmetric spin-SYK models, and they motivate quantum simulation of the low-temperature, large-system regime, which remains difficult for classical methods.

### Paper [85]
Title: Eigenvalue Crossings and Discontinuous BBP Transitions in Spiked Euclidean Random Matrices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15927
Authors: Pasquale Casaburi, Pierpaolo Vivo
Abstract: We study the recovery of a rank-one signal embedded in a symmetric Euclidean random matrix, whose dense and geometrically correlated entries generally lack rotational invariance. We show that signal recovery through the leading eigenvector is governed by a competition between the principal directions of the noise and the signal. In particular, the signal may already be encoded in a subleading eigendirection, while remaining undetected by the leading principal component. As the signal strength increases, a crossing between competing eigenvalues can change their ordering, causing the leading eigenvector to switch abruptly from a noise-dominated to a signal-bearing direction. This mechanism gives rise to a discontinuous BBP-type transition, in which the overlap with the signal jumps from zero to a finite value. Using the replica method, we reduce the problem to a Fredholm eigenvalue equation and derive general criteria for the existence of a positive recovery threshold, expressed in terms of the unperturbed spectral edge, the spike mean and the spatial averages of the principal eigenfunctions. We obtain analytically the limiting largest eigenvalue, eigenvector-component distribution and signal overlap, and illustrate the results with explicit solutions for quadratic distance and rank-two kernels, finding excellent agreement with numerical diagonalisation.

### Paper [86]
Title: What Hall Measures in Strongly Correlated Metals
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15940
Authors: Emily Z. Zhang, Thomas P. Devereaux
Abstract: The Hall effect provides a sensitive probe of electronic correlations in strongly interacting metals, yet its microscopic interpretation beyond quasiparticle transport remains poorly understood. Here, we develop a Liouvillian formulation of the Hall conductivity that organizes the response into different classes of flux-sensitive many-body interference operators. Using a Schrieffer-Wolff transformation of the Hubbard model, we identify how projected charge motion and virtual hopping processes contribute to oriented trajectories enclosing magnetic flux. A high-temperature expansion reveals a hierarchy of inverse-temperature contributions associated with these interference processes. This framework provides a possible microscopic interpretation of the integer power laws previously observed in determinant quantum Monte Carlo calculations, which emerge at temperatures below the regime where the expansion is formally controlled. Our results highlight the sensitivity of Hall transport to coherent many-body dynamics and establish a connection between lattice geometry, electronic correlations, and transverse charge response.

### Paper [87]
Title: A New High $\gamma$-$\gamma'$ Fraction Polycrystalline Co/Ni Superalloy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15969
Authors: Cameron Crabb, Mark C. Hardy, Michelle S. Conroy, David Dye
Abstract: An ingot-processable Co/Ni-based $\gamma/\gamma'$ superalloy has been developed for wrought parts in aircraft engines. An 8.5 kg VIM ingot was homogenised and hot rolled to a true strain of 1. A heat treatment was then developed to produce 50% $\gamma'$ and a grain size of 142 $\mu$m. High-temperature tensile testing showed yield strengths of 820 MPa at 700 $^\circ$C, 785 MPa at 800 $^\circ$C and 783 MPa at 850 $^\circ$C. Creep tests at 750 $^\circ$C/600 MPa and 800 $^\circ$C/300 MPa demonstrated reduced minimum creep rates and extended rupture lives relative to current wrought Ni-base alloys. Oxidation at 800 $^\circ$C for 100 h formed a 1 $\mu$m dual chromia/alumina scale with minimal subsurface degradation. Long-term exposure at 800 $^\circ$C for 8000 h showed limited $\gamma'$ coarsening and no deleterious intermetallic or grain boundary phases. The alloy therefore offers a promising balance of processability, strength and oxidation resistance for high-temperature static and rotating components.or grain boundary phases. The alloy therefore offers a promising balance of processability, strength and oxidation resistance for high-temperature static and rotating components.

### Paper [88]
Title: Elastic tricriticality at the collapse of magnetic order in a valence fluctuating Eu-based compound
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15984
Authors: Yassine Agarmani, Arif Ata, Lars Franke, Jan Zimmermann, Ulrich Tutsch, Marius Peters, Michelle Ocker, Kristin Kliemt, Cornelius Krellner, Bernd Wolf, Markus Garst, Michael Lang
Abstract: Spin and charge fluctuations are key for understanding the properties of materials with strongly correlated electrons, but their coupling to the atomic crystal lattice is often assumed to be weak and perturbative. In this work, we demonstrate that at tricritical points (TCPs), that are associated with electronic correlations, a coupling to crystal elasticity is generically non-perturbative. It will modify the tricritical universality class leading to elastic tricriticality, characterized by a compressibility that diverges in a universal manner with the correlation length. To test this scenario experimentally, we use single crystals of EuPd$_2$(Si$_{1-x}$Ge$_x$)$_2$, where, depending on $x$ and pressure, magnetic order collapses due to strong valence fluctuations. First, we confirm the presence of a TCP for $x \approx 0.1$ by using complementary probes, in particular, measurements of relative length changes under control of temperature and He-gas pressure. Second, by studying the elastic response, we uncover clear signatures of elastic tricriticality, notably a drastically enhanced compressibility.

### Paper [89]
Title: Mott transitions in Dirac nodal-line semimetals
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16033
Authors: Andressa R. Medeiros-Silva, Lucas O. Lima, Marcello B. da Silva Neto, Fakher F. Assaad, Natanael C. Costa
Abstract: Nodal-line semimetals provide a natural setting to investigate how the geometry of a nodal manifold affects interaction-driven quantum phase transitions. Here, we study the Hubbard model on a cubic lattice supporting either nodal-point (cubic $\pi$-flux model) or nodal-line semimetallic states using mean-field theory, determinant quantum Monte Carlo simulations, and a weak-coupling renormalization-group (RG) analysis. In both cases, antiferromagnetic order develops only above a finite interaction strength, with the nodal-line system exhibiting a smaller critical coupling due to its enhanced low-energy density of states and markedly stronger finite-size effects. For the nodal-point model, the quantum critical behavior is compatible with $(3+1)$-dimensional Gross--Neveu mean-field scaling. In contrast, the weak-coupling RG reveals that the nodal line itself is unstable against interactions: although the Hubbard interaction is perturbatively irrelevant, its second-order self-energy generates a symmetry-allowed relevant term proportional to $U^2\cos k_z$, reconstructing the nodal line into isolated, strongly anisotropic nodal points. This weak-coupling reconstruction is distinct from the strong-coupling antiferromagnetic transition and highlights the role of nodal-manifold geometry in interacting semimetals.

### Paper [90]
Title: Phase Transitions of Polar Nematic Liquid Crystals Driven by Electrostatics and Flexoelectricity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16035
Authors: Lincoln Paik, Jonathan V. Selinger
Abstract: Polar liquid crystals can exhibit a series of phases and phase transitions, from the conventional nematic phase at high temperature to a modulated nematic (antiferroelectric) phase at intermediate temperature, and then to a uniform polar (ferroelectric) phase at lower temperature. Here, we develop a theory for this phase behavior. The theory is based on a competition between flexoelectricity (which favors splay in the modulated phase) and electrostatics (which provides an energy penalty for splay). Minimizing the free energy leads to differential equations that describe the spatial variation of the director, the polar order, and the electric potential. By solving the differential equations numerically, we determine the structure of the antiferroelectric phase. At high temperature, the modulated structure is sinusoidal. At lower temperature, it crosses over to a structure of wide domains separated by narrow domain walls. These numerical solutions can be compared with simplified models that apply near the phase transitions.

### Paper [91]
Title: Folding of multishape metamaterials guided by plasticity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16045
Authors: Wenfeng Liu, Jean-Baptiste Chombart, Bernard Ennis, Corentin Coulais
Abstract: Multishape metamaterials can morph into several different shapes and offer exciting prospects in areas as diverse as tunable optics, wearable electronics, soft robotics, and aerospace. Yet, multishape metamaterials are typically actuated with complex multiaxial or multiphysics loading. Here, we introduce a uniaxial folding strategy that turns kirigami, 2D sheets patterned with cuts, into multishape metamaterials with tunable rigidity. Our folding approach leverages plasticity and loading history to guide multiple and tunable distinct shape changes upon cycles of stretching and compression. The deployed shapes exhibit a wide range of mechanical properties spanning three orders of magnitude. Self-guided plastic folding provides a uniquely scalable route to deploy load-bearing metamaterials from the micrometer up to the industrial scale.

### Paper [92]
Title: Quantifying sample heating by ablation-plasma x-rays in laser-driven shock experiments
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16054
Authors: R. F. Smith, I. K. OCampo, S. Singh, T. M. Hutchinson, T. E. Lockard, J. H. Eggert, C. P. McGuire, A. L. Coleman, J. M. McNaney, S. Pandolfi, B. Nagler, D. McGonegle, H. J. Lee, D. Khaghani, N. Czapla, A. Krygier
Abstract: Sample preheat due to absorption of x-rays emitted by an ablation plasma has long been a source of uncertainty in laser-driven dynamic compression experiments. This unknown heating complicates interpretation of experiments designed to reach well-defined thermodynamic states, such as the principal Hugoniot, particularly because the pressure drive and sample heating occur simultaneously and are difficult to disentangle. Here, we quantify sample heating from x-rays using a unique experimental design in which the sample is heated prior to the arrival of the pressure wave. Using in situ x-ray diffraction, we relate lattice expansion to temperature within a 1-um-thick Au sample for commonly used 25-um and 75-um-thick Kapton-B (polyimide) ablators over a range of laser irradiances. Heating, sufficient to melt Au (Delta T ~ 1050 K), is observed for 25-um-thick polyimide ablators, which is significantly enhanced by the addition of a 0.2-um Al flash coating. In contrast, heating is strongly suppressed for 75-um-thick, non-Al-coated Kapton-B, with no measurable heating observed in 2.5-um-thick Ti or 1-um-thick Fe samples at laser irradiances up to 1.2 x 10^13 W/cm^2. .

### Paper [93]
Title: Entropy production decomposition and fluctuation theorems under continuous feedback: From prescribed to stochastic protocols
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16070
Authors: Long Him Cheung
Abstract: When a system is driven by a prescribed protocol, the entropy production can be decomposed into the housekeeping and non-autonomous contributions, each satisfying a fluctuation theorem. Under feedback control, however, the feedback parameter depends on measurement outcomes rather than following a prescribed protocol. To study how the decomposition changes, we consider an overdamped Brownian particle at position $x$ with a low-pass-filtered measurement signal $D$ as the feedback parameter. Regarding each realization of $D$ as a stochastic protocol, we derive fluctuation theorems for the entropy production associated with the dynamics of $x$ and of $D$, and interpret them in terms of the information flow. For a solvable harmonic model, we map the dynamics onto an autonomous system in which the measurement and filtering process acts as an effective thermal bath. Feedback cooling thus corresponds to heat conduction from the physical bath through the particle to this colder bath, and the information flow can be interpreted as the entropy associated with the heat conduction between $x$ and $D$. We verify these results by numerical simulations.

### Paper [94]
Title: Tunable Long-Range Magnetic Exchange in Adatom-Decorated Transition Metal Dichalcogenide Monolayers: An ab initio Embedding Model Study
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16085
Authors: Xiaohang Jia, Ryan Weiss, Yusong Bai, Brenda Rubenstein
Abstract: Engineering magnetic interactions in two-dimensional materials offers a promising route toward programmable magnetic phases and reconfigurable spintronic functionalities. Here, we investigate magnetic adatoms on metallic monolayer transition-metal dichalcogenides (TMDs) as a platform for tunable indirect exchange, focusing on Fe adatoms on monolayer TaS$_2$. In the intermediate-distance regime, DFT+U calculations reveal atomic-scale oscillations in the exchange coupling between two Fe adatoms and show that biaxial strain tunes the coupling. We develop a first-principles embedding model that reproduces the DFT-calculated pair exchange and establishes a substantial RKKY contribution to the exchange; controlled comparisons further identify RKKY-mediated coupling as the primary origin of the exchange's strain tunability. These results point to combining adatom arrangement with strain as a means of engineering reconfigurable magnetic interactions in metallic monolayer TMDs.

### Paper [95]
Title: Uncertainty Aware AFM-IR Hyperspectral Cube Reconstruction via Convolutional-Attentive Conditional Neural Processes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16118
Authors: William F. Kent, Roberto does Reis, Gajendra Shekhawat, Emma Alexander, Vinayak P. Dravid
Abstract: Atomic force microscopy-infrared spectroscopy (AFM-IR) maps chemical composition at nanoscale spatial resolution, but the time required to acquire individual spectra limits hyperspectral imaging. Here, we introduce a convolutional-attentive conditional neural process (CACNP), a neural process architecture for reconstructing AFM-IR hyperspectral cubes from sparsely sampled spectra and a small set of structure images, each acquired at a selected wavenumber. The model is trained entirely on synthetic cubes generated from experimental infrared spectra and predicts both spectral intensities and their associated uncertainty. Validation on experimental datasets of poly(methyl methacrylate) and polystyrene particles embedded in epoxy demonstrates accurate reconstruction using only 5% of the available spectra, with competitive performance when compared to other methods. Inference takes less than 0.1 s on 128 X 128 hyperspectral cubes, supporting rapid reconstruction updates, while predictive uncertainty provides a basis for adaptive sampling of localized chemical features. This approach advances autonomous, high-throughput AFM-IR workflows and offers an architecture for accelerating other hyperspectral imaging methods.

### Paper [96]
Title: Direct imaging of plasmonic coupling between gold nanoparticles and one-dimensional van der Waals materials
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16132
Authors: Ibrahim Tanriover, Arindom Nag, Jong-Ho Lee, Huy Hoang, Haihua Liu, Thang Pham
Abstract: Hybrids of plasmonic metals and van der Waals (vdW) materials combine electromagnetic field enhancement with the electronic properties of the vdW component, but the studies reported so far concern two-dimensional layers, and the near field at the metal/vdW interface is generally inferred from far-field measurements. Here we report the plasmonic coupling between Au nanoparticles and a one-dimensional (1D) vdW material, NbS3, and measure the near field at the Au-NbS3 contact directly. NbS3 nanowires and nanoribbons were grown by salt-assisted vapor-liquid-solid growth on graphene-covered TEM grids, and atomic-resolution electron microscopy shows atomically sharp Au/NbS3 interfaces. Photon-induced near-field electron microscopy at 1030 nm shows that an Au particle in contact with NbS3 produces a near field of higher intensity and larger extent than an isolated particle of similar size, and that the hot spot at a contact is excited only when the incident polarization is parallel to the particle-NbS3 axis. Finite-difference time-domain simulations reproduce these observations and quantify the dependence of the coupling on the particle-NbS3 gap, the NbS3 thickness, and the dimension ratio of the NbS3 structure. Size-specific Au nanoparticles on NbS3 nanowires enhance the Raman scattering of rhodamine 6G by a factor of 1.8-4.2 relative to Au nanoparticles alone, with a detection limit of 10^-7 M. These results extend plasmonic metal/vdW hybrids to 1D vdW crystals and establish a multimodal approach that correlates the atomic structure of a metal/vdW interface with its nanoscale plasmonic response.

### Paper [97]
Title: Humidity-modulated Ostwald Step Rule in $\rm FAPbI_3$ Martensitic Transitions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16147
Authors: Ping Tuo, Ju Li
Abstract: Experimental observations reveal a dual role of moisture in $\rm FAPbI_3$ phase transitions. During fabrication at high temperatures, humidity improves the quality of the perovskite ($\alpha$ phase) films, whereas at low service temperatures, humidity accelerates degradation of the photoactive $\alpha$ phase into the photo-inactive hexagonal $\delta$ phase. Despite extensive experimental evidence, the microscopic mechanisms by which moisture modulates phase stability and transition pathways remain unknown. Here, using large-scale atomistic simulations powered by an ab initio machine-learned potential, we reproduce the experimentally observed increase in the $\delta$-to-$\alpha$ transition temperature under increased humidity. Our simulations reveal a layered metastable intermediate along the Burgers transition pathway that exhibits a strong affinity for water. The competing water uptake of this intermediate and the $\delta$ phase governs the humidity dependence of transition kinetics. These findings elucidate the dual role of moisture in perovskite phase transitions and demonstrate how environmental species can stabilize metastable intermediates and reshape transition pathways, providing an environment-dependent perspective on Ostwald's step rule.

### Paper [98]
Title: Emergence of Phonon Hydrodynamics as a Non-Hermitian Symmetry-Breaking Phase Transition
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16161
Authors: Xin Qian, Guanda Quan, Ning Wang, Yaoming Chu, Run Hu, Te-Huan Liu, Xiangfan Xu, Jianming Cai, Ronggui Yang
Abstract: Phonon hydrodynamics enables collective heat flow beyond the classical diffusive limit and offers a promising route to controlling thermal transport in low-dimensional materials and nanoscale devices. However, quantitative criteria for determining when this hydrodynamic state emerges and where its boundaries lie remain a longstanding challenge in condensed matter physics. Here, we show that the onset of phonon hydrodynamics can be formulated as a non-Hermitian dynamical transition that converts microscopic phonon scattering properties into predictive boundaries for wavelike heat transport. Starting from the Peierls-Boltzmann transport equation, we derive an effective Schrodinger equation for the coupled dynamics of temperature and phonon drift fields, allowing the hydrodynamic regime to be identified directly from material transport properties. Within this framework, a pair of exceptional points quantitatively define the lower and upper boundaries of the hydrodynamic window, providing a critical design criterion beyond the qualitative scattering-rate inequalities proposed by Guyer and Krumhansl six decades ago. Furthermore, we derive an analytical criterion for the existence of hydrodynamic transport, showing that a finite hydrodynamic window can be supported only when the effective thermal diffusivity exceeds the phononic momentum diffusivity. Using ab initio phonon properties as inputs, our theory accurately captures the experimentally observed temperature- and length-dependent ultrafast second-sound dynamics. These results establish a symmetry-based framework for understanding collective energy transport and provide quantitative design principles for identifying the materials, temperatures, and characteristic length scales that support wavelike heat transport in low-dimensional materials and functional devices.

### Paper [99]
Title: Evidence of anisotropic surface state residual loss in UTe$_2$ amid isotropic low-energy excitations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16196
Authors: Arthur Carlton-Jones, Jared Z. Dans, Gicela Saucedo Salas, Shanta R. Saha, Johnpierre Paglione, Nicholas P. Butch, Steven M. Anlage
Abstract: We explore the pairing mechanism and possible topological properties of the unconventional superconductor UTe$_2$ through their influence on both the inductive and resistive channels using resonant cavity perturbation techniques. We measure a composite surface impedance for a variety of UTe$_2$ single crystals at microwave frequencies, where the inductive and resistive channels are comparable and can be treated on equal footing. By studying the composite electrodynamics for a large number of resonant modes, we are able to provide a comprehensive picture of the directional dependence of the excitations out of the ground state. We find similar power-law temperature dependencies at low-temperature for both the penetration depth and surface resistance, which in aggregate across modes, are quadratic and isotropic. On the other hand, we find a distinct and systematic anisotropy of the residual loss. The power-law results are inconsistent with a single pair of point nodes on the Fermi surface under weak scattering. We discuss the possibility of topological superconductivity as an interpretation of these isotropic power-laws in conjunction with the anisotropic residual loss.

### Paper [100]
Title: Controlled Growth and Disorder Mitigation of GaAs1-xSbx/InAs Core-Shell Quantum Wires
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16203
Authors: Ran Zhuo, Huading Song, Yanlin Wu, Wenlong Yang, Haiyan Shi, Xiyu Hou, Sijun Chen, Zhichuan Wang, Donghai Li, Jinjin Bai, Runan Shang, Hao Zhang, Dong Pan
Abstract: We report the growth and transport properties of GaAs1-xSbx/InAs core-shell nanowires. Compared with the well-studied GaAs-InAs system, incorporating Sb substantially reduces the lattice mismatch between the core and shell. Accordingly, both the nanowire crystal quality and device performance improved significantly. Increasing x from 0.15 to 0.35, the density of crystalline defects, such as stacking faults in the InAs shell, decreases by half. Moreover, the field-effect mobility increases from 3850 cm2/Vs to 4800 cm2/Vs, consistent with the reduction of the lattice mismatch. The incorporation of Sb makes the nanowire easier to pinch off via the gate voltage, enhancing the electric field tunability over the nanowire. Magneto-conductance measurements reveal Aharonov-Bohm-like oscillations, suggesting a phase-coherent tubular InAs shell. Our results identify lattice mismatch as a critical disorder source in core-shell nanowires and provide a practical route toward low-disorder core-shell quantum devices.

### Paper [101]
Title: High-Quality InSb Nanowires Grown by Molecular-Beam Epitaxy via Precise Catalyst Modulation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16234
Authors: Wenlong Yang, Jiaye Xu, Shuai Yang, Wenyu Song, Haiyan Shi, Donghai Li, Xiyu Hou, Sijun Chen, Ke He, Hao Zhang, Dong Pan
Abstract: Semiconductor InSb nanowires are a major material candidate for topological quantum computation. The nanowires in current devices were mostly grown using metal organic chemical vapor deposition, incompatible with in situ growth of superconductor, a crucial requirement for low-disorder devices. Growth of InSb nanowires using molecular-beam epitaxy (MBE) can address this issue. So far, studies on MBE-grown InSb remain limited due to an extremely narrow parameter window for the growth and reproducibility. Moreover, the use of foreign catalysts can introduce deep-level impurities. Here, we report a self-catalyzed MBE growth of InSb nanowires without those foreign catalysts. We first grow GaAs nanowire stems, and then convert Ga catalyst droplets into In droplets in situ. Axial heteroepitaxial growth of InSb can be realized through the In droplets on top of GaAs stems. We further find that a low flux suppresses radial growth, enabling linear length tuning from 0.8 to 2.6 micrometer with growth time, while diameter stays nearly constant. These InSb nanowires have a pure zinc-blende structure free of twins, stacking faults. Transport measurement reveals an electron mobility near 19000 cm2 V-1s-1. Moreover, the observation of quasi-ballistic transport, quantum dots, and Kondo correlations demonstrates the coherent quantum transport capability of the MBE-grown InSb nanowires. Our work provides a substrate-independent, self-catalyzed route for the controllable synthesis of high-quality InSb nanowires, establishing a robust material platform for high-performance InSb-based electronic and quantum devices.

### Paper [102]
Title: Anti-Kibble-Zurek behavior driven by dichotomous Markov noise in a quantum Ising chain: Generalized stochastic resonance in the defect density
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16237
Authors: Mingwei Yang, Peng Li
Abstract: The anti-Kibble-Zurek behavior may arise under noise, adding a noise-induced contribution to the defect density beyond the conventional Kibble-Zurek term. Most investigations of such behavior have so far been restricted to Gaussian noise. For Gaussian colored noise (GCN), the power spectrum dictates a monotonic variation of this term with the correlation time. Here, we focus on dichotomous Markov noise (DMN), a prototypical non-Gaussian colored noise, in the quantum Ising chain. We show that the noise-induced defect density, taken as the response function, exhibits generalized stochastic resonance: it is nonmonotonic in the correlation time, growing in the fast-noise regime and decreasing in the slow-noise regime. This resonance stems from the anomalous AKZ behavior in the slow-noise regime, where the DMN-induced trend is nonmonotonic, in contrast to the monotonic GCN case. The underlying mechanism lies in the sparse realizations of DMN in the slow-noise regime, a feature not captured by the power spectrum. Our work thereby reveals a fundamental distinction between Gaussian and non-Gaussian noises in the nonequilibrium critical quench dynamics of the quantum Ising chain.

### Paper [103]
Title: Quantum Cost of Emptiness in a One-Dimensional Bose Gas
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16247
Authors: Bin Huang, Ziyan Zhu
Abstract: The probability $P_0(R)$ of an empty interval of radius $R$ probes the far tail of density fluctuations in the thermodynamic ground state of the repulsive Lieb--Liniger gas. Here $\rho$ is the bulk density and $c$ the repulsion. As $\ell = \rho R \to \infty$ at fixed $\gamma = c/\rho > 0$, we find $-\log P_0 = f(\gamma)\ell^2 + B(\gamma)\sqrt{\ell} - \frac{1}{4}\log\ell + O_\gamma(1)$, with no linear term and $B = 2\zeta(3/2)\sqrt{f/(\pi\gamma)}$. Exact Gaussian completion of the auxiliary-field determinant reduces the leading collective response to an electrostatic slit problem, yielding $f(\gamma)$ in elliptic integrals. Its dipole polarization tail drives auxiliary scattering returns whose local density saturates while the active rapidity range expands as $\sqrt{R}$, producing the square-root boundary cost. The full return spectrum and the occupied-rapidity endpoints together fix the $-1/4$ logarithmic coefficient. Through the contact identity, widening the void probes a dense healing scale at the boundary whose growing characteristic occupation supports an emergent classical-field description even in a strongly interacting bulk.

### Paper [104]
Title: Noninvertible Lieb-Schultz-Mattis Constraints and Deconfined Criticality
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16249
Authors: Linhao Li, Zhen Bi
Abstract: We derive Lieb-Schultz-Mattis theorems for one-dimensional qudit systems with a class of internal noninvertible symmetries implemented by bilinear phase map operators. We show that any Hamiltonian preserving these symmetries together with translation or inversion symmetry must either be gapless or have degenerate ground states. Unlike conventional LSM constraints based on projective representations, these new constraints arise from nontrivial algebraic relations between the internal and crystalline symmetries. We illustrate this mechanism in a $\mathbb{Z}_4$ qudit model hosting two competing phases that spontaneously break the internal noninvertible symmetry and inversion symmetry, respectively. Numerical simulations reveal a direct continuous transition between these phases, realizing a stable deconfined quantum critical point involving an internal noninvertible symmetry.

### Paper [105]
Title: Studying temperature-induced decrease of spontaneous polarization in PbTiO$_3$ from vibrational amplitude
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16266
Authors: Zhikai Gao, Yuanji Xu, Fuyang Tian
Abstract: PbTiO$_3$ is a typical ferroelectric polarization induced negative thermal expansion (NTE) material at a wide range of temperature. The elegant TiO$_6$ octahedron is used to study the polarization and NTE, whereas lacking its quantitative characterization. In this work, we propose a concise projection of vibrational amplitude to describe anisotropic distortion of atoms with temperature based on $ab$ $initio$ molecular dynamics simulations. We find that the huge negative mode Gr\"uneisen coefficient is mainly from the different vibrational modes of the TiO$_6$ octahedral rotation at two boundary symmetry points in tetragonal phase along $c$ direction. The potential energy surface as a function of amplitude suggests the $c$-axis compression facilitates the TiO$_6$ octahedral rotation. We show that the vibrational mode of TiO$_6$ octahedral rotation around negative-expansion axis can promote spontaneous polarization reduction with increasing temperature, making transformation of PbTiO$_3$ from tetragonal to cubic phase occur, via the projected amplitude, potential energy surface, and forces of individual atoms.

### Paper [106]
Title: Optically-detected room-temperature spin-coherence from the GaV$^-$ in diamond
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16281
Authors: Adam D. Dodson, Tiamike Dudley, Luca Basso, Leopold Diaz, Jesse J. Lutz, Raj Patel, Matthew Markham, Jacob Henshaw, Andrew M. Mounce
Abstract: Solid-state quantum defects (point crystal imperfections with optically addressable spins) are among the most promising platforms for quantum information science due to their long-lived electron and nuclear spin lifetimes. The negatively-charged nitrogen-vacancy (NV$^-$) and negatively-charged group IV vacancies (G4V's) in diamond are some the most promising quantum defects but, the NV$^-$ is highly susceptible to charge noise and has low emission from its zero-phonon line while the G4V's require low operating temperatures. Here we report the physical properties of the newly discovered negatively-charged gallium-vacancy in diamond, GaV$^-$.
