We find that the GaV$^-$, created through FIB implantation and high-pressure high-temperature annealing, exhibits a combination of the best properties of both the NV$^-$ and G4V's: optically-detectable spin coherence at room temperature ($T_2(293K) = 1.06 \mu$s), a large Debye-Waller factor $\sim$0.4, a short radiative lifetime ($\sim$3.83 ns), strong temperature dependence of the zero-field splitting (16x that of the NV$^-$), and charge-noise-protected D$_{3d}$ symmetry. These features (along with improved performance at low temperature) position the newly discovered GaV$^-$ as a key resource for quantum sensing, networking, and computation.

### Paper [107]
Title: ORCHID-SAGE: Fully Reproducible CALPHAD Assessment from Evidence to Phase Diagram
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16308
Authors: Raymundo Arr\'oyave
Abstract: Knowledge of phase stability is the first requirement for materials design, and predicting the conditions under which new materials can be synthesized has become a key capability of self-driving laboratories (SDLs). For inorganic materials, notably alloys, these predictions come from thermodynamic databases built with the CALPHAD method. Building a database, however, requires expert judgment at many steps, and because these decisions are rarely recorded, others can use a database but cannot regenerate or update it. Machines can already collect the data, build a first model with machine-learned interatomic potentials and refine it against experiments. The assessment itself, the sequence of judgments that turns these parts into a reliable database, is still carried out by human experts. We argue that it can be automated reproducibly only if the expert knowledge behind an assessment is encoded in formal protocols, so that an assessment delivers a record of its construction and not only its parameters. We introduce ORCHID-SAGE, a standard that organizes this record into reproducible and automatable protocols. In this first attempt, applied to Cr-Ni, the database was regenerated from its record by a script on a second machine, by an AI agent working only through the Model Context Protocol (MCP) and by a refit from public sources only, with the same predictions. Recorded in this way, a database need no longer be fixed at publication but can be updated by AI agents as SDLs and other sources provide new evidence, while the judgment remains with people and on the record.

### Paper [108]
Title: Optimal staircase structure for bacterial adaptation in antibiotic landscapes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16325
Authors: Kento Matsukawa, Shin-ichi Sasa, Namiko Mitarai
Abstract: Spatial heterogeneity in antibiotic environments drives the evolution of bacterial resistance. However, how the discrete structure of these environments controls the time required for adaptation of bacteria remains unclear. We study this question using a stochastic model on a two-dimensional lattice whose axes represent physical space and phenotype space, comparing different numbers of steps while holding the overall spatial and concentration ranges fixed and scaling the migration and mutation rates by their end-to-end first-passage times. We find that increasing the number of steps accelerates the search for the next adapted state but increases the cumulative cost of repeated population saturation; this competition can produce an optimum at an intermediate number of steps. Direct Gillespie simulations reproduce the predicted dependence of the adaptation time on the number of steps, providing testable predictions for how discrete environmental structure shapes the timescale of evolutionary adaptation.

### Paper [109]
Title: Information Thermodynamics in Closed Quantum Many-Body Dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16329
Authors: Yusuke O. Nakai, Shoki Sugimoto, Ryusuke Hamazaki
Abstract: Although information thermodynamics explains how the second law is modified due to the information acquired by Maxwell's demon, its formulation in many-body systems remains largely open. Here, we develop an information-thermodynamic framework for closed quantum many-body systems initially in microscopic thermal equilibrium (MITE), under physically motivated restrictions: local quantum measurements and finite-time controls generated by local Hamiltonians. By additionally assuming that each local feedback term depends only on nearby measurement outcomes, we rigorously prove that the extractable work coincides in the thermodynamic limit with that extractable from the corresponding canonical ensemble. We further express this extractable work in terms of quantum-information quantities, including the quantum-classical mutual information and the Holevo information for the corresponding canonical ensemble. We also show that feedback based on distant measurement outcomes can exploit long-range correlations hidden in MITE, yielding an extensive increase in extracted work relative to the canonical ensemble.

### Paper [110]
Title: Dynamic Tunneling a Prior Phenomenon to Intermediate Resistive States in Defect Engineered Mn-SrTiO3 RRAM Devices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16380
Authors: Gowtham Velpandi, Ishaan Tushir, Durairaj Murugan, Thanumalayaperumal Thirunavukarasu, Ranjith Ramadurai
Abstract: Resistive random-access memory devices work under the principle of resistive switching from high resistive state to low resistive state under applied electric field. In this work, 2%Mn doped SrTiO3, devices were fabricated using PLD in which, the defects are engineered such that the conducting filament formation is expected to have two oppositely charged defects: a) presence of Mn3+ dopants, giving rise to a negatively charged Mn and Ti and b) single and double positive oxygen vacancies. This process of two filaments emanating from both the electrodes has shown enhancement in the resistive switching characteristics aiding in forming-free switching that exhibits over 102 as ON/OFF ratio with a memory retention beyond 103 seconds at a low read voltage of 0.1V. All devices demonstrated a stable bipolar resistive switching dominated by conduction filament formation resulting from a synergic effect of oxygen vacancies and the varying oxidation states of Mn based negative defects that propagates toward each other under bias. The formation of defect-based filaments around the bias electrode was evidently observed using XPS of the devices before and after switching. A dynamic tunnelling process is observed as an intermediate process before the conduction filament forms in the SET region and following the snapping in the RESET region. Conduction filaments being heavily doped localizes conduction pathways with a small finite tunnelling barrier gap that are instantaneously formed and ruptures during switching cycles. The dynamic tunnelling behavior gives rise to a tiny spike in current and is observed at the intermediate resistive states which are potential for multi-state usage of these devices. Overall, Mn-STO devices of varying thicknesses show promising retention features and defect engineering offers control over single and/or multi-states memory.

### Paper [111]
Title: Continuous-Time Random Walks with Internal Stochastic Renewal Process
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16383
Authors: Boris Z. Huang
Abstract: We introduce a continuous-time random walk process via a structured density by tracking the state of the particle as the system evolves. Whenever the particle makes a jump, its internal state either increases by one or resets to zero, so that the internal state evolves according to a renewal process. A wide range of governing integro-differential equations may be obtained from this formulation, some of which exhibit various types of anomalous behaviour. For certain choices of reset probabilities, waiting times and jump distributions, we explicitly derive the mode-dependent memory kernels and investigate their continuum limits. The framework can recover the time-and-space-fractional Fokker-Planck equation, which traditionally models the interplay between long wait times and large jumps and exhibits anomalous diffusion. Furthermore, we obtain a process which converges toward the equilibrium distribution as time approaches some finite value through a sequence of waiting times which decrease geometrically. Finally, we investigate a process that incorporates self-excitation and self-inhibition into the waiting times for each state to produce a mean square displacement proportional to $t^\nu$ for any $\nu > 0$. Through these examples, we illustrate the link between the microscopic mechanisms in these processes and their non-local macroscopic governing equations.

### Paper [112]
Title: Quantum geometry drives orbital paramagnetism in superconducting fluctuations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16387
Authors: Zi-Ting Sun, Ying-Ming Xie, Chunli Huang, Naoto Nagaosa
Abstract: Superconductors expel magnetic fields through the Meissner effect. Even slightly above the superconducting transition temperature, electrons can briefly form Cooper pairs, leading to an orbital diamagnetic susceptibility that diverges as the transition is approached. In this work, we use Gaussian fluctuation theory to study a generic multicomponent superconductor above its transition temperature and show that fluctuations can instead produce a paramagnetic response. We identify two competing contributions: the usual diamagnetic contribution from the center-of-mass motion of Cooper pairs and a paramagnetic contribution from interference between different components of the superconducting order parameter. The latter is related to pair quantum geometry, which describes how the internal components of the Cooper pair change with momentum. We then apply our theory to a model of a kagome superconductor with two chiral pairing modes. When the parent state preserves time-reversal symmetry, the paramagnetic contribution can outweigh the conventional diamagnetic contribution, producing net fluctuation paramagnetism. However, when the orbital time-reversal symmetry is broken, e.g., by forming a weak loop-current order, the degeneracy between the two chiral modes is then lifted. Upon cooling toward the superconducting transition, the lower-energy mode dominates the fluctuations, and diamagnetism wins as single-component physics takes over. Our results provide a mechanism for fluctuation paramagnetism in superconductors and show that the orbital magnetic response can probe the quantum geometry of Cooper pairs.

### Paper [113]
Title: Why "quantum spin liquids'' are quantum singlet liquids
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16428
Authors: Sergey Syzranov, Arthur P. Ramirez
Abstract: The last couple of decades have seen enormous theoretical and experimental research efforts aimed at finding quantum spin liquids (QSLs) in geometrically frustrated (GF) magnetic materials. Experimental demonstrations of QSLs are challenging due to the indirect character of possible QSL signatures and the presence of impurities in materials, which may destroy or obscure a QSL. At the same time, theoretical literature often lacks consensus about the ground states of quantum spins on even the simplest GF magnetic lattices. We summarize experimental evidence that in the impurity-free limit, available GF magnetic materials that do not develop magnetic order at low temperatures would form {\it a quantum singlet liquid}, i.e. a disordered state of non-magnetic excitations. We further confirm the existence of such a singlet liquid and its gapless character with theoretical arguments. Such a liquid manifests itself in the thermodynamics of GF magnets while having no magnetic features at low temperatures.

### Paper [114]
Title: Multi-reference variational states resolve stripe selection and local $d$-wave pairing in the Hubbard model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16430
Authors: Honghui Shang, Jinlong Yang
Abstract: Whether stripes compete with $d$-wave superconductivity or promote pairing through fluctuations remains unsettled in the two-dimensional Hubbard model. Using multi-reference neural variational auxiliary-field quantum Monte Carlo (MR-nVAFQMC) at $U/t=8$, we superpose unpaired Slater determinants across stripe periods and domain-wall positions, reaching variational energies competitive with leading benchmarks on periodic lattices up to $32\times32$ sites. For cuprate next-nearest-neighbor hopping ($t'/t=-0.2$), diagonal hopping preserves electron-doped antiferromagnetism while kinetically frustrating hole motion into Yamada stripes with a dome of short-range connected $d$-wave pairing. At $1/8$ hole doping, hole delocalization across antiphase spin boundaries outweighs on-site repulsion, splitting the filled stripes of the pure model into half-filled walls with strong local pairing. Coherently delocalizing shifted walls strongly suppresses static charge modulation yet leaves short-range pairing unchanged, showing that local pairing is intrinsic to half-filled walls rather than driven by stripe fluctuations. Across intervening antiferromagnetic domains, however, pairing correlations become unresolved at one stripe period and beyond. Both parameter transfer to larger lattices without reoptimization and deep benchmark checkpoints retain stripe spin order without resolving long-range pairing, within uncertainties allowing weak superconductivity. Half-filled antiphase walls thus generate local $d$-wave pairs, whereas suppressing static stripe order alone yields no resolved coherence across stripes in the states studied.

### Paper [115]
Title: Thermal Hall sign reversal from competing magnons and magnon pairs in pyrochlore spin-nematic magnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16440
Authors: Tetsuya Sato, Emika Takata, Masahiro Sato
Abstract: Bound magnon pairs are central to spin-nematic order, yet experimentally distinguishing them from single magnons remains challenging. We show that their Berry-curvature-driven heat transport provides a characteristic signature in the thermal Hall response. For a spin-$3/2$ pyrochlore antiferromagnet with ferromagnetic biquadratic exchange in the magnetic-field-induced fully polarized phase, we construct single-magnon bands together with magnon-pair bands, include the Dzyaloshinskii--Moriya (DM) interaction, and evaluate their intrinsic thermal Hall conductivities. The lowest magnon and magnon-pair bands carry Berry curvatures of opposite sign. Because the gaps of the two quasiparticles respond differently to magnetic field, their competing contributions can drive both magnetic-field- and temperature-induced sign reversals of the thermal Hall conductivity. Estimates for chromium spinel oxides indicate that the effect can approach experimentally accessible magnitudes. We also find that the hybridization between the one- and two-magnon sectors induced by the DM interaction preserves this qualitative signature. These results suggest that a broad class of frustrated magnets with both magnons and magnon pairs provides a possible route to tuning the sign of the thermal Hall conductivity and probing bound magnon pairs through thermal transport experiments.

### Paper [116]
Title: Ferroelectric control of exciton photoluminescence in rhombohedrally stacked bilayer WSe$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16475
Authors: Wenlong Zhong, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Xiaoxiang Xi
Abstract: Sliding ferroelectricity offers a non-volatile route to electrically programmable electronic and optical functionality, but deterministic control of excitonic photoluminescence is often obscured by moir\'{e} reconstruction and domain averaging. Here we study an artificially assembled WSe$_2$ bilayer with near-zero twist in rhombohedral stacking and observe reversible, hysteretic electrical switching through excitonic photoluminescence. Under an out-of-plane electric field, momentum-indirect QK exciton emission exhibits Stark-slope reversals and abrupt energy jumps, resolving a four-stage sequence separated by two band realignments and one ferroelectric switching event. Polarization reversal also selects the momentum-direct KK interlayer-exciton branch that hybridizes with an intralayer exciton, producing history-dependent photoluminescence. Ferroelectric modulation of resonant Raman scattering provides a complementary readout, with hysteretic intensities correlated with the polarization-dependent interlayer field. These results establish an exciton-based quantification of the electronic structure in ferroelectric WSe$_2$ and open a route to electrically programmable optoelectronic functionality.

### Paper [117]
Title: Probing the Dirac Quantum Geometry in Quantum Magnets from Polarized Raman Spectroscopy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16491
Authors: Yuki Yamazaki, Doh Young Kim, Takahiro Morimoto, Bohm-Jung Yang
Abstract: Magnetic Raman spectroscopy is sensitive to quasiparticle quantum geometry, yet microscopic Raman vertices generally prevent a direct geometric interpretation. Here we establish a symmetry-based extraction principle for two-dimensional quantum magnets with two-band quasiparticle excitations. Opposite circular-polarization spectra separate vertex-weighted quantum-metric and Berry-curvature contractions. Near a $C_3$-symmetric massive Dirac valley, symmetry fixes the leading Raman and velocity doublets up to a common scale, allowing the unknown Raman strength and intensity normalization to cancel from their spectral ratio. For an inversion-related pair of massive Dirac cones, the normalized counter-circular Raman asymmetry at frequency threshold $\Omega_{\rm th}$ is fixed by the Chern number and follows a universal near-threshold frequency scaling. This yields a local spectroscopic readout without absolute-intensity calibration or frequency integration. We demonstrate the principle for Majorana fermions in the field-induced honeycomb Kitaev spin liquid and for topological magnons in a honeycomb ferromagnet, and identify the conditions for resolving it experimentally.

### Paper [118]
Title: Edge-scaling softening of a universal machine-learned potential: point-defect formation energies in a coronene graphene quantum dot
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16528
Authors: Andrii Tuzhykov, Vladimir Soloviev, Arnold Kiv, Andrii Bielinskyi
Abstract: Universal machine-learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) are increasingly used as computationally efficient surrogates for density functional theory (DFT) in high-throughput nanomaterials screening. Systematic softening has been reported for several universal MLIPs when high-energy and far-from-equilibrium configurations are underrepresented in training data. We examine how this behavior appears at under-coordinated edge sites in graphene quantum dots (GQDs), where a vacancy can have pronounced molecular-radical character. Using coronene (C$_{24}$H$_{12}$), a hexagonal GQD with $D_{6h}$ symmetry and three carbon classes, we benchmark SevenNet (7net-omni-i12) against spin-polarized PBE DFT (GPAW) for monovacancy, divacancy, and Stone-Wales defects. Beyond the full site- and bond-resolved landscapes, we perform a controlled matched-reconstruction comparison in which 28 vacancy and divacancy configurations are scored by both engines using self-consistent chemical-potential reservoirs. In this dataset, SevenNet underestimates all 28 vacancy and divacancy formation energies, and the mean deficit increases with under-coordination within each defect family: 0.30-0.74 eV for monovacancies and 0.65-1.11 eV for divacancies as the number of broken edge C-H bonds increases. By contrast, the mean Stone-Wales energies differ by only 0.013 eV. SevenNet also preserves the ordering of three hydrogen-dependent reconstruction basins and reproduces $D_{6h}$-equivalent sites to within a few meV. The within-class symmetry spread therefore provides a practical diagnostic of basin-sensitive relaxations. Within the present coronene benchmark, these results support MLIP-based structure screening and preliminary ranking, while absolute vacancy energies and multi-basin edge configurations still require DFT validation.

### Paper [119]
Title: Thermal and dynamical behaviors of the spin-nematic phases in a spin-1 triangular lattice
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16532
Authors: Lei Xu, Zhentao Wang
Abstract: The triangular lattice compound Na$_2$BaNi(PO$_4$)$_2$ has been recently established as a strong candidate for realizing the spin-nematic physics. In this theoretical work, we study the finite-temperature properties and the dynamics of a spin-1 triangular lattice model, which reproduces the spin-nematic physics and provides insights relevant to the compound. We show that a small biquadratic interaction can be used for stabilizing the SN phases in the semiclassical approach with SU(3) coherent states, which allows for a mathematical parameterization of the spin-nematic wave functions. With a semiclassical Monte Carlo calculation, the finite temperature transitions into the spin-nematic phases are shown to be related to BKT transitions associated with the proliferation of half vortices in the original spin-1 model. Using quantum Monte Carlo calculations with a low-energy effective model, the phase boundaries are determined and found to agree qualitatively with the semiclassical analysis. Within the spin wave approach, the low-energy spin dynamics can be captured equally well by either the spin-1 model with a small biquadratic term, or the effective spin-$1/2$ model. The accidental degeneracy of the semiclassical approach at zero field is revealed by explicit analytical parameterization, and is shown to be associated with the pseudo-Goldstone mode observed in the spin dynamics.

### Paper [120]
Title: Bulk CMSX-4 Single-Crystal Builds via Laser-Directed Energy Deposition: Epitaxial Stability and Microstructure Evolution
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16535
Authors: Divya Nalajala, Swapnil Bhure, R. Ajithkannan, Shubhang Galagali, Abhik Choudhury
Abstract: Single-crystal processing of Ni-based superalloys using laser-directed energy deposition (L-DED) is extremely challenging, given the interplay of a number of processing parameters. In this work, we develop strategies to maintain single-crystal growth in long cylindrical rod geometries by combining dynamic adjustment of laser power, informed by in-situ infrared thermal feedback, with a simulation-guided external cooling system. This combined approach ensures optimal heat extraction during additive manufacturing, maintaining near-steady-state conditions at the solidification interface and extending continuous epitaxial growth to nearly twice the build height achievable through process control alone. The developed strategy results in continuous single-crystal growth, as validated by electron backscatter diffraction (EBSD). The steady-state nature of solidification is revealed through the near-constant primary dendritic arm spacing. Further, the heat-treatment is optimized by reducing the solutionizing time (by 47\%), resulting in faster eutectic dissolution. Comparison of the aged microstructures reveals a similar distribution of precipitates between the classical Bridgman- and L-DED-processed samples.

### Paper [121]
Title: Noise-Protected Superconducting Qudits from Higher-Harmonic Josephson Junctions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16563
Authors: Hamed Vakili
Abstract: Qudits, which generalize qubits to $d$ levels, store more information per circuit element than qubits. As with qubits, however, their sensitivity to noise must be overcome before they can be used for fault-tolerant quantum computing. Here we show that, by exploiting the second harmonic of the current--phase relation, a superconducting loop of three Josephson junctions can host qutrits strongly protected against relaxation, while dephasing limits their coherence. When the second harmonic is positive, a simple loop with a small asymmetry between the junctions provides a tunable, symmetry-protected qutrit. When it is negative, a protected qutrit can be realized by adding a small symmetry-breaking term, such as a weak Josephson diode effect. The two designs involve different trade-offs: the first has an exact charge selection rule, whereas the second is nearly insensitive to charge noise and its quasiparticle errors can be corrected. The preferable choice therefore depends on the materials used. Both require a sizable second harmonic to begin with, and we identify altermagnetic Josephson junctions as a candidate platform. Finally, microscopic calculations show that a gate voltage selects the sign of the second harmonic in these junctions, although gate-voltage noise then becomes the leading source of dephasing. These findings establish the sign of the second harmonic as a design principle for protected superconducting qudits.

### Paper [122]
Title: Micromagnetic modelling and simulation of inertial magnetization dynamics in ferromagnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16567
Authors: Massimiliano d'Aquino
Abstract: Magnetization dynamics at sub-picosecond time scales can exhibit inertial effects beyond the conventional Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert description, giving rise to nutational motion at terahertz frequencies. Following its theoretical prediction and experimental observation in ferromagnets, magnetic inertia has attracted increasing interest for understanding and controlling ultrafast spin dynamics. In this topical review, we discuss the theoretical modelling and micromagnetic simulation of inertial magnetization dynamics in ferromagnetic nanostructures. Starting from the mechanical analogy underlying the inertial Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert equation, we introduce its spatially inhomogeneous micromagnetic formulation and discuss its main properties, including magnetization-length conservation, angular-momentum relaxation, and the generalized free-energy balance. We then review the linear dynamics of inertial spin waves, highlighting the coexistence of GHz precessional and THz nutational branches, the inertial redshift of precessional modes, spectral linewidths, and the effects of exchange, magnetostatic interactions, confinement, curvature and torsion. Particular attention is devoted to the large-wavenumber regime, where magnetic inertia leads to a finite limiting spin-wave propagation velocity. Finally, we address frequency- and time-domain micromagnetic simulation techniques for spatially nonuniform inertial dynamics, including geometric midpoint integrators preserving the fundamental constraints and energy properties of the continuous dynamics. Applications to the excitation and propagation of ultrashort nutational spin waves and ultrafast inertial magnetization switching illustrate the role of micromagnetic modelling in connecting inertial Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert theory with experimentally accessible nanoscale magnetization dynamics.

### Paper [123]
Title: Elasto-inertial force exerted on a sphere oscillating in a fluid near a soft interface
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16575
Authors: Axel Batalha (LOMA, L2C), Harshit Joshi (LOMA), Yacine Amarouchene (LOMA), Thomas Salez (LOMA, X-DEP-MECA)
Abstract: Fluid inertia can remain important in confined flows, even at low Reynolds number when convective inertia is negligible; yet its coupling with interface deformability is an open problem. Here, we theoretically investigate the high-frequency oscillations of an immersed sphere near an elastic wall in the lubrication regime. We first reconcile previous predictions for the added mass near a rigid wall by consistently matching the inner lubrication flow with the outer flow. Using this rigid-wall problem as a reference, we then develop a perturbative analysis of wall deformation and derive the leading elasto-inertial correction to the hydrodynamic force. Our results reveal that the coupling between interface softness and fluid inertia produces a dominant non-dissipative force contribution scaling as the power four of the frequency in the high-frequency limit, thus corresponding to the fourth time derivative of the sphere's position. This finding may have implications regarding the short-time dynamics of colloidal particles near deformable interfaces in biological and soft-matter settings.

### Paper [124]
Title: Small-angle scattering reveals heterogeneous nanoparticle size distributions through Bayesian inference
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16587
Authors: Songhao Yin, Yushiro Mori, Yui Hayashi, Zuolin Meng, Lester Canque Geonzon, Hiroyoshi Nakano, Hiroshi Noguchi, Masato Okada, Koichi Mayumi, Kumiko Hayashi
Abstract: Nanoparticles have attracted considerable attention owing to their broad range of applications. Surface functionalization further expands their functionality, making them useful in fields such as biomedicine, catalysis, sensing, and environmental remediation. In such applications, nanoparticles may form clusters through aggregation under high ionic-strength conditions, while biologically derived nanoparticles can be intrinsically heterogeneous in size. In this study, we apply small-angle neutron scattering (SANS) and small-angle X-ray scattering (SAXS) to nanoparticles and use Bayesian inference to analyze their heterogeneous particle-size distributions. Because SAXS and SANS probe the wavevector dependence of the static scattering intensity, they can sensitively resolve two distinct particle sizes even when a small fraction (< 10%) of larger particles is mixed with smaller particles. We construct multimodal particle-size distribution models within a Bayesian framework and demonstrate the importance of model selection for accurately estimating heterogeneous particle-size distributions. This approach should be broadly applicable to nanoparticle characterization across a wide range of applications.

### Paper [125]
Title: Sign changing pairing in single layer FeSe/SrTiO3 revealed by nonmagnetic impurity bound states
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16679
Authors: Huimin Zhang, Zhuozhi Ge, Michael Weinert, Lian Li
Abstract: The discovery of high-temperature superconductivity in single layer FeSe epitaxially grown on SrTiO3(001) substrates has instigated extensive debate over whether its pairing symmetry is conventional sign-preserving s-wave or unconventional sign-changing. Here we probe the pairing state for single layer FeSe/SrTiO3 grown by molecular beam epitaxy using scanning tunneling microscopy/spectroscopy. We observe robust in-gap bound states induced by non-magnetic Fe-vacancy defects within the Fe-plane, which exhibit strong spatial electron-hole asymmetry with two-fold symmetry in hole states and four-fold in electron states. The bound states exhibit no energy shift or splitting under an applied magnetic field, consistent with a sign-changing order parameter.

### Paper [126]
Title: Dislocation Arrays Hosting Topological Bound States in Epitaxial Tetragonal FeSe Films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16681
Authors: Huimin Zhang, Basu Oli, Zhengfei Wang, Michael Weinert, Lian Li
Abstract: Topological defects in real space can host robust, localized quantum states for quantum information processing and sensing, yet deterministic control over their formation in crystalline materials remain challenging.

### Paper [127]
Title: A superconducting quantum point contact in Ge
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16705
Authors: Anna Tacchini, Inga Seidler, Isabell Grandt-Ionita, Thibault Thuegaz, Stephen W. Bedell, Fabrizio Nichele
Abstract: Hybrid superconducting-semiconducting circuits provide a versatile platform for investigating coherent transport in mesoscopic weak links. Superconducting quantum point contacts (SQPCs) are particularly interesting in this context, as their supercurrent is carried by a discrete number of ballistic transport modes. Here, we investigate a gate-tunable superconducting quantum point contact realized in Ge/SiGe. By incorporating the QPC into a highly asymmetric superconducting quantum interference device (SQUID), we gain access to its current-phase relation (CPR) and switching current, enabling phase-sensitive studies of few-mode superconducting transport. We observe a direct correspondence between conductance quantization and switching-current quantization, linking the normal-state transport modes to the superconducting response. In addition, we demonstrate strong gate tunability of the effective transmission near the onset of the first conductance plateau. Under microwave irradiation, the QPC exhibits photon-assisted multiple Andreev reflections at lower transparencies and Shapiro replicas in the high-transparency, few-mode regime.

### Paper [128]
Title: Metric random tilings: exact entropy and the diffraction of counted disorder
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16730
Authors: Xinyan Wu, Qingqing Ji
Abstract: Tiling mathematics has twice become the language of a state of matter: crystals, then quasicrystals, whose thermodynamics are described by the random tiling of fixed shapes. There the counted variable is the arrangement rather than the geometry, although the metric is what chemical design sets and diffraction measures. Here we introduce metric random tilings, periodic networks of locked topology whose edge lengths fluctuate under exact face closure, so that the geometry itself is countable. Purpose-built counting engines enumerate Kepler's eleven uniform networks and divide them into seven zero-entropy ones and four carriers with positive metric entropy, each certified by an explicit construction. On the honeycomb, the densest carrier, all edge-length value domains considered carry extensive entropy, with the arithmetic class reaching $s = 0.544\,k_B$ per tile (fit band 0.53--0.58), the other two $\approx 0.2$. In diffraction, translational increase of the value domain leaves the entropy exactly invariant while the coherent fraction of the Bragg intensity at fixed patch size varies from $9\times10^{-4}$ to 0.58, following the Debye--Waller law of the amplitude flow. Metric disorder thus enters the exact-counting paradigm, and is identifiable in real materials by Bragg spots wrapped in metric halos.

### Paper [129]
Title: Freezing transition and shock-like transients in ballistic random walks under threshold resetting
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16733
Authors: Rahul Das, Satya N Majumdar, Arnab Pal
Abstract: We investigate the transient dynamics of a one-dimensional ballistic random walker under threshold resetting (TR). The walker starts at $x_0>0$, moves towards a threshold at the origin with a velocity drawn from a distribution $\phi(v)$, and on reaching the threshold is reset to $x_0$ with a new sampled velocity. Whenever the mean first-passage time to the threshold is finite, the walker reaches a steady state that is uniform on $[0,x_0]$ and independent of $\phi(v)$. The relaxation towards it, in contrast, is highly sensitive to the behavior of $\phi(v)$. For a large class of $\phi(v)$-s, we find, rather remarkably, that the relaxation rate depends explicitly on the spatial coordinate, in contrast to conventional relaxation phenomena, where the rate is typically position-independent. Furthermore, this space-dependent relaxation rate eventually freezes to a constant, position-independent value beyond a critical position, a phenomenon we identify as \textit{freezing transition}. Additionally, for a power-law velocity distribution, the system exhibits an anomalously slow, algebraic relaxation towards the steady state. We also showed that a sharp cutoff in $\phi(v)$ generates a shock-like discontinuity in the particle propagator that travels towards the threshold, with a jump height that decays with time. These results demonstrate how velocity fluctuations, despite leaving the steady state unchanged, can profoundly influence the transient spatial structures and showcase rich non-equilibrium behavior. Our analytical results are corroborated by Monte Carlo simulations, showing an excellent agreement.

### Paper [130]
Title: Tailored magnetic domain-wall ratchet effects through density gradients in triangular-hole arrays
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16735
Authors: Julia Herrero-Albillos, Mar\'ia \'Angeles Laguna-Marco, Mar\'ia V\'elez, Xabier Zubizarreta-Iriarte, Daniel Arechavaleta, Aurelio Hierro-Rodriguez, Carlos Quir\'os, Michael Foerster, Cristina Bran, Arantxa Fraile-Rodriguez, Jos\'e Ignacio Mart\'in
Abstract: We investigate domain wall (DW) dynamics and morphology in amorphous Co$_{0.86}$Zr$_{0.14}$ thin films patterned with engineered, density-graded triangular-hole arrays. Using Kerr microscopy and high-resolution XMCD-PEEM imaging, we demonstrate that DW propagation is governed by the interplay between hole orientation, density gradients, and local magnetostatic interactions. In cooperative configurations, the density gradient reinforces the geometric ratchet effect, whereas in competing configurations, the two mechanisms oppose each other, leading to an inverse ratchet and enabling experimental access to backward DW propagation. Remarkably, density-graded arrays substantially reduce the stochastic variability of depinning fields observed in periodic arrays, resulting in highly reproducible DW motion. The depinning fields are described by a rigid DW model and an empirical scaling law incorporating both inter-hole spacing and array periodicity. These results demonstrate that engineered density gradients provide an effective route to deterministic control of DW propagation, with potential applications in spintronic and neuromorphic devices.

### Paper [131]
Title: Work extraction from a single negative-temperature bath: a temperature gradient-driven heat flow
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16743
Authors: Wolfgang Rudolf Bauer
Abstract: Work dissipation into a thermal bath is standard, whereas the reverse process -- sole work extraction from a thermal bath without a net state change -- contradicts the Kelvin-Planck formulation of the second law of thermodynamics. However, this prohibition is bypassed for baths at negative absolute temperature, sparking ongoing debate regarding their thermodynamic stability. Here, we reconcile the second law with sole work extraction from a negative-temperature bath by considering a large virtual assembly of weakly coupled working systems rather than an isolated machine. By assigning this assembly a canonical temperature derived from its Shannon/information entropy, the framework naturally extends to molecular-scale machines. We demonstrate paradigmatic examples where the assembly's canonical temperature is cooler than the negative-temperature bath. Consequently, sole work extraction represents standard heat flow from a hot system (the negative-temperature bath) to a cooler system (the working assembly), rendering the process fully consistent with the second law. This informational and statistical-mechanical framework also naturally explains the counter-intuitive phenomenon of "donkey behavior", where increasing the workload in a negative-temperature bath leads to enhanced work extraction.

### Paper [132]
Title: Detecting non-Hermitian topology from long-time dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16745
Authors: Antonio D'Abbruzzo, Rosario Fazio
Abstract: We propose a protocol to measure the non-Hermitian topological invariant associated with the Lindbladian spectrum of chiral-symmetric open Gaussian systems from the relaxation of the covariance matrix. Importantly, the method works in the long-time regime and does not rely on isolating edge modes, so it remains applicable even when the Liouvillian skin effect may obscure them. We illustrate the technique on a Kitaev chain with reservoir-induced nonreciprocity, for which we first obtain a fully analytical and previously unknown description of its topological phase diagram. We then show that our protocol reliably distinguishes all the different phases.

### Paper [133]
Title: Physical interpretation of spin-glass models with correlated disorder
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16765
Authors: Hidetoshi Nishimori
Abstract: Ising spin-glass models with a specific type of correlated bond disorder have recently been used to relate properties of spin-glass phases, such as temperature chaos and magnetic-field chaos, to ferromagnetic order. This correlated disorder has a simple physical interpretation, which underlies the sampling procedure introduced by Oshima, Arai, and Hukushima for numerical simulations: the bond configuration is obtained from the Edwards-Anderson model with the symmetric $\pm J$ distribution by drawing one of its equilibrium spin configurations and writing the model in the gauge in which this reference configuration becomes the all-up state. The construction is analogous to the Mattis model, with the roles of the ferromagnet and the spin glass reversed: whereas the Mattis model is a ferromagnet in disguise, the ferromagnetic phase of the correlated model is essentially a spin-glass phase in disguise. The frustration statistics of the correlated-disorder model are exactly those of the symmetric $\pm J$ model, and, in a generalization with an additional parameter $\gamma$, the probabilities of the gauge equivalence classes are determined by $\gamma$ alone, whereas the inverse temperature at which the reference configuration is drawn determines only the distribution within each class. The magnetization of this model is the overlap with the reference configuration. Through this correspondence, studies of its ferromagnetic phase have led to unexpected results on temperature chaos and magnetic-field chaos in the spin-glass phase of the Edwards-Anderson model. Models with correlated disorder have thus provided a new perspective on spin glasses and have proved fertile ground for highly nontrivial results on the spin-glass problem.

### Paper [134]
Title: Large Pockels Effect in Multiaxial Ferroelectrics Induced by Polarization Reorientation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16773
Authors: Zhijun Jiang, Jinyang Ni, Hong Jian Zhao
Abstract: Materials with large linear electro-optic (Pockels) effect have significant potential for fabricating energy-efficient photonic devices. Previous strategies have mostly relied on structural softness---associated with phase boundary, domain wall, or strain induced phase transition---to create large Pockels effect in ferroelectrics. In this Letter, we show that polarization reorientation in multiaxial ferroelectrics may create structural softness and thus induce large Pockels effect. By developing a phenomenological theory, we find that multiaxial ferroelectrics exhibit a hysteretic relationship between the azimuth angles of the polarization and the electric field, and a possible divergence of the linear electro-optic coefficient may occur at an appropriate polarization azimuth angle. This suggests a brand-new strategy for engineering large Pockels effect in multiaxial ferroelectrics. We perform first-principles simulations on several multiaxial ferroelectrics and predict that polarization reorientation can significantly enhance the electro-optic coefficients in monolayer SnSe and bulk KNbO$_3$. This validates the soundness of our strategy and emphasizes multiaxial ferroelectrics as promising platforms toward large Pockels effect.

### Paper [135]
Title: Learning to control and predict light-driven electron-phonon dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16780
Authors: Matteo Puviani
Abstract: Driving lattice vibrations provides a direct route to manipulating electronic correlations through nonlinear electron-phonon coupling. Finding suitable driving fields, however, requires control over the coupled dynamics beyond a simple choice of pulse amplitude and frequency. Here we develop a differentiable framework that combines field optimization with neural-network prediction in a two-site electron-phonon model. We compare piecewise-constant, B-spline, feed-forward, and recurrent control parameterizations, optimized to increase the accumulated electronic double occupation. For the dissipation-free model at zero Hubbard interaction, all the methods increase double occupation, with the B-spline raising the mean total double occupation of approximately 30%. Alongside control optimization, a separate recurrent network learns to predict the next-step double occupation and phonon observables from their preceding values and the applied field. These results establish a computational proof of concept for shaping local electronic correlations through a phonon drive and identify a promising route toward learned models for nonequilibrium materials control.

### Paper [136]
Title: Systematic Evaluation of Training Data Generation Strategies for Machine Learning-Based Molecular and Materials Property Prediction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16792
Authors: Minh Nghia Nguyen, Beate Paulus, Akhil S. Nair
Abstract: Machine-learning (ML) models for molecular and materials property prediction are often trained on large datasets curated through arbitrary or generic sampling strategies. Such approaches can lead to the over- or under-representation of regions of chemical and materials space that are most relevant to the prediction task. An important challenge is therefore how to efficiently curate training data to maximize predictive performance while minimising the number of labelled examples required. Here, we systematically compare training data acquisition strategies for molecular and materials property prediction, ranging from random sampling to various active learning approaches. We evaluate their performance across different model classes, representations, datasets, and target properties. Our results show that the effectiveness of data acquisition strategies depend strongly on the interplay between the model, dataset, and prediction task, although uncertainty-based sampling generally outperforms alternative strategies. Based on these findings, we derive guidelines for selecting data acquisition strategies under a given labelling budget.

### Paper [137]
Title: Certifying Majorana persistence through Hamiltonian learning from boundary measurements
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16832
Authors: Tohid Farajollahpour, Alireza Qaiumzadeh
Abstract: Finite measurements at a chain end can certify local quantum dynamics without determining bulk topology. We combine Hamiltonian learning with commutator bounds to certify the persistence of localized Majorana operators in Kitaev chains. Time-resolved single-shot boundary measurements and local bond calibrations define joint confidence sets for local Hamiltonian coefficients. Under explicit device assumptions, these sets yield state-independent lower bounds on symmetrized autocorrelations with a prescribed failure probability, valid for all compatible continuations. With equal hopping and pairing on the first bond and no additional boundary terms, an exact identity enables inference from the end qubit alone, even with arbitrary time-independent interactions elsewhere. Synthetic benchmarks near a boundary sweet spot certify persistence for up to about 60 times the measurement window. Three- and six-qubit IBM records strengthen conditional guarantees for the programmed coherent Hamiltonian. Applications to quantum-dot models relate tuning precision to persistence times and detuning-induced splitting.

### Paper [138]
Title: Two Unconventional Hall Effects in the Kagome Magnet TmMn4Cr$_2$Sn$_6$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16841
Authors: Mohamed El Gazzah, Fehmi Sami Yasin, Bishal Thapa, Sk Jamaluddin, Abhijeet Nayak, Resham Babu Regmi, Mary Kate Carroll, Igor I. Mazin, Nirmal J. Ghimire
Abstract: We identify two Hall anomalies in the kagome magnet TmMn$_4$Cr$_2$Sn$_6$. We propose that the high-field anomaly reflects a discontinuous spin reorientation and differing sublattice contributions to Hall transport and magnetization. At low fields, Hall hysteresis persists despite negligible magnetic hysteresis, suggesting a chirality-related contribution, although its microscopic origin remains unresolved. These contrasting regimes show how magnetic transitions can mimic unconventional Hall signals and highlight the limits of magnetization-based Hall decomposition.

### Paper [139]
Title: Low-temperature vibrational anomalies and thermal transport in polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbon crystals
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16848
Authors: V. Sokolenko, A. I. Krivchikov, A. Je\.zowski, V. Boiko, O. A. Korolyuk, D. Szewczyk
Abstract: The boson peak is an excess of low-frequency vibrational states over the Debye prediction and is commonly associated with structural disorder. Similar heat-capacity anomalies also occur in ordered molecular crystals, but their dependence on molecular structure and their relation to heat transport remain incompletely understood. Here we compare six polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbon (PAH) crystals -- pyrene, tetracene, perylene, pentacene, coronene and corannulene -- using low-temperature heat capacity, low-frequency Raman spectroscopy and thermal conductivity measurements. For compounds with well-resolved maxima, the rising side of the heat-capacity excess is consistent with a raised-cosine scaling function with no adjustable shape parameter, whereas the decay is compound-dependent. The number and widths of resolved lattice-mode Raman bands vary across the series, although their assignment is constrained by spectral background and resolution. Thermal conductivity ranges from crystal-like to glass-like behaviour. An activated contribution is resolved in the five planar compounds but is not required to describe the corannulene data within the sensitivity of the present measurements. These results show that glass-like thermal and vibrational signatures are not unique to amorphous materials and are associated with differences in molecular structure and crystal packing; they do not, by themselves, isolate packing as the sole causal variable.

### Paper [140]
Title: Interfacial Effects of Thermal Reorganization on Charge and Spin Transport in Organic Spin Valves
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16852
Authors: Zihao Liu, Yunzhe Ke, Hajar Komekoz, Shuai Gu, Rico Ehrler, Chen Wang, Olav Hellwig, Frank Ortmann, Dietrich R. T. Zahn, Carsten Deibel, Michael Sommer, Shuaishuai Ding, Georgeta Salvan
Abstract: Organic semiconductors are attractive for spintronic applications owing to their weak spin-orbit coupling, small hyperfine interactions, mechanical flexibility, and tunable electronic properties. Organic spin valves (OSVs) provide a representative platform for investigating spin-dependent transport. However, the magnetoresistance (MR) response is highly sensitive to the ferromagnetic electrode/organic semiconductor interface (spinterface). Variations in interfacial morphology, chemical interaction, energy-level alignment, and spin polarization can strongly affect spin injection, transport, and detection, making reproducible OSV performance difficult to achieve. Here, we investigate how in situ thermal annealing affects the electrical and magnetotransport properties of complete NDI-based OSVs. Two top-electrode configurations are compared: directly evaporated Co/Au and transferred Gr (graphene)/Co/Au. Both as-cast devices exhibit clear MR responses, with a stronger absolute response observed for the transferred-electrode device. After in situ annealing, the resistance of both device types increases substantially, and no reproducible MR response is resolved, despite the enhanced structural order observed in companion films and an approximately 36-fold increase in the effective vertical conductivity extracted from electron-only devices. This contrasting behavior indicates that improved bulk order alone is insufficient to ensure enhanced spin-valve performance. Instead, annealing-induced changes in interfacial contact, contact homogeneity, and local current distribution can become dominant in determining the complete-device response. These results emphasize the critical role of spinterface stability and interface engineering in achieving reliable and thermally robust OSV operation.

### Paper [141]
Title: Complex nematic dynamics driven by steady Kolmogorov flows
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16857
Authors: Rahil N Valani, Sumesh P Thampi, Julia M Yeomans
Abstract: Complex nematohydrodynamics, including irregular defect dynamics, is commonly associated with time-dependent flows, most notably in active turbulence. Here we consider a simple model of a planar nematic subjected to a temporally steady but spatially varying flow and investigate the onset of time-dependent dynamics. Subjecting a passive nematic to steady Kolmogorov flow, while eliminating velocity fluctuations and hydrodynamic feedback, we find that increasing the flow strength drives a transition from stationary to periodic and, at stronger forcing, irregular spatiotemporal dynamics. The time-dependent states involve repeated local suppression of nematic order and director reorganisation, arising from the competition between spatially varying shear and nematic relaxation. Bifurcation and phase-space analyses characterise this progression, while reduced-order models identify the dynamical origins. A two-mode model captures the onset of periodic motion through a saddle-node infinite-period bifurcation. A five-mode Galerkin truncation further recovers the progression from stationary to periodic and irregular dynamics. These results allow us to explain how a fixed, spatially heterogeneous velocity field can generate temporal complexity through the nonlinear dynamics of nematic order alone.

### Paper [142]
Title: A deterministic theory of kinetic fluctuations in long-range interacting homogeneous systems without collective effects
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16893
Authors: Anwar El Rhirhayi, Jean-Baptiste Fouvry, Julien Barr\'e
Abstract: Finite-$N$ fluctuations govern the slow relaxation of long-range interacting systems, yet accessing their full statistics demands huge ensembles of costly simulations. We show that, in homogeneous systems without collective effects, this statistical problem collapses onto just three deterministic kinetic equations. Starting from the Landau-Dean-Kawasaki equation, i.e. the stochastic counterpart of the large deviation principle for the Landau equation, we derive the infinite hierarchy of $n$-point moments of the empirical distribution function at leading order in $1/N$. We prove that it is solved exactly by a quasi-Gaussian closure. The resulting minimal Landau fluctuation hierarchy is formed by the Landau equation, a Lyapunov equation for the two-point moment, and a kinetic equation for the three-point moment. From these three equations, all higher-order moments follow algebraically. The hierarchy exactly preserves the collisional invariants and their fluctuations, and predicts the full probability distribution of observables in the central fluctuation regime. Benchmarked against $N$-body realizations, it reproduces the mean, variance and higher-order moments with an accuracy that improves systematically with $N$. A single deterministic time-integration of three coupled partial differential equations thus replaces large ensembles of simulations, for any large $N$.

### Paper [143]
Title: Squeezed terahertz light from nonlinear diamagnetism in ultrastrongly coupled two-dimensional electrons
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16924
Authors: Dasom Kim, Junichiro Kono, Stephen Hughes
Abstract: Two-dimensional electron gases in terahertz cavities are among the most mature platforms for ultrastrong light--matter coupling, yet their collective response is captured by the linear Hopfield model, which has an exact classical correspondence. We show that the intrinsic band nonparabolicity of the electrons, recently revealed through nonlinear diamagnetism, turns this system into a quantum Kerr oscillator that squeezes terahertz light. Using a non-rotating-wave theory with ultrastrong-coupling-consistent input--output, an ab initio cavity bath, and a self-consistent model of electron heating, we predict output squeezing of 1.8--2.4\,dB with present GaAs samples in weakly confined cavities with quality factors of order $10^2$, and up to $\approx9$\,dB with modest improvements in mobility or cavity frequency. Carrier heating sets limits through a material figure of merit, which yields a simple design rule that the optimal cavity quality factor scales inversely with the ultrastrong-coupling strength.

### Paper [144]
Title: Symmetry-preserving gapped Dirac state revealed by site-selective $^{13}$C NMR in $\alpha$-(BETS)$_2$I$_3$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16939
Authors: Takahiko Sekine, Kazuya Miyagawa, Koichi Hiraki, Reizo Kato, Kazushi Kanoda
Abstract: We report evidence that the low-temperature insulating phase of $\alpha$-(BETS)$_2$I$_3$ preserves both inversion and time-reversal symmetries using site-selective $^{13}$C nuclear magnetic resonance. $\alpha$-(BETS)$_2$I$_3$ undergoes a metal-insulator transition or sharp crossover at approximately 50 K and has been proposed as a candidate spin-orbit-coupling-induced Dirac insulator, yet the nature of its insulating ground state remains unresolved. The NMR spectra exhibit no additional splitting down to 2 K and remain fully described by one quartet and two doublets associated with the three types of molecular sites, A (=A$^{\prime}$), B and C sites, demonstrating the preservation of inversion symmetry. Furthermore, no anomalous line broadening or critical enhancement of the nuclear spin-lattice relaxation rate is observed, indicating the absence of magnetic ordering and time-reversal-symmetry breaking. Analysis of the Knight shifts yields site-specific spin susceptibilities satisfying $\chi_{\mathrm{C}} > \chi_{\mathrm{A}} > \chi_{\mathrm{B}}$, a characteristic feature of tilted Dirac-cone electronic states as observed in $\alpha$-(BEDT-TTF)$_2$I$_3$ under pressure. However, the faint field-induced ferrimagnetic-like spin excitations observed in $\alpha$-(BEDT-TTF)$_2$I$_3$, which are ascribed to the gapless tilted Dirac cones, are absent in $\alpha$-(BETS)$_2$I$_3$. These results demonstrate that the insulating ground state of $\alpha$-(BETS)$_2$I$_3$ is a symmetry-preserving gapped Dirac state and provide microscopic constraints on proposed correlated topological phases in this molecular Dirac system.

### Paper [145]
Title: Interfacial Structure and Dynamics of Active-Passive Fluid Mixtures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16973
Authors: Linnea Heitmeier
Abstract: We investigate the interface between an active and a passive fluid using molecular dynamics simulations of active Brownian particles in contact with a passive Lennard-Jones fluid. We compute the surface tension via two independent routes, pressure anisotropy and capillary-wave analysis, and find that they disagree systematically once activity is switched on. Static density and orientation profiles reveal three distinct activity regimes, with particles preferentially oriented toward the interface. Going beyond these static properties, we characterize the interface dynamics: a threshold activity above which mixing becomes irreversible, coinciding with the onset of the mixing regime, and a transient overshoot in interfacial width before relaxation to steady state.

### Paper [146]
Title: Cup and cap synchronization: higher-order node dynamics from topology
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16979
Authors: Runyue Wang, Yuanzhao Zhang, Ginestra Bianconi
Abstract: Higher-order node-based synchronization provides distinct mechanisms for discontinuous synchronization transitions. However, a comprehensive theoretical framework capable of encompassing existing models and revealing the role of higher-order interactions beyond triangles is still missing. Here, combining algebraic topology and nonlinear dynamics, we introduce Cup and Cap Synchronization, a unifying theoretical framework for higher-order node-based dynamics that naturally incorporates the topological organization of the underlying higher-order network. We show that Cup and Cap Synchronization can display critical behavior belonging to the universality classes of two existing higher-order Kuramoto-like models, whose distinct critical behaviors were thought to originate from different mechanisms. Remarkably, we demonstrate that Cup and Cap Synchronization displays additional phenomenology not captured by the previously proposed models of higher-order node-based synchronization. Most relevantly, the inclusion of four-body interactions can fundamentally alter the critical properties of the synchronization transition, giving rise to collective phenomena that cannot be observed in models including only interactions mediated by edges and triangles. These findings reveal the importance of going beyond the commonly studied three-body terms and establish Cup and Cap Synchronization as a unifying theoretical framework for understanding collective dynamics in higher-order networks.

### Paper [147]
Title: Unconstrained $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauge theory on a triangular lattice: deconfined fermions in a vison crystal and superfluidity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16988
Authors: Welberth Kennedy, Jo\~ao C. In\'acio, Fakher F. Assaad
Abstract: We investigate an unconstrained $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauge theory coupled to fermions on the triangular lattice at half filling, using auxiliary field quantum Monte Carlo simulations. The triangular geometry gives rise to a global $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry that acts as an Ising symmetry on the plaquette fluxes. At weak transverse field $h$, this symmetry is spontaneously broken, stabilizing a vison crystal. We argue that this phase is deconfined: fermions, charged under an emergent $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauge field, propagate in an ordered $\pi$-flux background and form a Dirac semimetal. Increasing the transverse field triggers a first-order quantum phase transition to a confined $s$-wave superfluid, at which confinement and global symmetry breaking occur simultaneously. This contrasts with the square lattice, where the corresponding transition is continuous. We extend our analysis to finite temperature and determine the thermal phase boundaries of both ordered phases. The superfluid melts through a Kosterlitz--Thouless transition, whereas the vison crystal undergoes a thermal Ising transition. Together, our results establish the $T \times h$ phase diagram and demonstrate how the triangular geometry leads to qualitative changes in both the phases and the criticality of the model.

### Paper [148]
Title: Achiral-Chiral Dissociation and Structural Zero of the Current in the Spinterface Model of CISS
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16998
Authors: Seif Alwan
Abstract: We study the transport sector of the spinterface model of chirality-induced spin selectivity (CISS) under spin-resolved dissipation, extending the quantum non-equilibrium Green's function formulation of Ref.~\cite{Alwan2026QSpinterface} to spin-resolved lead broadenings. The spin current decomposes into an achiral part, driven by the interfacial moment, and a chiral part, driven by the Rashba spin flip. Under increasing lead spin asymmetry, the achiral part grows linearly while the chiral part follows the geometric-mean suppression, so the two sectors dissociate; they also peak at different coupling scales along the lead broadening. The coupling and bias optima are operating points of the reference configuration, not universal scaling laws. A structural zero appears at $\lambda_R\sin(\phi/2)=t_{\mathrm{mol}}$ in the collinear-lead configuration, where the chiral signal collapses to machine precision universally across chain lengths, frequency resolutions, and twist angles; the zero originates from the rank-one singular bond matrix, whose null eigenvector traps an electron in a spin-superposition dark state that does not hop. With non-collinear ferromagnetic leads, the zero is broken in a controlled way: the residual is generically linear in the transverse polarization, arising from a single coherent spin flip at a boundary, and becomes quadratic at a tuned cancellation angle where two boundary contributions interfere destructively, with the scaling exponent jumping sharply from one to two. These results establish the spinterface model as a transport platform with a two-axis dissociation structure, non-scaling operating optima, and an exact structural zero whose non-collinear deformation is a clean linear-plus-quadratic hierarchy.

### Paper [149]
Title: Reflection Zeros Set the Scattering Invariant of Finite Majorana Wires
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17001
Authors: Alex Krasnok
Abstract: Majorana modes, zero-energy excitations of superconducting wires that are their own antiparticles, could store quantum information protected from local noise. In transport experiments, however, ordinary bound states mimic their signature, a conductance peak at zero voltage, and whether Majorana modes have been observed remains debated. A sign, $Q$, of the electron reflection at zero energy marks the topological phase of a long wire, but what $Q$ certifies in a finite device has been unclear. Here we show for the first time that $Q$ counts reflectionless states, waves that enter through one contact and leave entirely through the other: $Q=-1$ when an odd number of them lie in the lower half of the complex energy plane. In a finite wire this means that the two Majorana modes are split by less than their coupling to the contacts. With equal contacts, every sign change of $Q$, including the bulk topological transition, is an exceptional point at which two such states merge and transmission is maximally flat. At zero temperature, $Q=-1$ exactly when the zero-bias conductance measured at a contact exceeds $e^2/h$. These results show what transport can certify about a Majorana device and how to read the invariant from conductance.

### Paper [150]
Title: Sedimentation of flexible fibers through an ordered array of pillars
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17023
Authors: Ursy Makanga, Camille Duprat, Blaise Delmotte
Abstract: In this study, we extend our previous work [Makanga \textit{et al.}, Physical Review Fluids 8, 044303 (2023)] to investigate the effects of a periodic array of pillars on the settling dynamics of a flexible fiber in a viscous fluid. By means of numerical simulations, we show that the fiber trajectory falls at long times into one of two migration modes, ``zigzag" and ``displacement", which are determined by the short-time scattering dynamics induced by fiber-pillar interactions. In the zigzag mode, there is no average displacement relative to the direction of gravity; the center of mass of the fiber follows a quasi-straight line. Conversely, in the displacement mode, there is a net displacement relative to the direction of gravity; the fiber center of mass follows a cyclical skew-bumping path. We show how this long-time behavior can be predicted using a theoretical model with minimal ingredients. These findings, together with the long-time transport properties of the fiber, provide insights into future experiments, as well as the design of gravity-based deterministic lateral displacement (DLD) devices to sort elongated particles based on their geometric and/or mechanical properties.

### Paper [151]
Title: Theory of critical behavior at non-polar structural phase transitions from functional renormalization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17081
Authors: Max Fornoville, Kilian Fraboulet, Michael M. Scherer, Laura Classen
Abstract: $O(N)$ models provide a versatile framework for describing continuous phase transitions in a wide variety of physical systems. We investigate an extension of these models arising from order parameters that transform under interlocked internal and spatial symmetries which describe transitions towards non-polar structural order. Such order occurs, for instance, in dipolar antiferromagnets, spin-orbit-coupled magnets, non-polar ferroics, and certain nematic systems. The resulting effective theory is an $O(d)$ model with an additional symmetry-allowed kinetic term $h(\partial_i\phi_i)^2$, which modifies the relative stiffness of longitudinal and transverse fluctuations. We study both thermal and quantum phase transitions within this $O(d)$ model using the functional renormalization group (FRG). In contrast to conventional $O(N)$ models, the interlocking of internal and spatial symmetries distinguishes spatial and imaginary-time derivatives, such that the quantum model is not simply obtained from the thermal one by a dimensional extension $d\rightarrow d+1$. We derive and analyze the corresponding FRG flow equations, paying particular attention to the tensor structure of the two-point function, which is essential for consistently extracting the flow of the kinetic terms. Our analysis shows that the additional kinetic coupling is irrelevant at the $O(d)$ Wilson-Fisher fixed point in both thermal and quantum cases, where quantum-to-classical correspondence thus re-emerges. However, its small but sizable correction-to-scaling exponent gives rise to substantial corrections to scaling in the vicinity of the critical point.

### Paper [152]
Title: Time reparametrization across kinetic rules in a glass-forming liquid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17092
Authors: Leonardo dos Santos Vitoria
Abstract: Monte Carlo algorithms combining swap moves with kinetic constraints allow the relaxation pathways of a liquid to be modified without changing its equilibrium measure. Recent work suggests that much of this change can be absorbed into a time reparametrization, yielding a near-collapse of parametric relations between overlaps measured at different spatial scales. We examine this correspondence in a three-dimensional polydisperse hard-sphere liquid by comparing a planar $f_P$ rule that confines the translation of a fraction of particles to randomly oriented planes with a pure-$f_R$ rule that restricts swap participation. At each packing fraction, the two rules are tuned to comparable structural relaxation times, and time is eliminated explicitly. For $0.58 \leq \phi \leq 0.62$, we find a near-collapse accompanied by a small but resolved residual that persists when directly confined particles are excluded from the observable. A self--distinct decomposition shows that the statistics of individual displacements reproduce most of the scale of this residual. Among translationally free particles, the probability of large displacements decreases as the relative directions to constrained neighbors become more aligned with their forbidden normals, even when the number of constrained neighbors is held fixed. This association is consistent with zero in a ghost control, where the same normals impose no translational restriction. Our findings complement the time-reparametrization picture by showing that a near-collapse of collective parametric relations can coexist with rule-dependent displacement statistics and an orientational signature in the mobility of particles that are not directly constrained.

### Paper [153]
Title: High-pressure behavior of the low-frequency mode of water
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17096
Authors: Maria Rescigno, Guillaume Radtke, Umbertoluca Ranieri, Richard Gaal, Luigi Paolasini, Andrea Piovano, Ferdinando Formisano, Paola Giura, Livia Eleonora Bove
Abstract: The low-frequency (LF) mode observed in the THz spectrum of liquid water near 10 meV has been known for decades, yet its microscopic origin remains debated. Leading interpretations assign it to a transverse acoustic-like excitation, a transverse optic-like mode, or a mode-mode coupling mechanism. We address this question by probing the collective dynamics of H$_2$O and D$_2$O at room temperature and pressures from 0.1 to 1 GPa using high-resolution (1.6 meV) inelastic X-ray scattering (IXS). The LF mode remains heavily damped, close to critical damping, throughout the explored range, while its frequency hardens markedly with pressure, much more than expected for a transverse acoustic mode. Comparison with IXS measurements and \textit{ab initio} simulations of crystalline Ice VI reveals a cluster of weakly dispersive modes in the same energy range, suggesting that the low-frequency mode in liquid water reflects a mixed character arising from the superposition of several branches rather than a single well-defined excitation, providing a coherent microscopic picture of water's anomalous THz dynamics under compression.

### Paper [154]
Title: Acoustically driven motion of single molecules at hBN surfaces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17112
Authors: Hande N. A\c{c}{\i}kg\"oz, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Peter G. Steeneken, Sabina Caneva
Abstract: Developments in single-molecule imaging and sensing techniques have enabled direct observation of biomolecular dynamics at interfaces, which has important implications for the fields of membrane biophysics, nanofluidic sensing and transport. However, integrating active surface transport, localization and control over molecule motion remains challenging, limiting further progress in the realization of single-molecule devices. Here, we introduce an integrated surface acoustic wave (SAW)-hexagonal boron nitride (hBN) platform as a contactless actuation platform for directional transport of biomolecules over hBN surfaces. hBN provides an atomically flat surface on which DNA can be confined and still retain in-plane mobility, whereas SAW actuation enables active and guided transport without requiring specific electrical, dielectric or magnetic properties of the biomolecules. Trajectory- and jump-based analyses show that increasing the SAW power leads to higher displacement bias in the streaming direction, resulting in transport velocities along increasingly more linearized trajectories. We thus demonstrate that SAWs can induce tunable directional transport of DNA molecules confined on hBN surfaces, providing a new approach for the on-chip manipulation of biomolecules in nanofluidic systems. Our findings have important implications for the development of advanced single-molecule sequencing platforms and other technologies for biomolecular manipulation and analysis.

### Paper [155]
Title: Exact characterization of three-terminal fermion counting under one-body scattering
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17122
Authors: Zhanning Wang, Yongtao Li, Rub\'en Seoane Souto, Jos\'e C. Abadillo-Uriel
Abstract: Quantum scattering is a fundamental probe of the scattering region and the quantum dynamics of outgoing particles. In two terminals, the statistics of non-interacting fermions is described by a generalized binomial distribution, that arises from the Pauli exclusion principle, while deviations are a signature of interactions. In this work, we extend this result to a scattering region coupled to three outgoing terminals (or channels), obtaining exact bounds for the statistics of the outgoing fermions. In particular, for two fermions and three terminals, four inequalities involving six measured probabilities determine whether a non-interacting scatterer can explain the observed statistics. Applying these inequalities to recent scattering experiments in cold atoms, we are able to identify a regime where interactions have a significant contribution on the outgoing particles. Therefore, our work opens up the possibility of identifying fermion interactions at the nanoscale in systems that range from mesoscopic conductors to optical traps in cold atoms.

### Paper [156]
Title: A neural quantum state variational Monte Carlo study of Aharonov-Casher bands
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17144
Authors: Conor Smith, Nicol\'as Morales-Dur\'an, Jingtian Shi, Andrew J. Millis, Miguel A. Morales, Shiwei Zhang
Abstract: We use neural-network-based variational Monte Carlo to investigate the competition between fractional Chern insulators and generalized Wigner crystals in the Aharonov-Casher Hamiltonian, a continuum model for moir\'e materials. We introduce a variational wave function inspired by flux attachment that captures fractionalized phases and, by comparing its energy with that of a Slater--Jastrow ansatz that captures crystalline phases, we determine the phase diagram at fractional fillings in regimes of strong band mixing and for system sizes beyond the reach of other methods. We find that discrete translation symmetry, encoded in a nonuniform quantum geometry, affects fractionalized states at filling fractions $\nu=1/3$ and $\nu=2/3$ differently, and that band mixing can stabilize incommensurate crystalline ground states. Our results further reveal the delicate competition between topological and non-topological crystalline phases.

### Paper [157]
Title: Reentrant Nematicity from Landau Quantization near a Pomeranchuk Instability
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17150
Authors: Lu\'is E. Leite Mac\^edo, Daniel G. Barci
Abstract: Landau quantization can induce nematic order in a two-dimensional Fermi liquid that is isotropic at zero magnetic field. Using a mean-field theory near a quadrupolar Pomeranchuk instability, we show that varying the magnetic field produces alternating nematic and isotropic regions, with transitions that can be either continuous or first order. The resulting nematic state also leaves distinctive signatures in the de Haas--van Alphen oscillations. At fixed chemical potential, the field-dependent deformation of the Fermi surface can produce substantial changes in the oscillation frequency, even when the cyclotron mass varies only modestly. At fixed particle density, conservation of the Fermi-surface area preserves the leading oscillation frequency, while the cyclotron mass and harmonic amplitudes remain sensitive to nematic order. These contrasting behaviors provide experimentally relevant signatures of field-induced nematicity and reveal the importance of thermodynamic constraints in quantum-oscillation measurements.

### Paper [158]
Title: The de Haas--van Alphen effect in a two-dimensional nematic Fermi fluid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17166
Authors: Lu\'is E. Leite Mac\^edo, Daniel G. Barci
Abstract: Quantum oscillations are widely used to probe the Fermi surface of metals, but their behavior becomes more complex when electronic interactions can spontaneously deform that surface. We investigate this interplay in a two-dimensional model of interacting electrons capable of developing nematic order, using a uniform mean-field approximation in a perpendicular magnetic field. We show that Landau quantization can favor or suppress nematic order as the field varies, producing alternating isotropic and nematic regions near a Pomeranchuk instability, with both continuous and first-order transitions. Conversely, the evolving nematic order modifies the de Haas--van Alphen oscillations in ways that depend on the thermodynamic conditions. At fixed chemical potential, the oscillation frequency can change significantly even when the cyclotron mass varies only modestly. At fixed particle density, conservation of the Fermi-surface area preserves the leading Onsager frequency, while the cyclotron mass and harmonic amplitudes remain sensitive to nematic order. The magnetization and magnetic susceptibility provide additional signatures of the nematic transitions. These results show how quantum oscillations can both reveal and influence electronic order, offering a framework for interpreting experiments in strongly correlated metals.

### Paper [159]
Title: Lattice response theory for subsystem symmetry-protected topological phases
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17182
Authors: Yuki Furukawa, Takuya Okuda
Abstract: We develop a framework to study subsystem symmetry-protected topological (SSPT) phases using lattice response theory.
