We begin with a systematic construction of spacetime lattice gauge fields from general microscopic $X$-type symmetry actions on qudit systems, including global symmetries, subsystem symmetries, and more general modulated symmetries. Given a gauge-invariant response action, we then construct a corresponding entangler and explicitly show that it implements stacking with the SSPT phase described by the response action. We show that a broad class of SSPT phases can be described by intersection pairings and realized by cluster entanglers. We also study SSPT phases beyond intersection pairings, including those described by subsystem analogues of type-II group cocycles involving the Bockstein homomorphism. We construct a new 3+1d SSPT phase protected by two 2-foliated planar subsystem symmetries and a global 0-form symmetry, whose response action is a subsystem analogue of a type-III cocycle. On its boundary, the 0-form symmetry acts by stacking with a 2+1d linear SSPT phase, signaling a mixed 't Hooft anomaly.

### Paper [160]
Title: Optimizing Chern Bands for Moore-Read States: A Parent Hamiltonian Approach
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17188
Authors: Bishoy M. Kousa, Yung-Chun Chao, Jung-Jung Su, Allan H. MacDonald
Abstract: The recent realization of fractional quantum anomalous Hall states in moir\'e materials motivates efforts to stabilize non-Abelian topological order in the absence of a magnetic field. Here, we propose a framework for optimizing interactions to favor the non-Abelian Moore-Read state. Our approach compares band-projected three-particle spectra to that of the Moore-Read parent Hamiltonian, which is defined by a three-body interaction, and quantifies the comparison by defining a cost function that estimates proximity to the ideal Moore-Read limit. We apply our approach to Landau levels and to the Chern bands of twisted MoTe$_2$.

### Paper [161]
Title: Cubic, Non-Fermi Liquid Exchange Couplings for Doublet Impurities
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17192
Authors: Anna I. T\'oth, Davide Marenduzzo, Andrew D. Huxley
Abstract: We construct non-Fermi liquid (NFL) quantum impurity models for doublet impurities in a cubic metal, involving conduction electrons with half-integer total angular momentum, $J$. We highlight the only two crystal-symmetry-protected NFL quantum impurity models: the spin-half impurity, $J=3/2$ conduction electron Kondo model, and the two-channel Kondo model originally derived in the context of the quadrupolar Kondo model. We show explicitly that the quadrupolar Kondo model preserves time-reversal symmetry. We also construct two cubic quantum impurity models in which NFL behavior is not symmetry-protected. Each has $\Gamma_8$ and either $\Gamma_{6}$ or $\Gamma_{7}$ conduction electrons screening the impurity simultaneously, one model for a non-Kramers doublet and the other for a Kramers doublet impurity ground state. To our knowledge, the latter model has not been considered before; we expect it to realize a new NFL fixed point, but its solution is left for future work. Finally, we derive the relevant cubic perturbation that drives the non-symmetry-protected systems away from their NFL scaling regimes.

### Paper [162]
Title: Integer and Fractional Chern Plateaus Shaped by Layer-Dependent Disorder
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17217
Authors: Ziyue Qi, Zhan Wang, Hongming Weng, Kun Jiang
Abstract: The realization of integer and fractional Chern insulators in moir\'e systems provides a lattice setting for exploring how the structure of Bloch wave functions influences the interplay between topology and disorder. Here we investigate how layer-dependent disorder shapes Hall plateau widths and transitions. Using a realistic three-orbital model of twisted bilayer MoTe$_2$, we compare Hall and bulk transport under onsite-potential, displacement-field, and twist-angle disorder. At equal bare disorder strength, onsite-potential disorder produces broad integer plateaus with nearly direct transitions, whereas displacement-field disorder yields narrow plateaus separated by conducting regions with pronounced Hall-resistance dips. These contrasting behaviors arise from the layer composition of the Bloch states: contributions from different layer under displacement-field disorder partially cancel, weakening the bulk localization. Composite-fermion mean-field theory further yields fractional plateaus at $\nu_h=2/3$ and $3/5$ with the same disorder dependence of plateau widths. These findings identify layer-dependent disorder as an important mechanism shaping Chern plateau stability in moir\'e systems beyond bare disorder strength alone.

### Paper [163]
Title: Circular phonons generate negligible magnetization in insulators
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17222
Authors: C-C. Wu, M. Buzzi, A. Iudica, R. Romero III, G. De Vecchi, R. Merlin, A. Cavalleri
Abstract: The optical excitation of circular phonons has opened up non-equilibrium phenomena with no equilibrium counterparts, ranging from the generation of synthetic magnetic fields to the control of topological order or the manipulation of ferroaxial states. In this area, significant controversy has arisen on whether sizeable "true" magnetizations accompany circular phonons. Several confounding factors cloud the interpretation of the experiments, which are based on probing polarization rotation in the driven material. These range from the interpretation of the Faraday effect in non-equilibrium conditions to spurious nonlinear optical contributions in pump probe experiments. Here we resolve these ambiguities by measuring both polarization rotation inside the driven material and the stray magnetic field generated outside it with a proximate Faraday detector. For a 5 MV/cm mid-infrared drive, we observe a colossal polarization rotation inside a driven SiC crystal, similar to what was reported in previous work. Depending on the assumptions made, this could correspond to magnetizations as high as $\mu_0 M = 0.3 T$ or as low as 4 $\mu T$. However, when measuring the magnetic stray field outside the driven sample we unambiguously determine that the magnetization in SiC is negligibly small ($\mu_0 M \lt 0.001 T$).

### Paper [164]
Title: Inter-Nanoparticle Repulsion Effect on Self-Organization in Polymer Nanocomposites: A Langevin Dynamics Simulation Study
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17254
Authors: Paawan Chandrakanth, Nikos Ch. Karayiannis, Subhalaxmi Das, Supriya Roy
Abstract: Demixing through liquid-liquid phase separation is relevant to nanoparticle (NP) aggregation in polymer nanocomposites (PNCs), biomolecular compartmentalization, and liquids embedded in elastic networks. Mixing-demixing phase behavior depends on factors, such as particle size distribution, system dimen-sionality, intermolecular interactions, and relative concentrations. Here, using Langevin Dynamics simu-lations, we systematically investigate the effect of inter-nanoparticle repulsion on the self-assembly of PNCs under extreme confinement. A nanoparticle assembly, in the form of a compact droplet, is immersed in a polymer matrix of semiflexible, rod-like chains. By varying the nanoparticle-nanoparticle repulsion strength, while fixing all other interactions, we identify four distinct regimes with respect to the self-assembled NP morphologies: droplet, slab, crystalline droplet, and dispersed. Cluster-based analysis reveals a progressive loss of nanoparticle droplet integrity, leading to fragmentation within the dispersed regime. Local order of the nanoparticle assembly and global order of polymer chains are gauged through refined descriptors, quantifying the degrees of crystallinity and of nematic order, respectively. Interest-ingly, the slab regime exhibits negligible nanoparticle crystallization yet demonstrates the strongest polymer nematic ordering. In contrast, the crystalline droplet displays extensive triangular close-packed crystallites accompanied by a significant reduction in polymer nematic order, owing to its rhomboid shape. In the dispersed regime, nanoparticle fragmentation facilitates efficient mixing and local smectic-like ordering of the polymer chains. These findings demonstrate that fine-tuning inter-NP repulsions can control self-organization and the resulting morphologies, providing insights into PNCs with tailored functionality.

### Paper [165]
Title: Interactions from memory footprints: Cage-hopping, mnemotaxis & nonreciprocity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17257
Authors: Jyoti Prasad Banerjee, Sambodh Sinha, Shashi Thutupalli, Rituparno Mandal, Deb Sankar Banerjee
Abstract: Cellular motility is integral to biological processes ranging from foraging to immune responses and cancer metastasis. While cells often navigate by sensing pre-existing environmental cues, many actively steer their future trajectories by remodeling their surroundings via shared, long-lived physicochemical trails called memory footprints. These stigmergic responses to shared footprints also influence large-scale self-organization, such as the early stages of microcolony formation. Despite recent progress, a unified framework connecting single-cell dynamics and collective organization through effective pairwise interactions remains elusive. To bridge this gap, we perform extensive agent-based simulations of an interacting random walk model that explicitly accounts for footprint deposition and decay. Our approach reveals distinct cage-hopping dynamics in solitary cells, as well as complex mnemotactic responses to externally imposed memory gradients. Furthermore, we demonstrate that shared spatial memory fields generate nontrivial effective pairwise interactions, both reciprocal and nonreciprocal, that shape the collective organization of cell populations. Beyond multiscale cellular dynamics, our findings have broader implications for the collective behavior of synthetic active matter, such as swarm robots.

### Paper [166]
Title: SiC-BN composites with extreme thermal and environmental stability
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17283
Authors: Abhijit Biswas, Eugenio Garcia-Granados, Saman Zare, Mozhdeh Mirakhory, JungHun Park, Shuvodeep De, Jessica Reyes, Hunter B. Schonfeld, Noah A. Burke, William Riffe, Michael S. Lafferty, Sudaice Kazibwe, Maryam Amiri, Shreyasi Chattopadhyay, Mingrui Xu, Gelu Costin, Tanguy Terlier, Jianhua Li, Ching-Wu Chu, Liangzi Deng, Bharat K. Jasthi, Manoj Tripathi, Venkataramana Gadhamshetty, Gi-Dong Sim, Kathy Lu, Adri C. T. van Duin, Patrick E. Hopkins, Sanjay Sampath, Pulickel M. Ajayan
Abstract: Ceramic materials that maintain structural integrity and resist thermal-shock under extreme conditions are critical for many applications. Here we show that spark plasma sintered composites containing equal weight fractions of hexagonal silicon carbide (6H-SiC) and boron nitride (h-BN) exhibit exceptional stability under extreme high-temperature oxidizing conditions and unusually stable temperature-independent thermal conductivity across a broad temperature range. Under high-velocity oxygen-fuel torch exposure in air, it withstands prolonged heating and thermal-cycling at surface temperatures reaching >1500 {\deg}C, whereas pure 6H-SiC and h-BN rapidly fractures or erodes. Microstructural evaluation shows the formation of a multi-phase surface silica layer that further protects the material from oxidation. Finite-element analysis heat-flux map shows that intrinsic porosity mitigates thermal stress, while ReaxFF molecular dynamics simulations reveal thermally driven interfacial reconstruction and the formation of a Si-N/C-B-rich bonding network, enhancing the interfacial cohesion at elevated temperatures. This lightweight, easily machinable ceramic composite will be a promising structural material in extreme environment applications.

### Paper [167]
Title: Statistics of Fock-space correlation lengths in the many-body localised phase
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17306
Authors: Bibek Saha, David E. Logan, Sthitadhi Roy
Abstract: The presence of long-ranged, many-body resonances in the many-body localised phase of disordered quantum systems is manifest in strong inhomogeneities of eigenstate amplitudes on the Fock space. We develop a quantitative, microscopic theory for this, centred on two-point Fock-space correlation functions. Exact diagonalisation of the disordered tilted-field Ising chain uncovers a stark contrast: typical correlations decay perfectly exponentially, whereas average correlations exhibit subexponential decay with systematic system-size dependence driven by rare resonant nodes. We show that this behaviour stems from an underlying distribution of Fock-space correlation lengths with a variance that scales inversely with system size. Crucially, the interplay of this distribution with the high-dimensional geometry of the hypercubic Fock-space graph, leads to the qualitative difference between the typical and average behaviours. The theory also allows us to derive the singularity spectrum of the many-body localised eigenstates, and to establish theoretically the anomalous symmetry violation of quantum multifractality as an inherent consequence of the Fock-space geometry.

### Paper [168]
Title: Discrete Time Crystals from Anti-Unitary Floquet Driving and Dynamical Freezing
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17333
Authors: Anirban Das, Asmi Haldar, Arnab Das
Abstract: We show anti-unitary periodic drive paves a direct path for stabilizing a Discrete Time-Crystal by Dynamical Freezing (DF). The magnetization appears as an emergent conserved operator, and an exact pairing of two-cycle quasi-energies pins the subharmonic response. DF keeps the effective Hamiltonian $\textit{quasi-local}$; infinite-chain simulations show no detectable decay for a generic state with any finite magnetization. We outline an experimental realization of the anti-unitary drive.

### Paper [169]
Title: Long-lived light-driven bimagnons at finite momentum in NiO
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17335
Authors: Xian P. Yang, Christopher T. Parzyck, Martin Cross, Ella Blake, Octave Duros, Yinchuan Lv, Hari Paudyal, Che-Min Lin, Sharon S. Philip, Lingjia Shen, Daniel Jost, Giacomo Coslovich, Joshua J. Turner, Choongwon Seo, Elyse Schriber, Michael E. Flatt\'e, Xiaoqin Li, Yuri Suzuki, Durga Paudyal, Harold Y. Hwang, Georgi L. Dakovski, Matthias C. Hoffmann, Wei-Sheng Lee, Alexander H. Reid
Abstract: Light can manipulate magnetic order and its excitations on ultrafast timescales, including through resonantly driven phonons. In antiferromagnets, light can also create correlated magnon pairs, or bimagnons. Unlike single magnons, whose damping has been measured and modeled in detail, little is known about the lifetime, interactions and decay of such correlated pairs: prior two-magnon measurements show broad linewidths or coherences that dephase within 200 fs. Here we pump the phonon-assisted two-magnon absorption of NiO with mid-infrared pulses, far below its bandgap, and probe the resulting magnetic dynamics at finite momentum with time-resolved resonant inelastic X-ray scattering (RIXS). Upon photoexcitation, the one-magnon linewidth and energy are essentially unchanged, indicating that the long-range magnetic order is preserved without the photocarrier-induced demagnetization characteristic of above-bandgap excitation. A pump-induced two-magnon signal appears on the energy-gain (anti-Stokes) side of the spectrum. Its spectral weight peaks 4 ps after the pump, well beyond our 0.5 ps time resolution, indicative of a delayed growth likely due to momentum redistribution of the light-induced magnon pairs across the Brillouin zone. This two-magnon signal decays with a time constant of $28\pm11$ ps, exceeding the previously reported two-magnon dephasing time by more than two orders of magnitude. These results establish long-lived light-driven bimagnon correlations beyond the one-magnon state, akin to the higher-order correlations measured in ultracold gases.

### Paper [170]
Title: Fast Accurate Quantum Monte Carlo without Metropolis Adjustment
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10187
Authors: Reuben Cohn-Gordon, Gabriel Pescia, Sumner N. Hearth, Jakob Robnik, Uro\v{s} Seljak, Peter Lunts
Abstract: In quantum systems, physical quantities of interest can be expressed as expectation values of a probability distribution, which allows their estimation by Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) methods. This approach, an instance of Quantum Monte Carlo (QMC), plays a central role in obtaining predictions of system properties relevant to condensed matter and high energy physics. The use of Hamiltonian dynamics to design a Markov kernel, known as Hybrid or Hamiltonian Monte Carlo (HMC), is widespread in quantum applications. In this context, it is standard to use an HMC kernel that is ``adjusted'' with the Metropolis-Hastings (MH) criterion, so that estimates of expectations converge to their true value in the limit of infinite sample size. However, recent work in computational statistics suggests that an \emph{unadjusted} HMC kernel can be significantly more efficient, while safely maintaining an asymptotic bias which is small relative to the error arising from the variance of the finite sample size. We adapt this approach to the QMC setting, focusing on a challenging and high-dimensional \emph{spin-fermion} model of a quantum phase transition. We find that unadjusted methods outperform adjusted HMC in terms of effective sample size per gradient call, while retaining accurate results.

### Paper [171]
Title: Universal Critical Relations and Nonuniversal Scaling in Inverse-Square Ising Chains
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11546
Authors: Dyuman Bhattacharya
Abstract: For ferromagnetic Ising chains with $n^2J_n\to B>0$, the critical excess magnetization in a field inversely proportional to spin separation determines the connected correlation. Their slowly varying profile has finite logarithmic integral exactly when the ordered thermal slope is finite. For any finite nonnegative core, an exact tail gives inverse-logarithmic correlations and a square-root cusp. A relative correction $c/\log(n+e)$ with $c>1$ gives continuously varying logarithmic powers and a linear cusp. A lower bound on its disordered susceptibility excludes the usual Kosterlitz--Thouless law.

### Paper [172]
Title: Hands-on fabrication of 2D material field-effect transistors for the classroom using off-the-shelf ionic gel
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14565
Authors: Yigit Sozen, Tayfun Kutlu, Juan J. Riquelme, Andres Castellanos-Gomez
Abstract: We introduce a hands-on experimental method that enables students to fabricate and operate van der Waals semiconductor-based field-effect transistors (FETs) using readily available materials and minimal infrastructure. The approach is based on ionic-gel gating using commercially available hydrogel conductors originally developed for muscle electrostimulation devices. For device fabrication we rely on commercially available thin-film interdigitated electrode arrays and for the semiconductor channel, we deposited MoS2 flakes produced by high-throughput mechanical exfoliation. By avoiding the need for cleanroom processing, vacuum deposition, or custom electrolytes, this method allows functional FETs based on van der Waals materials to be realized in a standard teaching laboratory. The experiment is well suited for undergraduate and early graduate courses and is intended to complement concepts introduced in electronics and solid-state physics by providing students with first-hand experience in the fabrication and electrical characterization of emerging electronic devices. Representative electrical measurements demonstrate gate-dependent modulation of the channel conductance and illustrate characteristic features of ionic-gated transistor operation. Overall, the proposed platform provides an accessible route for students to fabricate, characterize, and analyze field-effect devices without access to conventional semiconductor foundry.

### Paper [173]
Title: Rotor-driven piecewise cross polarization of wide quadrupolar lines: bath-contact pumping of vector and tensor order in dynamically polarized solids
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14575
Authors: D. Keller, M. F. Hossain
Abstract: In a dynamically polarized solid with a common nuclear spin temperature, spin-1 $^{14}$N in ammonia sits at $P_N\simeq0.14$ when the protons are at $0.90$, and a deuteron in a proton-rich matrix at $P_D\simeq0.29$. Hartmann--Hahn cross polarization (CP) from the protons could raise either toward $P_H$, but the first-order quadrupole interaction spreads the $^{14}$N line over $5$~MHz at $5$~T, far beyond any single contact bandwidth. We develop the theory of CP into one transition of a quadrupolar spin-1, with slow sample rotation carrying each crystallite through a fixed-frequency contact window. A bath contact is an idempotent map on the populations of the contacted transition, so repeated or continuous contacts accumulate rather than cancel, and the hit-once and parity constraints that force stepped rotation for pulsed inversions do not arise. In the small-reservoir limit the population ratio across the contacted transition becomes $(1+P_H)/(1-P_H)$, giving $\Pzz=+0.88$ in one contact and $\Pzz\to-1.8$ in two from $P_H=0.9$, beyond the unitary bound $[-0.85,+0.65]$. Under continuous rotation, a window at the Pake horn reaches every bond for an axis perpendicular to the field, while a window in the clean pedestal region, needed for a definite sign of tensor order, reaches a fraction $\sin2\theta_c=0.943$ at the magic angle $54.74\degree$. Steady-state order is set by the contact rate against rotation-driven cross-branch relaxation, negligible for $^{14}$N. In the cubic limit one contact places the spin-1 at the proton temperature ($\Pzz=+0.85$), but only a resolved quadrupole splitting permits negative tensor order. The limiting resource is the proton spin lock, which must far exceed the $\simeq28$~kHz local field for a contact short compared with $T_{1\rho}$, unmeasured at $1$~K. Protocols are given for $^{14}$N in NH$_3$ and deuterons in NH$_2$D.

### Paper [174]
Title: The bias interval of the elephant random walk
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14576
Authors: Arjun Pemmasani
Abstract: Gu\'erin, Laulin, Raschel, and Simon conjectured that the initial biases giving the superdiffusive elephant random walk a bimodal limit law form an interval centered at one half, and they described the problem as challenging. We prove this. For every superdiffusive memory, strongly biased starts are unimodal, and the limit density has two local maxima exactly when the bias lies in a proper open interval. Outside it, including its endpoints, the density has a unique local maximum. The interval is nonempty exactly above a single memory threshold, numerically about $0.6688$, whose uniqueness follows from a positive-weight integral for the central curvature of the fair-start density. We control sign changes in the finite walk's first and second differences, including those created at the boundary, and pass them to the limit. The bias boundary is continuous and tends to zero at least quadratically fast as memory tends to one.

### Paper [175]
Title: Probing Krylov Dynamics in Driven Many-Body Systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14611
Authors: Andr\'as Grabarits, Adolfo del Campo
Abstract: Krylov complexity has become a central probe of operator growth, scrambling, and quantum chaos. Yet, a basic fact hinders its experimental study: laboratory quantum systems are routinely driven in time, whereas the Krylov construction assumes a fixed generator and breaks down once the Hamiltonian varies. Here, we solve the time-dependent many-body Krylov problem exactly and in closed form for scale- and rotation-invariant systems realized in current quantum-simulation and control platforms. We find exact relations that map the driven dynamics onto mutually orthogonal Krylov subspaces and yield a closed-form spread complexity, determined solely by the spectral properties of the initial Hamiltonian and the driving schedule. The construction extends to Liouville space and solves the time-dependent density-matrix Krylov problem, which makes the Krylov dynamics of initial thermal states experimentally accessible. In scale-invariant quantum gases, such as the driven Lieb-Liniger model, the Tonks-Girardeau gas, and the Calogero-Sutherland model, the complexity is set by the width of the atomic cloud and its rate of change, directly accessible via in situ and time-of-flight imaging. In rotation-invariant spin systems such as the Haldane-Shastry and central-spin models, it is governed by collective spin precession that can be probed and read out in trapped-ion, cavity-QED, and circuit-QED platforms.

### Paper [176]
Title: Squeezed Light Generation by Cooper Pair Recombination in a Superconductor-Semiconductor Structure
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14622
Authors: Sima Buchbinder, Avi Koriat, Alex Hayat
Abstract: We theoretically demonstrate squeezed light generation in a compact, electrically driven device utilizing the nonlinear light-matter interaction of Cooper-pair recombination in a hybrid superconductorsemiconductor structure. On the basis of second-order perturbation theory, we developed an effective Hamiltonian which incorporates the electronic component of the interaction as a photon coupling strength coefficient, enabling a model to describe the quadrature squeezing effect of Cooper-pair recombination on the photonic state. We find the squeezing strength to be proportional to the superconducting energy gap squared and device length, as well as increasing with applied voltage. For practical device length, materials and voltage bias, we calculated squeezing values comparable to those of existing squeezed light generation methods when operating below the critical temperature. This theoretically developed concept shows viability for electrically pumped squeezed light generation and can pave the way towards realization of squeezing in compact integrated structures.

### Paper [177]
Title: Quantum synchronization in XX chains under relaxation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14657
Authors: K\"ubra S\"umer, Ege Yi\u{g}it Ald{\i}rmaz, Steve Campbell, Bar{\i}\c{s} \c{C}akmak, G\"oktu\u{g} Karpat
Abstract: We study stable synchronization in qubit XX chains with site-dependent frequencies and exchange couplings under multi-local amplitude damping noise, extending the uniform-chain results of B. \c{C}akmak et al., [Phys. Rev. A 114, L030202 2026] in several directions. We establish that the dark-mode energies are precisely the eigenvalues shared by all undamped segments separated by the damped sites. Generic stable synchronization of the edge qubits occurs if and only if these segments share exactly one eigenvalue and this eigenvalue is nonzero, in which case every qubit on the support of the corresponding dark mode is synchronized with locking constants independent of the initial preparation. This spectral matching condition identifies the Hamiltonian parameters that support synchronization, while the couplings to the damped sites control the relative oscillation amplitudes. We present analytical examples which show how inhomogeneity can create synchronization absent in uniform chains and turn preparation-dependent locking into generic synchronization. We also calculate the asymptotic pairwise concurrence and prove that, in the single-dark-mode regime, every pair participating in synchronized oscillations remains entangled with a nonzero, constant concurrence.

### Paper [178]
Title: Emergent Anyons in a Weakly Interacting Gauged Chern Insulator
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14658
Authors: Sven Bachmann, Leonardo Goller, Marcello Porta
Abstract: We derive a microscopic relation between the many-body Chern number $C$ of the fermionic ground state and the braiding of fluxes in $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ gauge theory coupled to matter: under a spectral gap condition, dressed flux loops obey $W_2W_1=e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}C}W_1W_2$ on the ground-state sector. We realize $C=1$ by gauging charge parity in a weakly interacting Qi-Wu-Zhang insulator. Reflection positivity and chessboard estimates show that flux defects are massive, which yields a gapped, topologically ordered phase with four locally indistinguishable ground states.

### Paper [179]
Title: SunLightBots: Turning sunlight into self-sustained, high-power motion in soft robots
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14729
Authors: Yoo Jin Lee, Melanie Torres Crespo, Zhongxiao Deng, Andrew Tejada-Vega, Rebecca Kramer-Bottiglio, Wenzhong Yan, Yimin Luo
Abstract: Soft robots built from active materials can directly convert environmental stimuli into motion, offering a route to energy autonomy and embodied control. However, environmental energy is generally harvested at low power, whereas rapid, mechanically demanding actions require high mechanical output power. Reconciling this mismatch while sustaining self-regulated cyclic actuation is challenging, particularly at the subgram scale, where limited payload capacity constrains the integration of conventional power and control hardware. Here, we present SunLightBots, a class of subgram, sunlight-powered, untethered soft robots that achieve autonomous locomotion under continuous illumination near 1 Sun (100 mW/cm2). Their operation is enabled by a self-resetting snapping (SRS) mechanism that integrates liquid crystal elastomers (LCEs) with snap-through instability to accumulate solar energy supplied at low power, release it as rapid, high-power motion, and reset through photothermal-mechanical feedback. Through coordinated optimization of the LCE actuation temperature, photothermal conversion, actuation stress, and structural energy barrier, the system operates near 1 Sun and achieves a peak mass-specific mechanical power of 89 W/kg. At this irradiance, the optimized LCE generates an actuation stress of 0.1 MPa at approximately 60 deg C. SunLightBots demonstrate sustained swimming, crawling, and repeated impulsive jumping. Under 1.5 Suns, the swimmer advances more than three body lengths per cycle. This work provides a materials-mechanics route toward autonomous soft robots powered entirely by the environment.

### Paper [180]
Title: Correlation-Consistent Gaussian Basis Sets for Periodic Solids. I. Hydrogen and p-block Elements from Boron to Chlorine
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14768
Authors: Jincheng Yu, Hong-Zhou Ye
Abstract: We present the cc-pVXZ-s series (X = D, T, and Q) of correlation-consistent Gaussian basis sets for solids, covering hydrogen and the p-block elements from boron to chlorine. The series includes basis sets of consistent quality for the all-electron potential and for various pseudopotentials and effective core potentials commonly used in periodic calculations. Our design combines Dunning's construction of the molecular cc-pVXZ series with a revised material-constrained atomic optimization (MCAO) algorithm for improved numerical performance. MCAO regularizes standard atomic optimization to suppress overly diffuse basis functions and ensure numerical stability in a set of reference solids. Benchmarks against standard cc-pVXZ calculations for molecules and plane-wave calculations for periodic solids establish the accuracy, transferability, and numerical stability of cc-pVXZ-s in both mean-field and correlated wavefunction calculations and confirm reliable extrapolation to the complete-basis-set (CBS) limit. As an application, we use all-electron cc-pVXZ-s to compute basis-set-converged lattice constants, bulk moduli, and cohesive energies for 7 bulk semiconductors. Using a hierarchy of correlated wavefunction methods up to local coupled cluster with singles, doubles, and perturbative triples [CCSD(T)], we find systematic convergence toward experimental bulk moduli and cohesive energies, while a small systematic overestimation of lattice constants suggests potential limitations in the approximate finite-size treatment. This work provides a foundation for the further development of systematically convergent Gaussian basis sets for periodic calculations.

### Paper [181]
Title: Bayesian inference with a polymer probabilistic computer
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14782
Authors: Travis Wanless, Yuriy Bandera, Lubomir Kostal, Stephen H. Foulger
Abstract: Bayesian inference weighs competing explanations of noisy data by sampling from a posterior distribution, a task that deterministic hardware performs only by simulating randomness. Probabilistic bits (p-bits) supply this randomness physically, but the few hardware networks of p-bits built so far use inorganic devices, address optimization or learning, and are judged without reference to the spread expected from finite sampling. Here we report a probabilistic computer built from solution-processed conjugated-polymer memristors. Four polymer p-bits, tracked online for drift and coupled in a closed Gibbs-sampling loop, sample competing, degenerate and cooperative posteriors over sixteen states with statistics that fall within the range of ideal p-bits, both in their distribution and in their autocorrelation, without any parameter fitted to the target. This agreement extends to new simulated sensor readings whose outcome was predicted before each run, including readings that point away from the true answer. Reprogrammed in software, the same circuit and controller reproduces the invertible AND gate of earlier p-bit networks, with its temperature fixed in advance rather than fitted. Polymer p-bits thus open a route to printed, large-area probabilistic computers that infer alongside their sensors.

### Paper [182]
Title: Randomly growing uniform hypergraphs
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14824
Authors: P. L. Krapivsky
Abstract: We investigate growing uniform random hypergraphs whose hyperedges are $(k-1)$-simplices. At each step, one of two processes occurs: (i) a new vertex is added with probability $p$; (ii) a simplex $\langle v_1,\ldots,v_k\rangle$ with randomly chosen distinct vertices is added with probability $1-p$. Emerging random uniform hypergraphs exhibit a percolation transition. A giant component is formed when $p<p_c=1-(2k-1)^{-2}$. The fraction of vertices in the giant component scales as $\exp[-C/\sqrt{p_c-p}]$, a signature of the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless phase transition. In the non-percolating phase, the cluster size distribution has an algebraic tail with a continuously varying exponent, while at $p=p_c$, it acquires a logarithmic correction. We analyze triadic hypergraphs ($k=3$) in detail and then generalize to higher $k$. We show that the Euler characteristic and the first Betti number of the giant component scale linearly with system size. Higher Betti numbers fluctuate between realizations but remain finite, and hence the normalized Euler characteristic and the first Betti number of the giant component are asymptotically equal, up to a sign.

### Paper [183]
Title: Spontaneous mechanical patterning in active elastic tissues with stress feedback
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14943
Authors: Henrik Weyer, Divyoj Singh, M. Cristina Marchetti
Abstract: The active mechanics of biological tissues is often governed by embedded active contractile fibers responding to mechanical feedback. We introduce a minimal model of a solid-like tissue where the mechanics is controlled by feedback of local active and elastic stresses on fiber organization. The resulting tissue generically exhibits a bistable response to external strain and spontaneously segregates into domains of orthogonally aligned fibers. Our theory thus provides a minimal mechanical feedback mechanism generating orthogonal structural alignment ubiquitous in biological tissues.

### Paper [184]
Title: Distilling an atomistic foundation model is all you need for large-scale molecular dynamics of semiconductor interfaces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14948
Authors: Feiyu Zhou, Wei Qiu, Fei Shuang, Yilun Liu, Shengying Yue, Penghua Ying
Abstract: State-of-the-art atomistic foundation models provide broadly transferable energy and force predictions, but their direct deployment in large-scale molecular dynamics remains computationally costly. Here we examine whether their knowledge can be distilled into compact potentials while retaining the physical responses of heterogeneous semiconductor interfaces. From 128 pretrained models spanning 22 model families, we select a fixed EquiformerV3 teacher and train atomic cluster expansion (ACE) and neuroevolution potential (NEP) students without target-specific teacher fine-tuning, with both simulation speed and accessible system size improved by several orders of magnitude relative to the teacher model. Matched training configurations and architectures enable direct comparisons with students trained on density-functional-theory (DFT) reference labels. For 79,920-atom BAs/GaN interfaces, direct distillation preserves termination-dependent thermal conductance and dominant spectral features, with conductance differing by at most 6.6% from DFT-trained counterparts. In 29,792-atom Cu/diamond specimens loaded at 500 K, tensile strength and loading work differ by 2.8% and 1.5%, respectively. Exploratory SiC/a-C/SiC simulations retain the principal thermal bottleneck and the direction of the annealing response. Furthermore, a four-model committee retrieves 42-56% of the highest-error teacher configurations with a 10% selection budget and improves high-error force fitting, but does not consistently improve downstream properties. These findings demonstrate that direct distillation can preserve key interfacial thermal and mechanical responses in compact interatomic potentials, opening a path towards large-scale, high-throughput simulations of heterogeneous interfaces.

### Paper [185]
Title: Feedback-induced mechanical bistability and perpendicular-domain formation in active elastic tissues
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14964
Authors: Divyoj Singh, Henrik Weyer, Harry Kroft, M. Cristina Marchetti
Abstract: Contractile fibers in muscle tissues are highly aligned over the scale of many cells. Muscle layers surrounding organs with an inner lumen are often arranged in orthogonal directions. Here, we study a minimal mechanism of how structural order and its patterning into orthogonally aligned domains can emerge from mechanical feedback alone. The central idea is that contractile fibers align in the direction of the local active and elastic stresses, a feedback motivated by the maturation of muscle fibers under tension. We thus model muscle tissue as an active nematic solid with stress feedback: an elastic medium with embedded contractile fibers that are reinforced along the directions of the local tissue stresses. We find that the feedback from the contractile fiber stress is self-amplifying and drives spontaneous fiber alignment. Feedback from the elastic stress couples the alignment to the mechanical boundary conditions and tissue geometry. Depending on the mechanical boundary conditions, the elastic-stress feedback stabilizes two orthogonally aligned tissue states, resulting in a structurally and mechanically bistable tissue that shows a hysteretic stress-strain response. In spatially extended tissues, this bistability gives rise to orthogonally aligned domains separated by domain walls. In an annulus surrounding a pressurized lumen, a radially aligned inner domain and a circumferentially aligned outer domain stably coexist because the domain wall is pinned at a position that results in the fibers carrying the lumen pressure. Our results show that stress feedback can pattern structural order by coupling spontaneous alignment to the tissue geometry. Our mechanism thus provides a purely mechanical route to orthogonal fiber patterning in muscle tissues.

### Paper [186]
Title: Local enhancement of finite-strength code preparation: recoverability and Wilson coherence
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14967
Authors: Tong Xu, Yuanchen Zhao, Dong E. Liu
Abstract: Stabilizer measurements provide a direct route to preparing quantum error-correcting codes and long-range entangled states. In realistic implementations, however, finite measurement strength can coherently deform the encoding map away from the projective limit. Certain nonlocal signatures associated with long-range order can survive such a deformation, but this alone does not guarantee that the resulting state protects encoded quantum information. We show that, at fixed strength, local logical leakage obstructs asymptotically faithful recovery; along logical operators' supports, its accumulation forces the relevant measurement strength to grow logarithmically with code distance. We then prove that this obstruction can be removed locally: for fixed toric registers under sufficiently weak local-stochastic errors, enhancing only a vanishing fraction of checks near selected logical supports restores recovery while the bulk remains at finite measurement strength. With recoverability restored, we prove a further dimensional distinction in logical operations. In the strict three-dimensional deconfined phase, suitable enhanced tubes restore the equivalence of macroscopically separated representatives, so either representative asymptotically implements the same controlled logical action on a fixed encoding. In two dimensions, recoverability alone does not restore this equivalence: the representatives can remain inequivalent even when both associated encodings are recoverable.

### Paper [187]
Title: A unified finite-site framework for scaffold-client biocondensation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15007
Authors: Hamza Coban, Alfredo Alexander-Katz
Abstract: Client recruitment into biomolecular condensates does not determine how stronger binding changes the bulk free-energy advantage of demixing. Here, we use a unified finite-site thermodynamic framework to examine how scaffold-client interactions affect both the response of a fixed-composition mixture and the organization of its ternary phase diagram. The thermodynamic response to increasing client affinity is determined by the difference in heterotypic bonding between the complete equilibrium phase-separated sample and the corresponding homogeneous state. A finite region of the model exhibits positive scaffold-client concentration covariance but negative heterotypic bond contrast: stronger binding reduces the demixing advantage even when both species enrich in the dense phase. This reduction also occurs with independent scaffold self-association and client-binding site pools, without direct displacement of scaffold bonds. A negative response does not by itself imply condensate shrinkage or dissolution. Across composition space, the same finite binding constraints support scaffold-rich and heterotypic coexistence regimes with distinct bond populations. For divalent clients, these regimes can occupy disconnected regions of the phase diagram and reconnect upon increasing client affinity, depending on scaffold cohesion. Critical-point continuation identifies the folds and cusp organizing these changes, while client valence restricts the parameter range over which disconnected coexistence occurs. Explicit-particle Monte Carlo simulations reproduce the qualitative reentrant response and provide finite-size evidence for disconnected coexistence. These results provide a bond-resolved characterization of fixed-parent affinity response and a complementary description of coexistence under specified association constraints.

### Paper [188]
Title: Reducing TLS Losses in Superconducting Circuits Using Solid Neon
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15021
Authors: A. Palmer, K. Matkovic, P. Russell, E. Helgemo, L. Delventhal, Y. Ashlea Alava, K. Zuo, S. N. Coppersmith, M. C. Cassidy
Abstract: Two level systems (TLSs) are a major source of loss in superconducting devices, limiting the performance of qubits, detectors, and resonators. These defects arise from a range of sources, including surface oxides, crystal defects, as well as fabrication residues which are often difficult to remove. Using NbTiN coplanar waveguide resonators, we show that depositing and subsequently removing a solid neon film can significantly improve device performance. Analysis of the temperature and power dependence indicates that this enhancement arises from reduced TLS dissipation, which we attribute to the redistribution and removal of surface contaminants during neon deposition and evaporation. Atomic force microscopy images of contaminated hexagonal boron nitride flakes before and after neon exposure show that neon treatment can redistribute and remove surface residues. These results demonstrate a simple, non-destructive approach for mitigating TLS losses in superconducting devices and other sensitive materials that cannot withstand aggressive cleaning processes.

### Paper [189]
Title: Crystal Nucleation Driven by Microjets from Hydrodynamically Interacting Thermocavitation Bubbles
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15259
Authors: Nagaraj Nagalingam, Adithya Vignesh, Vikram Korede, Herman J. M. Kramer, Peter J. T. Verheijen, Jan Groenewold, Remco Hartkamp, Johan T. Padding, Huseyin Burak Eral
Abstract: Primary nucleation predominantly dictates the physicochemical, optical and mechanical properties of crystals emerging from solution. Here, we reveal a previously unrecognized nucleation mechanism arising from bubble-bubble hydrodynamic interactions using laser-induced thermocavitation bubble pairs. Under confinement, antiphase generation of two bubbles produces a micron-scale, high-speed liquid jet. Upon impact with the surrounding supersaturated liquid, this microjet generates intense localized shear, thereby inducing crystallization of the model inorganic salt, potassium permanganate. Under identical laser energies and supersaturation levels, crystallization was not observed in single-bubble experiments, in which no microjet was present. We show that the crystallization probability increases with microjet velocity. Boundary integral simulations reveal how experimental parameters such as confinement, bubble spacing, and laser energy govern microjet formation and velocity. We quantify the relationship between jet velocity and the observed enhancement in crystal nucleation kinetics by incorporating shear into the kinetic prefactor in classical nucleation theory. By combining experiments, theory, and simulations, we establish how microjets emerging from bubble-bubble hydrodynamic interactions may influence crystallization kinetics, opening new opportunities for the manufacture of crystalline materials.

### Paper [190]
Title: Cumulants of Operator Semigroups for Hierarchies of Many-Particle Evolution Equations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15304
Authors: V. I. Gerasimenko
Abstract: The paper presents a brief overview of recent advances in the mathematical theory of evolution equations for quantum many-particle systems.
  The structure of expansions representing non-perturbative solutions to the Cauchy problem for hierarchies of fundamental evolution equations for many quantum particles is outlined. It has been shown that the concept of cumulants of operator semigroups forms the basis for non-perturbative representation of solutions to hierarchies of evolution equations that describe the state and observables.
  It is proven that the constructed dynamics of correlations underlie the description of the evolution of the state, both for finitely and infinitely many particles, governed by the BBGKY hierarchy for reduced density operators or by the hierarchy of nonlinear evolution equations for reduced correlation operators. In fact, it is demonstrated that the behavior of particle correlations governs the dynamics of states in quantum systems with an infinite number of particles.

### Paper [191]
Title: Quantization Breaks Classical Mpemba Constraints
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15349
Authors: Yue Liu, Tan Van Vu, Fr\'ed\'eric van Wijland, Hisao Hayakawa
Abstract: The Mpemba effect, in which an initially hotter system relaxes faster than a colder one, has been widely studied in both classical and quantum systems, yet identifying the specific effects of quantization requires a consistent comparison with the corresponding classical dynamics. Starting from the Caldeira-Leggett model, we establish a quantum-classical correspondence for thermal relaxation. We find that the locality of classical energy diffusion forbids the Mpemba effect in single-well and symmetric double-well potentials. Conversely, quantum dynamics allows for direct transitions between nonadjacent energy levels, overcoming the constraints of classical local energy diffusion and enabling the Mpemba effect in these potentials. In the semiclassical limit $\hbar\to0$, the nonlocal transitions become increasingly suppressed, restoring the classical constraints. Our findings reveal that quantization fundamentally unlocks anomalous relaxation pathways, rather than merely modifying relaxation rates.

### Paper [192]
Title: Are Scaling Laws of Universal MLIPs Universal?
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15603
Authors: Theo Keane, Anthony Onwuli, Richard Tomsett, Robert M. Forrest, Jonathan J. Bean
Abstract: Neural scaling laws enable the prediction of returns from larger training datasets, but machine-learned interatomic potential (MLIP) datasets are heterogeneous and expensive to generate, and equal numbers of structures need not contain equal amounts of useful information. We study data scaling under fixed model configurations for M3GNet, MACE, and PET trained on subsets of a combined MatPES-MP-ALOE training pool. Across training structure subsets from 10,000 to 640,000, together with the full 1.16-million-structure training set, we compare uniform random sampling, three chemistry- and structure-informed stratification policies, iterative DIRECT, and furthest-point sampling using embeddings from three pretrained MLIPs. Models are evaluated using cohesive-energy, force, stress, and aggregate losses on both a held-out test set from the parent distribution and the distribution shifted MP-r$^2$SCAN test set. We observe power-law scaling improvements with increasing data, but the fitted scaling exponent, apparent error floor, and relative sample efficiency depend on the model architecture, target quantity, data sampling policy, and test set distribution. Sampling-policy rankings can reverse across test distributions: chemical stratification can improve cross-dataset performance while degrading in-distribution performance, whereas the embedding-space diversity policies frequently under-perform random sampling. Relative loss is strongly associated with test-to-train chemical-space divergence. These results show that structure count is a necessary but insufficient scaling variable for MLIPs. For training MLIPs, the return on additional first-principles calculations depends not only on how many additional structures are included, but also on how their chemical and configurational content aligns with the model's intended use.

### Paper [193]
Title: Defect-induced stabilization of Wigner negativity and parity-protected phase-locking
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15624
Authors: Beatrice Donelli, Stefano Gherardini
Abstract: We show that a parity-preserving delta-shaped potential, mimicking the presence of a localized defect, reduces the continuous $U(1)$-symmetry of a quantum van der Pol oscillator to the discrete $\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetry. This mechanism guarantees protected even-parity stationary quantum coherences, resulting in the generation of non-classical steady-states characterized by Wigner function negativity. Thanks to a non-trivial interplay between the defect and non-linear dissipative channels, this kind of negativity entails parity-protected quantum phase locking, meaning that the oscillator's energy levels simultaneously lock into two opposite phases. Finally, we present a realistic experimental proposal involving the motional state of ultracold alkaline-earth-like atoms in optical tweezers, where the localized defect is engineered via state-selective contact interactions, while the non-linear dissipative channels are driven by Raman sidebands.

### Paper [194]
Title: Intense and stable second-harmonic generation in robust MePEA-based quasi-2D halide perovskite crystals
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15772
Authors: Grigorii Verkhogliadov, Dmitry Semyonov, Elizaveta Sapozhnikova, Dmitry Tatarinov, Marina Tepliakova, Nina Ratmanova, Ivan Andreev, Ivan Melnichenko, Natalia Kryzhanovskaya, Alexey Zhukov, Quynh Le-Van, Daria Khmelevskaia, Anatoly Pushkarev
Abstract: Quasi-2D halide perovskites offer a unique platform for nonlinear optics applications, where structural and optical properties are tuned by the variation of organic spacer cations. Notably, quasi-2D perovskite single crystals can demonstrate intense and stable second-harmonic generation signals when a proper composition is selected. Here, the influence of phenethylammonium and 4-methyl-2-phenylethylammonium spacers on the crystal symmetry and second-harmonic signal response of quasi-2D perovskite phases with a varying number of single-octahedron [PbX$_6$]$^{4-}$ layers ($n$) is investigated. High-quality perovskite crystals with lateral dimensions of several hundred micrometers are fabricated using a cavity-confined crystallization synthesis method. Optical characterization of the crystals shows strong and temporally stable second-harmonic generation. Power-dependent and polarization-resolved measurements confirm the second-order nonlinear origin of the observed response and reveal anisotropic emission patterns. Our results demonstrate a direct correlation between spacer-induced center-symmetry breaking and nonlinear optical activity. These results establish quasi-2D halide perovskites as a promising class of symmetry-engineered materials for efficient frequency conversion and integrated nonlinear photonics.

### Paper [195]
Title: Monopole crystal and effective theory for a monopole-enhanced 3D U(1) gauge theory
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15922
Authors: Alessandro Mariani
Abstract: Recently, a version of U(1) lattice gauge theory on the cubic lattice with a monopole enhancement term was considered in the literature. In this work, we further our understanding of this theory with a mixture of analytical and numerical methods. We show that for sufficiently large monopole enhancement, the monopoles are no longer dilute and they arrange themselves into an antiferromagnetic, N\'eel-ordered ground state. Nonetheless, a variant of Polyakov's argument for the dilute excitations over the dense ground state leads to an effective sine-Gordon model which is qualitatively similar to that of the standard theory. We also predict analytically the presence of first-order transitions as the monopole chemical potential is varied at fixed gauge coupling, corresponding to a change in the dominant monopole charge. Similarly, we predict a transition in the Ising universality class as the gauge coupling is varied. We then use numerical methods to study the phase diagram of the theory and check some of our predictions.

### Paper [196]
Title: Atomistic simulations of electrochemical systems with machine learning potentials
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15939
Authors: Marialore Sulpizi, J\"org Behler
Abstract: Modern machine learning potentials (MLPs) enable large-scale atomistic simulations at unprecedented accuracy. The incorporation of explicit physical interactions such as long-range electrostatics has continuously broadened their applicability to increasingly complex systems in chemistry and materials science. Extending MLPs to electrochemistry and electrocatalysis, however, is still challenging as it requires the consideration of electrode potentials and electric fields in the atomic interactions. Such approaches have emerged only recently, and although the field is evolving rapidly, further conceptual advances are still needed for carrying out reliable simulations at finite temperature under applied potential. In this review, we provide a comprehensive overview about recent progress in the atomistic simulation of electrified interfaces using MLPs. Current developments fall into three different categories. The first comprises linear response models that describe polarisation, polarisability, and Born effective charges, extending conventional MLPs to finite electric fields. The second includes methods that directly learn a field-dependent potential energy surface. The third consists of a series of hybrid approaches combining classical models with machine learning. Although still under active development, these methods represent a major step toward more realistic simulations approaching the quality of electronic structure calculations. Finally, we discuss open challenges and possible future directions for advancing MLP-based simulations aiming for a bottom-up understanding of atomistic processes at electrified interfaces.

### Paper [197]
Title: Comprehensive quantum electrodynamics of fast electrons interacting with dissipative nanostructures and quantum light
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15967
Authors: Alessandro Ciattoni
Abstract: Theoretical descriptions of the quantum electrodynamical interaction between fast electrons and nanostructures conventionally rely on standard macroscopic quantum electrodynamics (MQED), which, when applied to bounded objects in vacuum, formally requires an asymptotic limiting procedure to account for free-space radiation modes. To explicitly incorporate these modes from the outset, we employ the modified Langevin noise formalism (MLNF), which independently quantizes both the asymptotic scattering modes and the electromagnetic field generated by dissipative material sources. Utilizing an exact second-order Magnus expansion, we derive a closed-form, nonperturbative master formula for the transverse-momentum-resolved energy exchange probability. In the unilluminated regime, we demonstrate that at finite nanostructure temperatures, the vacuum scattering and thermal material fluctuations do not mathematically recombine into the total imaginary part of the dyadic Green's function. This breakdown does not violate the Fluctuation-Dissipation Theorem, because the explicit zero-temperature scattering field acts as an independent dynamical system that maintains the configuration out of global thermodynamic equilibrium. This fundamental separation reveals an intrinsic Stokes--Anti-Stokes asymmetry, yielding distinct transverse diffraction patterns for energy loss and thermal energy gain, and establishing a generalized detailed-balance relation that governs the radiative reduction of the apparent temperature in nanoscale electron thermometry. In the illuminated regime, our framework rigorously incorporates quantum-optical states into macroscopic dissipative electrodynamics, demonstrating that...

### Paper [198]
Title: What Can Next Generation Ultrafast Light Sources Tell Us About Quantum Materials?
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15997
Authors: Allan S. Johnson
Abstract: Ultrafast spectroscopy of quantum materials has emerged as a principle application of modern ultrafast optical sources. Measurements made with ultrashort pulses allow the normally strongly coupled internal degrees of freedom inside these systems to be de-coupled in the time-domain, revealing the key interactions for the different orders that lead to dramatic emergent phenomena. This has naturally motivated a wide range of experiments, and quantum materials have now been studied with ultrafast sources spanning from the hard X-ray to the THz, with fields intense enough to create non-equilibrium states, and using pulses down to the attosecond duration. As the optical spectroscopist's toolbox becomes increasingly complete, it is worth pausing and reflecting on what are the capabilities that we currently lack for examining quantum materials, and what are the problems that require the next generation of ultrafast sources now coming on-line or being designed. Here I will offer an overview of the remaining missing capabilities in ultrafast optical sources as required for studying quantum materials, and identify some outstanding problems where I believe these new sources can provide decisive impact in our understanding of quantum and strongly-correlated materials.

### Paper [199]
Title: The importance of shear strain in narrow Si/SiGe quantum wells
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16015
Authors: Hudaiba Soomro, Talise Oh, M. A. Eriksson, Benjamin D. Woods, Mark Friesen
Abstract: Several heterostructure profiles have been proposed for Si/SiGe quantum wells, to improve the operation of quantum-dot spin qubits. These structures share a common goal of generating either of the two wave vectors that govern the conduction-band valley coupling, dubbed $k_0$ and $k_1$. Here, we focus on the long-wavelength $k_1$ term that couples valleys when paired with shear strain, and which governs the Dresselhaus spin-orbit coupling. We show that shear strain induced by metal top gates may be enough to activate the $k_1$ valley-coupling mechanism, mitigating the need for "ultrasharp" interfaces, as commonly assumed for large valley energy splittings. We propose a scheme featuring narrow quantum wells that does not require ultrasharp interfaces, but should still provide large deterministic valley splittings (when paired with shear strain), and large spin-orbit couplings and $g$-factor corrections, which are of great interest for experimental implementations.

### Paper [200]
Title: Quantum Material Generation via Coherent Hamiltonian Search
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16016
Authors: Jiang Yuhan, Matthew Otten
Abstract: Discovering new materials with targeted properties is a central goal of materials science, yet the space of candidates is vast. Data-driven generative models learn the distribution of known materials, but they extrapolate poorly, and a generated structure is not known to be stable. A material's properties follow from its Hamiltonian, and a quantum computer can calculate them from it. We introduce the quantum material generative algorithm (QMage), a coherent search, guided by the Hamiltonian, over crystalline configurations of atomic composition, unit cell structure, and atomic positions, for one whose calculated properties meet the targets to within a given error tolerance. The computation is a single coherent quantum circuit in which each configuration's Hamiltonian is block-encoded, its target eigenstate isolated and properties evaluated, and the satisfying configurations amplified by fixed-point search and measured. One framework covers many targets, from formation energy and band gaps to elasticity and conductivity. We design the algorithm for the mature fault-tolerant era and estimate its cost in logical qubits and Toffoli depth from a classically routine benchmark to targets where established methods are unreliable. The approach generates candidate materials from first principles, rather than generalizing from those known, and could enable materials discovery when the target properties are difficult to predict classically.

### Paper [201]
Title: Mapping the High-Field Response of Superconducting Nanowire Single-Photon Detectors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16071
Authors: Harshit Agarwal, Yueh-Chun Wu, Katyayani Seal, Niels Noordzij, Andreas Fognini, Ronan Gourgues, G\'abor B. Hal\'asz, Benjamin J. Lawrie, Nirjhar Sarkar
Abstract: Superconducting nanowire single-photon detectors (SNSPDs) are increasingly being integrated into cryogenic quantum-sensing platforms that operate in large magnetic fields. Here, the high-field limits of these measurements were probed by characterizing the dark count response of a NbTiN SNSPD at up to 14~T using in situ control of the detector orientation and benchmarking that performance against normal detector operation in smaller magnetic fields. The detector performance is strongly anisotropic: perpendicular magnetic fields rapidly reduce the usable bias-current range, whereas near-parallel alignment enables operation at substantially larger total fields, with a finite operating margin retained to 12~T and measurable dark-count response at the 14~T field limit. The angular dependence is governed predominantly by the perpendicular component of the magnetic field, consistent with vortex-mediated suppression of the superconducting current scale. The same field-dependent current scale also determines the electrical pulse amplitude, linking high-field detector operation directly to readout performance. These results establish a practical route for deploying SNSPDs in multi-tesla quantum-sensing and high-field cryogenic experiments.

### Paper [202]
Title: Holographic Wilsonian RG of Yang-Mills theory in AdS
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16074
Authors: Soner Albayrak
Abstract: In this work, we extend the equilibrium-evolution duality between stochastic quantization and Wilsonian renormalization to non-abelian gauge theories in anti-de Sitter space, for which the radial coordinate of AdS plays the role of the stochastic time. We show, to all orders in the fields for bulk theories without explicit radial dependence, that the Langevin distribution at time $t$ is the product of the ultraviolet and infrared radial wavefunctions at the cutoff $\epsilon=t$. We therefore identify this Langevin process as the ground-state (Doob) transform of the radial evolution. By explicit calculation, we also extend the existing dictionary in the stochastic-renormalization duality: the radial component of the bulk field is Zwanziger's stochastic gauge-fixing function; radial gauge is Parisi-Wu quantization; and the bulk gauge $A_z=\alpha^{-1}\partial_iA_i$ is Zwanziger's gauge. We also obtain the expressions for the triple-trace and quadruple-trace couplings of the holographic Wilsonian action at finite cutoff in closed form; in the infrared, they reduce to the known gluon correlators and, at the total-energy pole, to flat-space Yang-Mills amplitudes as expected. Although our explicit results are provided at tree-level and derived in AdS$_4$, we also comment on the de Sitter counterpart of these statements and on the loop corrections.

### Paper [203]
Title: A thermodynamically consistent, fully compressible, fluctuating hydrodynamics model of a Cahn Hilliard binary fluid mixture
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16121
Authors: Anuj Chaudhri
Abstract: We derive, from first principles, a thermodynamically consistent set of fully compressible fluctuating hydrodynamic equations for a Cahn-Hilliard binary fluid mixture, in isothermal and non-isothermal form and for symmetric and non-symmetric mixtures. The derivation uses the GENERIC formalism, with the friction operator constructed from flux fluctuations following Jelic and Ottinger, so that deterministic and stochastic fluxes derive from the same operator and correlation matrix and the fluctuation-dissipation theorem holds by construction. We show that the momentum equation must use the total mass density, that the Cahn-Hilliard variable is the mass fraction, and that the diffusion flux must be driven by the specific, not the volumetric, exchange potential. We derive a generalized Korteweg tensor for square-gradient energies in density and composition, and show that the reversible pressure tensor of the non-isothermal model is fixed by the degeneracy condition, producing a thermal capillary stress when the interfacial free energy has an entropic part. Thermodynamic consistency is proved in full: Jacobi identity, positivity and degeneracy, local energy conservation with interstitial-working fluxes, a local entropy balance, Soret-Dufour reciprocity, and Einstein statistics of the linearized equations. The final equations are written in conservative form for finite-volume discretization, and closed-form static and dynamic structure factors are given for all models as analytic benchmarks. An appendix shows that the same models follow from classical irreversible thermodynamics with the Helmholtz or Gibbs free energy; this route is unique for isothermal flows and has a gauge freedom in the non-isothermal case that GENERIC removes.

### Paper [204]
Title: Integrated optical isolators enabled by 2D semiconductors via spin-momentum locking
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16125
Authors: Fernando Mart\'in-Romero, V\'ictor J. G\'omez, Alberto G. Curto
Abstract: Integrated optical isolators based on spin-momentum locking hold great potential for protecting photonic integrated circuits from unwanted reflections. Currently, the most common implementations are magneto-optical isolators that require an externally applied magnetic field. Nevertheless, a wide range of nonreciprocal effects remains to be exploited in alternative material platforms. Although the majority of these effects induce changes to the propagation of light as a function of its polarization, they can be directly connected to the propagation direction through spin-momentum locking. We employ a computational approach to investigate various designs of integrated isolators based on nonreciprocal 2D semiconductors, studying the influence of their geometry and optical properties on the device performance. Additionally, we propose a novel concept for an all-optical isolator based on spin-valley polarization and optically tunable magnetism in transition metal dichalcogenide monolayers. Our results establish 2D semiconductors as an advanced platform for integrated nonreciprocal devices, enabling functionalities that are difficult to achieve with conventional magneto-optical materials like all-optical operation.

### Paper [205]
Title: Proliferating against the current: how multistep branching nucleation can sort microtubule polarity in the spindle
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16200
Authors: Ido Lavi, Sebastian F\"urthauer, Daniel J. Needleman, Michael J. Shelley
Abstract: The spindle, which segregates chromosomes during cell division, is a paradigm of cytoskeletal self-organization: a highly ordered structure that persists for hours is assembled from dynamic microtubules that branch, grow, slide, and turn over within tens of seconds. Its microtubules point inward, with minus ends clustered at the poles and plus ends extending toward the chromosomes at the center of the structure. In large meiotic spindles this arrangement is puzzling, since microtubules nucleate near the chromosomes and grow at their plus ends much faster than motors slide them backward. One would therefore expect the plus ends, rather than the minus ends, to radiate outward. Here, we propose a resolution to this seeming paradox through a kinetic model that integrates the core filament-level processes. When branching complexes assemble with a sufficient delay, for instance through several sequential rate-limiting steps, they accumulate on old microtubule lattice sites near the backward-sliding minus ends of mother microtubules. Each generation is then born farther back than the last, so the self-renewing population drifts toward the minus ends even though each filament's center advances toward its plus end. For the sliding and growth velocities measured in Xenopus egg extract spindles, a multistep mechanism requires at least four rate-limiting steps. The kinetic theory has a surprisingly amenable structure: the filament length configuration variable can be eliminated exactly, yielding a finite space-time hierarchy, and the direction of population drift follows from a general criterion comparing the mean lattice age at branching sites with the ratio of plus-end advance to sliding speed.

### Paper [206]
Title: Stretched bilayer-cytoskeleton links hold echinocytes in a model splenic slit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16241
Authors: Zhaojie Chai, Jianlu Zheng, He Li, Ming Dao, George Em Karniadakis
Abstract: Stored red blood cells become echinocytes, growing spicules before losing membrane; the spleen clears the resulting small, spherical cells. Whether spicules alone hinder splenic passage is unknown. Shape and area change together. In a two-component model whose shrinking cytoskeleton raises spicules, we drove a discocyte and stage I to III echinocytes of equal area and volume through a model splenic slit. The pressure drop needed to pass rose with stage; stage III echinocytes were held at 37 to 50 times the pressure drop that passed the discocyte. Bilayer-cytoskeleton links stretched near full extension held these cells; lengthening them about twofold released a held cell. Capillary-sized channels, as in our device, separated the stages far less. Of the shape classes in stored blood, the model held at high pressure drops only storage-induced microerythrocytes, which perfused human spleens clear most. Spicules, besides membrane loss, may decide which stored cells the spleen retains.

### Paper [207]
Title: Intrinsic Chiral HOTI Stabilized by a Single Diagonal Reflection
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16261
Authors: Danilo Polo Ojito, Jackson Saunders, Ellis Bender, Camelia Prodan, Emil Prodan
Abstract: Higher-order topological insulators and superconductors, under combined point and fundamental symmetries, display topological modes supported by boundaries of higher codimensions, such as a corner mode. In this work, we explain a theoretical device that enables us to claim that there exists {\it one and only one} intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase for the quarter-plane geometry with point-symmetry given by reflection about the diagonal of the corner. Furthermore, we supply a minimal tight-binding model of this phase and design an acoustic crystal whose dynamics reproduce that of the model. The robust properties of the corner mode are investigated by COMSOL simulations.

### Paper [208]
Title: Visibility graphs of irregularly sampled two-dimensional fields via Voronoi tessellations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16336
Authors: Francisco J. Mu\~noz, Juan Carlos Nu\~no
Abstract: Visibility graphs map a time series into a network by linking two data whenever the straight line between them is not obstructed by intermediate data. Their extension to two-dimensional fields has so far been restricted to regular lattices, where visibility is checked along a finite number of lattice directions. We propose a definition for scalar data sampled at arbitrary, irregularly scattered points of the plane. Each point is assigned its Voronoi cell, so that the data become a landscape of prisms, and two points are linked when the line of sight between them clears the cells crossed by the connecting segment. This yields horizontal ($\HVGV$) and natural ($\NVGV$) Voronoi visibility graphs that reduce exactly to the classical horizontal and natural visibility graphs when the points are collinear, contain the Gabriel graph of the point set, and can be built by an exact walk through the Delaunay triangulation. For uncorrelated heights, the horizontal graph depends only on the ranks of the data, so its statistics are independent of the marginal distribution. The probability that two points see each other is $2/[(M+1)(M+2)]$, where $M$ is the number of Voronoi cells crossed, which reduces the problem to the geometry of the tessellation. For Poisson points this gives a degree distribution with a scale-free tail $P(k)\simeq(\pi^3/4)\,k^{-2}$ and a mean degree that grows as $(\pi^3/8)\ln N$, in contrast with the finite value $\langle k\rangle=4$ of one-dimensional series. Two dimensions are therefore the marginal case in which isotropic horizontal visibility ceases to be local. Numerical simulations with up to $N=1.28\times10^5$ points support these predictions.

### Paper [209]
Title: Exact power spectra and effective dimensionality in critical block-GOE networks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16427
Authors: Konstantinos Grammenos, Dionissios Hristopulos
Abstract: Criticality in neural networks is crucial for optimal information processing. Recent studies have shifted focus from static criticality to frequency-dependent behavior, particularly in oscillatory systems modeled by block-Gaussian orthogonal ensemble (block-GOE) matrices. However, analytical tools to predict spectral properties such as power spectrum and effective dimensionality remain scarce. In this work, we derive exact solutions for the network-averaged power spectrum $\operatorname{Tr}_{N}[\mathbf{S}(\omega)]$, the network autocovariance $\operatorname{Tr}_{N}[\mathbf{C}(\tau)]$, and the normalized effective dimensionality $D_N(\omega)$ in block-GOE ensembles. These results provide a comprehensive framework for understanding how oscillatory networks operate near criticality, offering insights into the dynamic balance between stability and flexibility in large-scale neural systems.

### Paper [210]
Title: Gatemon qubits based on in-situ ternary InAsSb/Al nanowire Josephson junctions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16595
Authors: Zhenhai Sun, David Feldstein-Bofill, Sangeeth Kallatt, Sabbir A. Khan, Morten Kjaergaard
Abstract: Ternary InAsSb/Al nanowires combine compositionally engineerable semiconductor properties with epitaxial proximity superconductivity, making them attractive for hybrid quantum circuits and mesoscopic superconductivity. Here, we realize gatemon qubits based on shadow-defined in-situ InAsSb/Al Josephson junctions in a single growth process. Gate-dependent microwave spectroscopy demonstrates electrostatic tuning of the qubit transition over several gigahertz. Rabi oscillations and Ramsey interference show coherent qubit control, with representative values T1 = 0.79 us and T2* = 0.91 us. Across five gatemons distributed over three chips, the measured relaxation times range from approximately 0.2 us to 1 us and decrease with increasing qubit frequency. Their magnitude is comparable to a circuit estimate of spontaneous emission into the electromagnetic environment. These results establish in-situ InAsSb/Al junctions as coherent, gate-tunable circuit elements and introduce cQED architecture as a microwave probe of the dynamics of the ternary weak link.

### Paper [211]
Title: Quantum tunneling of a measurement-driven active particle
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16626
Authors: Tobias Nadolny, Matteo Brunelli, Christoph Bruder, Michel Fruchart
Abstract: Active particles can exhibit persistent motion set by their orientation. We demonstrate this key property for a quantum active particle: The particle's motion is locked to the orientation of its spin due to spin-orbit coupling and damping, and the orientation is made persistent through continuous measurement via the quantum Zeno effect. As a result, individual quantum trajectories feature an active run-and-tumble motion. When the particle impinges on a potential barrier, its persistent velocity leads to accumulation near the barrier, in contrast with the reflection of a free quantum particle. We show that quantum tunneling enhances the active particle's transmission rate compared to that of an analogous classical particle.

### Paper [212]
Title: Geometric Theory of Quasiperiodically Driven Systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16683
Authors: Chengzhi Ye, Marin Bukov
Abstract: Quasiperiodically driven systems give rise to a rich set of physical phenomena in contemporary quantum physics, with applications ranging from nonequilibrium dynamics to Hamiltonian engineering and control. However, unlike periodically driven (Floquet) systems, their study is hindered by the absence of a generic theory to describe their evolution using an effective time-independent Hamiltonian and its eigenstates.
