Based on counterdiabatic driving, we construct a general framework to identify and determine the dynamically invariant spaces of arbitrary quasiperiodic drives (i.e., the generalization of Floquet states). We decompose their unitary evolution using the exact effective average-energy operator {\AE} and the corresponding gauge potential, which we compute via a variational principle in frequency-lattice operator space. We then formulate a four-fold taxonomy of quasiperiodic drives based on the geometry and topology of their dynamically invariant spaces into (non)abelian and (un)twisted classes. The abelian untwisted class admits a direct extension of Floquet's theorem to incommensurate quasiperiodic drives; going beyond, we derive a nonabelian generalization of Floquet theory. Finally, we propose a concept of ergodicity whose onset is marked by coalescing dynamically invariant eigenspaces, in direct analogy to the emergence of ergodicity in a mixed classical phase space. The effective mathematical framework, variational principle, and nonabelian ergodicity we put forward apply beyond quasiperiodic drives.

### Paper [213]
Title: Discrete anomaly inflow from SymTFT of $U(1)$ global symmetry: the modified Villain resolution
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16696
Authors: Napat Poovuttikul, K. Sapmee
Abstract: We construct a lattice SymTFT for a $U(1)$ global symmetry with anomaly coefficient $k$ using the modified Villain formulation, in which the spin structure required for odd $k$ is realised explicitly by lattice Majorana fermions. We demonstrate that the same Majorana fermions sit at the junctions of the symmetry lines, exactly where the fusion of two $U(1)$ elements wraps around the circle. From the lattice algebra of lines and junctions we derive the $R$- and $F$-symbols in the bulk and on the gapped boundary, and show that for odd $k$ the boundary $F$-symbol obeys the Gu-Wen relation for the $U(1)$ symmetry defect, without passing through a bosonic shadow. Restricting to $\mathbb{Z}_2\subset U(1)$, we reproduce the mod-4 anomalous phase from a mapping torus and the fermionic stacking rule. As a simple application of this SymTFT, we also determine which subgroups $\mathbb{Z}_d\subset U(1)$ can be gauged and the symmetry of the resulting theories. This is a first step towards deriving, from the anomaly inflow of a continuous symmetry, the junction data of global anomalies beyond group cohomology.

### Paper [214]
Title: Symmetry-Controlled Criticality and Zeno Localization in a Monitored Quasiperiodic Superconductor
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16912
Authors: Pinaki Singha, Marcin Szyniszewski, Nilanjan Roy, Auditya Sharma
Abstract: Measurement-induced transitions of free fermions depend strongly on symmetry, but their interplay with quasiperiodic localization and superconducting pairing remains largely unexplored. We study a continuously monitored model of free fermions with pairing, where quasiperiodicity both controls localization and changes the symmetry class of the monitored field theory. Using topological entanglement entropy as a probe of nonlocal correlations in quantum state diffusion simulations, we identify a transition between a critical phase whose von Neumann entanglement entropy scales as $(\ln L)^2$ and an area-law phase, whereas no transition occurs in the clean limit, consistent with the corresponding DIII and D nonlinear sigma models. The transition line exhibits strong multifractality and depends nonmonotonically on quasiperiodicity, which we associate with the symmetry crossover. Deep in the Zeno regime, we derive an effective non-Hermitian Bogoliubov-de Gennes description of individual trajectories and obtain a closed-form localization length that quantitatively agrees with numerics. Our results reveal how symmetry, quasiperiodic localization, and measurement backaction combine to determine both critical entanglement and microscopic trajectory localization in monitored superconductors.

### Paper [215]
Title: Unified master equation for driven multilevel quantum systems: Derivation, dynamics, and spectral signatures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16952
Authors: Sagarika Basak, D. Blume
Abstract: Drive-modified dissipation in multilevel quantum systems is not, in general, accurately captured by conventional master equations. We derive a unified master equation using Lewis-Riesenfeld dynamical invariants, accommodating arbitrary driving within Born-Markov theory. We demonstrate the applicability of the unified master equation to constant-Rabi-coupling (continuous-wave), periodically-modulated-Rabi-coupling, and nonperiodic (ramped and chirped) driving protocols. Fluorescence spectra of a periodically driven three-level $\Lambda$ system reveal signatures missed by conventional descriptions. We trace the limitations of the conventional descriptions to inaccurate bath-frequency sampling and the neglect of nonsecular dissipative couplings, and map out the parameter regimes that require a unified treatment. The fluorescence spectra provide experimentally accessible signatures, which serve as a spectroscopic guide to reliable modeling of dissipative processes that can inform quantum control, simulation, sensing, and information processing.

### Paper [216]
Title: Higher-Group Statistics on Lattices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16962
Authors: Chen Yang, Yu-An Chen
Abstract: Crossing a domain wall can permute anyons, and intersecting strings can create a particle. These couplings between defects of various dimensions are specified by higher groups. We develop a lattice framework defining higher-group statistics as Berry phases of closed sequences of hopping operators, invariant under local modifications of the operators. Our central example is a closed Majorana chain, a 1-dimensional topological superconductor, embedded in 3 dimensions. We construct a loop-flip process with phase $+i$, refining the usual $\pm 1$ loop-flip invariant through intermediate transfer of fermion parity between the chain and physical fermions. In two dimensions, charge-wall statistics distinguish two $\mathbb Z_4$ toric codes enriched by $\mathbb Z_2$ charge-conjugation symmetry, with identical anyon statistics but distinct gauged theories: the quantum doubles of the dihedral group $D_4$ and the quaternion group $Q_8$. We provide two complementary constructions of statistical processes. The first extends the Smith normal form algorithm in [Phys. Rev. X $\textbf{16}$, 011010 (2026), arXiv:2412.01886] to higher groups, generating processes from lattice geometry and prescribed local moves. For a finite higher group $\mathbb G$, we prove that statistical processes on a $d$-sphere are classified by $H_{d+2}(B\mathbb G,\mathbb Z)$. The second constructs processes directly from homology classes without examining exponentially many defect configurations, enabling us to study statistics for complex higher-group structures where the previous method is computationally prohibitive. These processes detect boundary anomalies of higher-group symmetry-protected topological phases. For example, we distinguish 2-group anomalies in 1, 2, and 3 dimensions. Higher-group statistics provide new invariants for characterizing coupled defects in quantum many-body systems.

### Paper [217]
Title: Lorentz Boosts and Governing Equations of the Quantum Geometric Tensor in 4D Energy-Momentum Space
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16967
Authors: Yiming Pan
Abstract: In solids, the quantum geometric tensor (QGT), $Q_{\mu\nu}=g_{\mu\nu}-\frac{i}{2} {\Omega}_{\mu \nu}$, combines the quantum metric and Berry curvature. Its imaginary part governs anomalous velocity and quantized Hall transport, while its real part determines the quantum weight and geometric contributions to flat-band superfluid stiffness and dielectric response. Here, we extend the QGT to four-dimensional (4D) energy-momentum space ($\omega/c,k$), treating frequency and momentum as independent coordinates. Invariance of the real-spacetime plane-wave phase fixes their Lorentz transformation in energy-momentum space. Lorentz boosts of static Dirac and Weyl systems then generate frequency metrics, quadrupolar frequency-momentum shear metrics, and electric-like Berry-Maxwell fields. We identify five Lorentz-invariant contractions and derive the governing field equations and flow conservation laws for both the quantum metric and Berry curvature from static Weyl geometry and Lorentz covariance. We further connect Gaussian spectral amplitudes of the QGT to localized Gaussian events in 4D spacetime. Finally, we discuss experimental realizations in spacetime crystals or time-varying media and clarify how this unified quantum geometry of a gravitational-like metric and electromagnetic-like gauge fields connects geometric self-consistency with physical event dynamics.

### Paper [218]
Title: Dynamical mean-field theory of latching Potts networks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16999
Authors: Anindita Basu, Carlo Orientale Caputo, Fernando Lucas Metz, Alessandro Treves
Abstract: Spontaneous transitions between memories, as observed in internally generated cognition such as mind-wandering and free association, require neural dynamics capable of destabilizing one attractive memory state and moving towards another. We ask what minimal representational structure is required for such transitions to emerge in random associative networks. To address this question, we study Potts associative networks with $S$ active states per unit and slow adaptive thresholds. The $S=1$ case can model a standard Hopfield-like network of binary units. We employ dynamical mean-field theory in the extensive-load regime, where the number of stored patterns scales linearly with network size and connectivity, and we focus on transitions between two condensed memories, which are singled out in the connectivity matrix. In the finite-load regime, we show that transitions are governed by an effective drive term, whose sign and temporal evolution distinguish high-latching (structured), low-latching (random), and non-latching regimes. The resulting phase diagram shows that adaptation alone is not sufficient: transitions only occur within certain ranges of the overlap between the two memories. In the binary case $S=1$, in particular, no unit can be active in both memories but in different Potts states, thus strongly supressing the high-latching, structured transitions that exist for $S>1$. In the extensive-load regime, non-condensed memories generate colored fluctuations and retarded self-interactions through an effective memory kernel. We find that latching trajectories change the effective timescale of this kernel, a dynamical signature of transitions in the extensive-load regime. These results indicate that multi-state Potts-like representations are essential for structured memory-to-memory transitions, a hallmark of spontaneous thought.

### Paper [219]
Title: Ergodicity breaking for data classification in a dissipative Ising model with competing two- and four-body interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17036
Authors: Simon Kochsiek, Mario Boneberg, Igor Lesanovsky
Abstract: We explore the close relationship between the processing of data and the dynamics of interacting many-body systems. For example, certain classes of quantum neural networks can be mapped onto dissipative quantum Ising models whose evolution is described by a Lindblad master equation. In these architectures, emergent nonlinearities lead to non-ergodic dynamics under which initial states located in different basins of attraction evolve towards distinguishable stationary states. This mechanism underlies the classification of quantum data. Motivated by this connection, we consider an Ising model with competing two- and four-body interactions. Using a combination of mean-field calculations and exact small-scale numerics, we show that the stationary state manifold can be designed to enable multi-class classification. We also investigate the structure of the ensuing basins of attraction, thereby showing that these systems allow for the classification of quantum states with nonlinear decision boundaries.

### Paper [220]
Title: De Sitter Gravity and Emergent Time from Fusion Matrix Product States and SYK
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17045
Authors: Shinsei Ryu, Yu-ki Suzuki
Abstract: We propose a quantum-mechanical model dual to two-dimensional de Sitter (dS) gravity. It is a quantum channel, defined by a matrix product state that fuses one spin at each step, and is related to a limit of the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev model without chord crossings. Its simplest part is a random walk with an emergent $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ symmetry, which we identify with the symmetry of the future boundary of dS. It selects a time variable in which the walk obeys the Schwarzian action and yields the disk amplitude of dS Jackiw-Teitelboim gravity. As the simplest case, we consider free massless scalar fields in the fixed background of dS$_2$. We find the agreement of the two-point function in the Bunch-Davies vacuum and the matching of symmetry. Our construction provides a concrete model for the dS/CFT correspondence for two-dimensional de Sitter gravity.

### Paper [221]
Title: Multi-particle quantum walk search on a two-dimensional lattice
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17061
Authors: Gon\c{c}alo Meneses, D. O. Oriekhov
Abstract: In the present paper we study the formulation of a search problem and the efficiency of multi-particle quantum walk search algorithms. The discrete-time evolution of each particle in space is treated according to Grover's quantum walk evolution operator. We focus on the local interactions models, where the particles' states are modified only when two or more of them appear on the same grid site. The main focus is given to the two-dimensional grid in space. We study the dependence of the search algorithm asymptotic success time and probability on the interaction strength and the generated maximal entanglement between particles. We show that the interacting and non-interacting two- and three-particle search algorithm attains a constant factor improvement over the single-particle counterpart. In addition, we provide a new generalized model of interactions for multi-particle quantum walks.

### Paper [222]
Title: Asymmetric Frequency Conversion and Violation of Kirchhoff's Law of Thermal Radiation in Phased Floquet Metasurfaces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17062
Authors: Yao-Ting Wang, Emanuele Galiffi, Sander A. Mann, Mauro Antezza, Andrea Al\`u
Abstract: We study a system of three closely spaced conducting sheets, whose thermal emission largely violates Kirchhoff's law through nonreciprocal frequency conversion enabled by Floquet phenomena. We leverage surface mode transitions controlled by temporal modulation of one of the metasurfaces, resulting in largely asymmetric frequency conversion. Our findings broaden the capabilities of Floquet metasurfaces for wave conversion and energy manipulation, paving the way towards magnet-free nonreciprocal photonic devices with opportunities for thermal management and efficient photonic refrigeration.

### Paper [223]
Title: Physically Interpretable Basin Selection in Discrete-Variable Origami Design
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17196
Authors: Lucy Liu, Leon M. Kamp, Katia Bertoldi, L. Mahadevan
Abstract: Automatic differentiation has enabled rapid progress on continuous-variable mechanical design problems, but gradient-based optimization still struggles with discrete variables. The standard approach is to optimize using a continuous relaxation of the choices, then commit to the nearest discrete design at the end. Why this procedure selects unfavorable basins is poorly understood. Here, we introduce a practical, interpretable design problem inspired by origami, in which automatic differentiation is used to design shapes with a target functional behavior. Each origami sheet has a fixed geometry, and a bilevel optimization framework tunes which one of four discrete hinge types to place at each fold. Baseline gradient-based design settles into basins with a large discretization gap: relaxed optimization solves 97% of runs, but the final, discrete solutions succeed only half the time. We find that the gap can be relieved with a surprisingly simple modification. An initialization prior uniformly biased toward the stiff, flat hinge option boosts the final solve fraction from 49% to 69%. Applying the prior adds no additional compute and leaves the optimization process unchanged. Compared to the baseline, sheets produced with the prior have greater geometric compatibility and mechanical stiffness. Directly penalizing frustration and floppiness in the outer loss function produces an even greater improvement than the prior does, raising the solve fraction to 78% and indicating that these properties help distinguish the unfavorable basins. Our results demonstrate that loss landscapes for mechanical design can have physically meaningful basins that are not robust to discretization and cause solves to fail. Targeting these basins' physical signatures, even with just an initialization bias, helps avoid them.

### Paper [224]
Title: Quantum Metrology and Quantum Battery in a Hyperfine-Coupled Spin System
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17310
Authors: Omar Bachain, Mohamed Amazioug, Rachid Ahl Laamara
Abstract: We investigate multiparameter quantum metrology and the energetic properties of an effective electron--proton spin system inspired by the hyperfine structure of the hydrogen atom. Considering the external magnetic field $\mathcal B$ and the hyperfine coupling strength $\Lambda$ as unknown parameters, we derive the quantum Fisher information matrix and establish the corresponding quantum Cram\'er--Rao bounds for their simultaneous estimation. We analyze the estimation precision and the correlations between the two parameters, showing that the weak-commutativity condition is satisfied despite the noncommutativity of the associated symmetric logarithmic derivatives. Independently, we explore the energetic properties of the system under a unitary charging protocol, characterizing its ergotropy, anti-ergotropy, storage capacity, and energy-basis coherence. We investigate the dependence of these quantities on the magnetic field, hyperfine coupling, temperature, and charging time. Our results provide a comprehensive characterization of the metrological properties and energetic performance of a hyperfine spin-based quantum system, highlighting its potential as a platform for quantum sensing and energy storage.

### Paper [225]
Title: Ensemble phase transitions in measurement-unitary hybrid quantum circuits
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17322
Authors: Saptarshi Mandal, Sthitadhi Roy
Abstract: The universal properties of state ensembles generated by quantum dynamics provide a refined lens into Hilbert-space ergodicity and deep thermalisation, or lack thereof. We investigate deep-ergodicity breaking transitions of an ancillary qubit entangled with a system undergoing dynamics via hybrid quantum circuits, where unitary evolution is interspersed with projective measurements. Specifically, we analyse the statistical properties of two distinct state ensembles: the trajectory ensemble (TE) conditioned on the measurement outcomes of the hybrid circuit, and the projected ensemble (PE) generated by further projective measurements of the system at the final temporal boundary. We find that both ensembles undergo non-analytic changes -- ensemble phase transitions -- at the critical point of the measurement-induced purification transition, despite their first moments remaining completely maximally mixed and blind to the transition. For Haar-random circuits, the TE transitions from a trivial identity ensemble to the uniform Haar ensemble, while the PE transitions from the Haar ensemble to a trivial singleton pure state. In Clifford circuits, absence of non-stabiliserness limits the PE to a $1$-design, while the TE forms a $k$-design for $k \leq 3$. We demonstrate that these genuine ensemble transitions offer a clear physical mechanism for the measurement-induced entanglement or purification transitions, illustrating how the capacity to ergodically steer a quantum subsystem shifts from the temporal boundary measurements in the mixed phase to the bulk trajectory measurements in the pure phase.

### Paper [226]
Title: A general formula for the amplitude-frequency ratio in shaking induced Mott insulator of atomtronic transistors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11355
Authors: Wenxi Lai, Yu-Quan Ma, Yi-Wen Wei
Abstract: Mott insulator of atomic transport can be realized in shaken optical lattices by choosing particular ratio of driving amplitude and frequency, which has been studied as Floquet engineering with time-independent effective Hamiltonian approach. Here, we give a general formula of amplitude-frequency ratio for realization of the shaking induced insulator-conductor transition in a double-well open system, using numerical computation with instantaneous eigenstates approach. The result is owing to the fact that the instantaneous eigenstates approach is applicable in wider parameter range compared with the time-independent effective Hamiltonian approach. Analysis from the results of quantum master equation shows that the insulator effect is originated from coherent localization of atom wave packets in optical wells.

### Paper [227]
Title: An Infinite Two-Parameter Family of Octagonal Tilings
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.04133
Authors: April Lynne D. Say-awen, Sam Coates
Abstract: We construct an infinite two-parameter family of substitution rules with inflation factors $\delta_{(m,n)}=m+n(1+\sqrt{2})$ for integers $m\geq0$ and $n\geq1$, excluding pairs in which $m$ is even and $n$ is odd. Extending four examples from our previous work, we show that each substitution admits a tiling with $8$-fold rotational symmetry and that the associated tilings are repetitive and have finite local complexity. We also classify the inflation factors according to their PV and unimodular properties and illustrate selected cases through perpendicular-space projections and Fourier spectra.

### Paper [228]
Title: Electron--phonon matrix elements for hybrid functionals in the projector augmented-wave method: Eigenvalue projection, nondiagonal supercells, and Wannier interpolation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11013
Authors: Qian Wang, Yu Xie, Yanming Ma
Abstract: We present a finite-displacement approach to electron--phonon matrix elements that requires only eigenvalues and wave functions from the displaced calculations, for both norm-conserving pseudopotentials and the projector augmented-wave (PAW) method. Extending the eigenvalue-projectability approach, previously formulated for norm-conserving pseudopotentials, we reconstruct the displaced Hamiltonian from the displaced eigenvalues and from overlaps evaluated in the PAW metric of each displaced structure. Analytic terms constructed from the PAW dataset then yield the all-electron matrix element, without derivatives of the local potential or of the PAW strengths. The approach therefore applies in principle to any electronic-structure method available in PAW. To reduce the supercell cost, we combine it with nondiagonal supercells that sample the Brillouin zone equivalently to the primitive cell and recover the full matrix elements by crystal symmetry. Implemented in the ARES-EPC code, the method reproduces matrix elements from density-functional perturbation theory (DFPT) and from VASP+phelel for seven materials with mean absolute errors below 0.5 meV. Nondiagonal supercells reproduce the diagonal-supercell results to the same accuracy and reduce the cost of the displaced calculations by up to a factor of 23. Through an interface to EPW, the norm-conserving matrix elements give an electron mobility of Si within 0.9% of that from DFPT. The HSE06 hybrid functional increases the mobility of Si by 13% relative to the PBE functional, because the lighter conduction band and the harder phonons outweigh the stronger coupling.

### Paper [229]
Title: Quantum geometric ferromagnetism by singular saddle point
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01089
Authors: Taisei Kitamura, Hiroki Nakai, Akito Daido, Youichi Yanase
Abstract: We propose ferromagnetism that occurs in electrons at a saddle point with band touching, which we call the singular saddle point. At the singular saddle point, the divergent quantum metric induces ferromagnetic correlation, and the logarithmic divergence of the density of states ensures ferromagnetism within Stoner theory. This is a prototypical example of quantum geometric ferromagnetism. The two-dimensional $t_{2g}$-orbital model accommodates the ferromagnetism by this mechanism, which is continuously connected to the exactly proven flat-band ferromagnetism.

### Paper [230]
Title: Resolving the Quantum Origin of Anisotropic Raman Response in Quasi-1D $Ta_2$$Ni_3$$Te_5$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05809
Authors: Fei Wang, Qiaohui Zhou, Hong Tang, Fan Zhang, Yanxing Li, Steven A. Hindmarsh, Chengkun Xing, Keyuan Bai, Sidra Younus, Ana M. Sanchez, Haidong Zhou, Chih-Kang Shih, Adrienn Ruzsinszky, Xin Lu, Jiang Wei
Abstract: Probing anisotropic electron-phonon interactions (EPIs) is fundamental to understanding low-dimensional quantum materials, yet isolating their intrinsic signatures remains a challenge due to the confounding influence of extrinsic effects. Here, we resolve this ambiguity in quasi-one-dimensional $Ta_2$$Ni_3$$Te_5$ through a combination of angle-resolved polarized Raman spectroscopy and density functional perturbation theory (DFPT). Anomalous four-lobe angular dependences were observed in Ag phonon modes, which deviate from the real-valued Raman tensor prediction. Further, we explicitly highlight that, despite their distinct physical origins, light absorption and birefringence yield mathematically identical angular dependencies in $Ta_2$$Ni_3$$Te_5$, rendering phenomenological fitting alone insufficient to identify the true physical mechanism. By employing a full quantum treatment incorporating detailed interaction mechanisms, we capture the qualitative characteristics and mode-to-mode diversity of the anomalous angular responses observed experimentally. The DFPT calculations further reveal mode-specific and momentum-resolved EPIs, providing microscopic support for anisotropic EPIs as the mechanism of anomalous Raman responses.

### Paper [231]
Title: Continuous symmetry breaking in 1D spin chains and 1+1D field theory
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21540
Authors: Adam Nahum
Abstract: We argue that ground states of 1D spin chains can spontaneously break U(1) ``easy-plane'' spin rotation symmetry, via true long-range order of $(S^x, S^y)$, at the phase transition between two quasi-long-range-ordered phases. The critical point can be reached by tuning a single parameter in a Hamiltonian with the same symmetry as the XXZ model, without further fine-tuning. Equivalently, it can arise in systems of bosons with particle-hole symmetry, as a long-range-ordered transition point between two quasi-long-range-ordered superfluids. Our approach is to start with the continuum field theory of the isotropic Heisenberg ferromagnet and consider generic perturbations that respect easy-plane symmetry. We argue for a renormalization-group flow to a critical point where long-range order in $(S^x, S^y)$ is enabled by coexisting critical fluctuations of $S^z$. (We also discuss multicritical points where further parameters are tuned to zero.) These results show that it is much easier to break continuous symmetries in 1D than standard lore would suggest. The failure of standard intuition for 1D chains (based on the quantum--classical correspondence) can be attributed to Berry phases, which prevent the 1+1D system from mapping to a classical 2D spin model. The present theory also gives an example of an ordered state whose Goldstone mode is interacting even in the infra-red, rather than becoming a free field.

### Paper [232]
Title: Probing tensor monopoles and gerbe invariants in three-dimensional topological matter
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22116
Authors: Wojciech J. Jankowski, Robert-Jan Slager, Giandomenico Palumbo
Abstract: We show that momentum-space tensor monopoles corresponding to nontrivial vector bundle generalizations, known as bundle gerbes, can be realized in bands of three-dimensional topological matter with nontrivial Hopf invariants. We provide a universal construction of tensor Berry connections in these topological phases, demonstrating how obstructions therein lead to $\mathbb{Z}$-quantized bulk magnetoelectric and nonlinear optical phenomena in the presence of spacetime inversion ($\mathcal{PT}$) symmetry. We then pinpoint that these quantum effects are supported by intraband and interband torsion leading to nontrivial Dixmier-Douady classes in most known Hopf phases and in more general topological insulators realizing gerbe invariants falling beyond the tenfold classification of topological phases of matter. We furthermore provide an interacting generalization upon introducing many-body gerbe invariants by employing twisted boundary conditions. This opens an avenue to study gerbe invariants realized through higher-dimensional charge fractionalizations that can be electromagnetically probed.

### Paper [233]
Title: Evidence for time-reversal symmetry breaking and glassy dynamics in superconducting (La,Pr,Sm)3Ni2O7 films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16412
Authors: Haoran Ji, Zheyuan Xie, Yaqi Chen, Guangdi Zhou, Keyi Liang, Rui Liu, Mengqiao Geng, Meixuan Li, Longxin Pan, Heng Wang, Haoliang Huang, Jun Ge, Yi Liu, Yang Liu, Guang-Ming Zhang, Yihua Wang, Ziqiang Wang, Qi-Kun Xue, Zhuoyu Chen, Jian Wang
Abstract: Superconductivity and magnetism are generally distinct and competing orders, characterized by different broken symmetries and order parameters. Although they may coexist in some correlated systems, whether and how these orders can become inherently intertwined in unconventional high-transition-temperature (high-Tc) superconductors remains a central open question. Here, we report time-reversal symmetry (TRS) breaking and glassy dynamics in the superconducting state of nickelate (La,Pr,Sm)3Ni2O7 films. Magnetoresistance hysteresis and zero-field non-reciprocal charge transport, complementary evidence for broken TRS, are observed in the superconducting state, while absent in the normal state. Within the superconducting transition regime, logarithmically slow resistance relaxations following magnetic-field perturbations provide strong evidence for glassy dynamics. Remarkably, the reproducible temperature hierarchy across multiple films reveals that these novel phenomena emerge concurrently with developing superconducting phase coherence, preceding the zero-resistance state. Consistently, scanning superconducting quantum interference device (sSQUID) microscopy directly resolves the zero-field magnetic-flux patterns indicative of spontaneous TRS breaking, which emerge simultaneously with local superconducting diamagnetism and phase stiffness. Our observations suggest a frustrated multicomponent superconducting order parameter in the nickelate films, whose internal relative phases may carry TRS-breaking chirality and freeze into a textured phase glass. Collectively, our findings identify a distinctive form of superconductivity in which internal phase degrees of freedom may support additional broken symmetries and glassy order, offering a new perspective on intertwined ordering phenomena in unconventional high-Tc superconductors.

### Paper [234]
Title: Odd-Parity Altermagnetism Originating from Orbital Order
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18361
Authors: Zheng-Yang Zhuang, Di Zhu, Dongling Liu, Zhigang Wu, Zhongbo Yan
Abstract: Odd-parity spin splitting plays a central role in spintronics and unconventional superconductivity, yet its microscopic realization in collinear magnetic systems remains elusive. We propose a general symmetry-based strategy for realizing odd-parity altermagnetism by stacking two noncentrosymmetric monolayers in an interlayer antiferromagnetic configuration. In this setting, odd-parity spin splitting originates from nonrelativistic complex orbital order rather than spin-orbit coupling. By exploiting lattice symmetries, our framework enables the realization of both $p$- and $f$-wave altermagnets with nontrivial topological properties. Depending on the symmetry principles governing the bilayer stacking, the resulting altermagnet can host either quantum spin Hall or Chern insulator phases. Our work expands the landscape of altermagnetic phases and opens new avenues for discovering fascinating physical phenomena.

### Paper [235]
Title: Multiple Many-Body Localization Transitions in Driven Non-Hermitian Chains with Spatially Correlated Potentials
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14696
Authors: Sanchayan Banerjee, Ayan Banerjee, Tapan Mishra, Flore K. Kunst
Abstract: We investigate the fate of a many-body-localized phase in a periodically driven non-Hermitian chain of hard-core bosons with directed hopping subject to a binary onsite potential in the form of a deterministic quasiperiodic Fibonacci sequence. While Floquet heating generally results in the thermalization of the many-body-localized system with increasing driving period, our initially many-body-localized phase displays a sequence of localization-delocalization transitions, leaving isolated extended islands between many-body-localized phases. Non-Hermiticity makes these extended islands strongly boundary sensitive, and gives rise to a Floquet many-body skin effect under open boundary conditions. We show that the re-entrant many-body localization~(MBL) is independent of our choice of potential by reproducing this phenomenology when instead considering a potential with a genuinely disordered random-dimer sequence. Furthermore, we find that the directed hopping plays a dual role: it stabilizes MBL in regimes that are only weakly localized in the Hermitian limit, while promoting delocalization and broadening extended regions elsewhere. Our work advances the fundamental understanding of non-Hermitian and nonequilibrium quantum matter by revealing how directed hopping and spatial correlations in the onsite landscape jointly govern MBL in periodically driven interacting systems

### Paper [236]
Title: A stochastic heat engine driven using a nonlinear protocol
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15992
Authors: Amrutayani Panda, Biswajit Das, Shuvojit Paul, Arnab Saha, Ayan Banerjee
Abstract: A colloidal particle confined in a time-dependent optical trap can function as a microscopic heat engine, with optimization strategies offering a route to enhanced performance. Here, we numerically investigate a Stirling heat engine operating in passive and active environments using a protocol inspired by Engineered Swift Equilibration (ESE). We analyze various fluctuating parameters throughout the cycle to validate the robustness of the engine, and demonstrate a significant enhancement in performance compared to conventional Stirling engines. Most crucially, we observe that the nonlinear protocol can even transform a heat pump-like operation into a genuine heat engine under strong activity, thereby surpassing bounds imposed on efficiency by high-temperature and quasi-static conditions. In contrast to the standard Stirling protocol, we observe that the nonlinear protocols appear to suppress the finite-time relaxation lag of the system from reaching an instantaneous equilibrium distribution, thereby improving engine performance at short cycle times. An analytical analysis of the variance dynamics where we quantify the relaxation lag between the actual and instantaneous equilibrium states clearly vindicates our understanding about the improved efficacy of the non-linear protocols. The proposed protocol is designed with experimental feasibility in mind, providing a practical route toward efficient microscopic heat engines.

### Paper [237]
Title: Dynamics of $\mathrm{CP}^{N-1}$ skyrmions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17403
Authors: Seungho Lee, Hyojae Jeon, Jung Hoon Han
Abstract: We derive several exact results for the dynamics of CP$^{N-1}$ skyrmions with arbitrary $N$. Fractonic continuity equation is shown to hold for arbitrary CP$^{N-1}$ fluid implying the conservation of the topological charge and the dipole moment. Inclusion of the Gilbert damping modifies the continuity equation, resulting in the violation of the dipole moment conservation but not of the topological charge. Thiele's equation for the CP$^{N-1}$ skyrmion follows from the modified continuity equation. The Girvin-MacDonald-Platzman (GMP) algebra in the long-wavelength limit is derived for arbitrary CP$^{N-1}$ fluid. In the case of CP$^2$ skyrmions, we construct a finite-size skyrmion with dominant ferromagnetic moments. We find that its magnetic dipole texture carries a nonzero CP$^1$ charge equal to half the CP$^2$ skyrmion charge and argue that the topological Hall effect of electrons should exist due to their coupling to the ferromagnetic part of the CP$^2$ texture.

### Paper [238]
Title: Magnetocaloric effect for the altermagnetic candidate MnTe
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02777
Authors: N. N. Orlova, V. D. Esin, A. V. Timonina, N. N. Kolesnikov, E. V. Deviatov
Abstract: We experimentally investigate magnetocaloric effect for single crystals of altermagnetic candidate MnTe at the transition to the state with spontaneous spin polarization, i.e. well below the N\'eel temperature of MnTe. The isothermal magnetic entropy change $\Delta S$ is calculated from the experimental magnetization curves by using Maxwell relation. We observe well-defined magnetocaloric effect as a narrow $\Delta S$ peak around the critical temperature $T_c\approx 81$~K, which reflects the sharp magnetization jump. This behavior is unusual for standard ferromagnetic transitions, so it confirms the predicted spin-orbit-induced spin polarization in the MnTe altermagnetic state and, therefore, confirms MnTe as a prototypical altermagnet candidate.

### Paper [239]
Title: Demon's variational principle for informational active matter
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13145
Authors: Kento Yasuda, Kenta Ishimoto, Shigeyuki Komura
Abstract: The interplay between information, dissipation, and control is reshaping our understanding of thermodynamics in feedback-regulated systems. We develop the informational Onsager-Machlup principle, a generalized variational framework that unifies energetic, dissipative, and informational contributions within a single formalism. This framework introduces a conditioned Onsager-Machlup integral to quantify path entropy under specified memory states and enables the derivation of cumulant generating functions for arbitrary observables in systems with measurement and feedback. Our formulation is consistent with stochastic thermodynamics and information thermodynamics. To demonstrate the utility of the IOMP, we apply it to an information swimmer and show that it systematically reproduces the analytical expressions for the mean velocity and higher-order cumulants obtained by a direct approach, while also enabling the calculation of entropy production in the single-measurement case. For repeated measurements and the steady state, we derive approximate analytical expressions by using a Gaussian closure for the distribution of measured velocities. Our analytical results show good agreement with numerical results, except for cases of extreme drag asymmetry.

### Paper [240]
Title: Equation of state for hard sphere fluids across stable and metastable regimes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05488
Authors: Can Cui, Jianxiang Tian
Abstract: We review selected equations of state (EOSs) for three-dimensional hard-sphere fluids and revise a closed-virial construction to describe stable and metastable disordered states.

### Paper [241]
Title: Predicting Coherent B2 Stability in Ru-Containing Refractory Alloys Through Thermodynamic Elastic Design Maps
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01326
Authors: Avik Mahata
Abstract: The development of next-generation refractory superalloys is fundamentally constrained by competing requirements of high thermodynamic stability and low interfacial energy. Ru-based B2 intermetallics possess strong intrinsic ordering, but their use as coherent reinforcing phases requires balancing formation enthalpy against elastic strain. Here, we present a physics-guided machine-learning framework that integrates high-throughput density functional theory (DFT), Random Forest screening, and symbolic regression to map this design space. The approach addresses the "binary paradox" observed in recent experimental studies, where strongly ordered Ru-refractory compounds do not necessarily achieve the highest solvus temperatures. The resulting closed-form relation, T_solvus ~ 0.11(Delta H/Delta S) - 20000|delta|, quantifies the competing effects of thermodynamic stabilization and lattice misfit. Because the electronic-structure calculations are static 0 K calculations, the relation is treated as an effective calibrated design equation rather than a directly calculated finite-temperature free-energy boundary. The model is benchmarked against published B2 solvus measurements, while calculated formation enthalpies and lattice parameters are independently compared with calorimetric and diffraction data. Within the fitted relation, a 1% lattice misfit corresponds to an effective solvus penalty of approximately 200 degrees C. Al/Si substitution further shows that improved lattice matching is accompanied by reduced thermodynamic stabilization, with zero misfit near 11 at.% Al and 7 at.% Si for Nb75V25.

### Paper [242]
Title: Manipulating anomalous transport via crystal symmetry in two-dimensional altermagnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01564
Authors: Dan Li, Shuaiyu Wang, Jiabin chen, Zeling Li, Chaoxi Cui, Lei Li, Lei Wang, Zhi-Ming Yu, Xiaodong Zhou, Xiao-Ping Li
Abstract: Anomalous transports, including the anomalous Hall effect (AHE) and anomalous Nernst effect (ANE), are typical manifestations of time-reversal-symmetry-breaking responses in materials. In general, the two Hall states with opposite Hall conductivities can be regarded as time-reversal pairs coupled to magnetic order, and switching between them relies on reversing the magnetization via an external magnetic field or electric current. Here, we introduce an approach for manipulating anomalous transport through crystal symmetry engineering in two-dimensional (2D) altermagnetic systems. Through symmetry analysis and lattice models, we show that for systems with out-of-plane N\'eel vectors, breaking the symmetry that connects two magnetic sublattices will induce an anomalous Hall response. Crucially, the signs of the anomalous Hall and anomalous Nernst conductivities can be flexibly controlled via symmetry-breaking perturbations, enabling the realizing tunable sign-reversible anomalous transport. Using first-principles calculations, we investigate crystal-symmetry-controlled anomalous transport in the 2D altermagnetic materials V$_{2}$STeO and Cr$_{2}$O$_{2}$. The results indicate that these systems with out-of-plane N\'eel vectors exhibit pronounced AHE and ANE signals under uniaxial strain. Remarkably, the sign of the anomalous transport coefficients can be reversed by simply rotating the strain direction by $\pi/2$, thus enabling the switching of anomalous transverse currents without an external magnetic field. Our findings provide a new strategy to manipulate anomalous transport, and should have significant potential applications.

### Paper [243]
Title: Magnon equilibrium spin current in collinear antiferromagnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16184
Authors: Vladimir A. Zyuzin
Abstract: We theoretically predict that the Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction can induce a magnon equilibrium spin current in collinear antiferromagnets. Such a current, being a response to the effective magnon vector potential, can be considered the magnon analog of the supercurrent in superconductors. The large amplitude of the predicted effect may compensate for the smallness of the Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction, making the equilibrium spin currents experimentally observable. Importantly, we show that the current being the ground state property isn't zero at zero temperature. We suggest that an external electric field can play the role of an effective flux that magnons interact with, and we propose an experiment based on the interference of magnons in a ring geometry to verify the concept.

### Paper [244]
Title: When does a lattice higher-form symmetry flow to a topological higher-form symmetry at low energies?
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20935
Authors: Ruizhi Liu, Pok Man Tam, Ho Tat Lam, Liujun Zou
Abstract: We study the lattice version of higher-form symmetries on tensor-product Hilbert spaces. Interestingly, at low energies, these symmetries may not flow to the topological higher-form symmetries familiar from relativistic quantum field theories, but instead to non-topological higher-form symmetries. We present concrete lattice models exhibiting this phenomenon. One particular model is an $\mathbb{R}$ generalization of the Kitaev honeycomb model featuring an $\mathbb{R}$ lattice 1-form symmetry. We show that its low-energy effective field theory is a gapless, non-relativistic theory with a non-topological $\mathbb{R}$ 1-form symmetry. In both the lattice model and the effective field theory, we demonstrate that the non-topological $\mathbb{R}$ 1-form symmetry is not robust against local perturbations. In contrast, we also study various modifications of the toric code and their low-energy effective field theories to demonstrate that the compact $\mathbb{Z}_2$ lattice 1-form symmetry does become topological at low energies unless the Hamiltonian is fine-tuned. Along the way, we clarify the rules for constructing low-energy effective field theories in the presence of multiple superselection sectors. Finally, we argue on general grounds that non-compact higher-form symmetries (such as $\mathbb{R}$ and $\mathbb{Z}$ 1-form symmetries) in lattice systems generically remain non-topological at low energies, whereas compact higher-form symmetries (such as $\mathbb{Z}_{n}$ and $U(1)$ 1-form symmetries) generically become topological.

### Paper [245]
Title: Non-Equilibrium Quantum Many-Body Physics with Quantum Circuits
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22375
Authors: Bruno Bertini
Abstract: These are the notes for the 4.5-hour course with the same title that I delivered in August 2025 at the Les Houches summer school ``Exact Solvability and Quantum Information''. In these notes I pedagogically introduce the setting of brickwork quantum circuits and show that it provides a useful framework to study non-equilibrium quantum many-body dynamics in the presence of local interactions. I first show that brickwork quantum circuits evolve quantum correlations in a way that is fundamentally similar to local Hamiltonians, and then present examples of brickwork quantum circuits where, surprisingly, one can compute exactly several relevant dynamical and spectral properties in the presence of non-trivial interactions.

### Paper [246]
Title: Confinement Limits Cell Shape and Mitotic Pressure During Division
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18909
Authors: Amit Singh Vishen
Abstract: Cells often divide under mechanical confinement, where surrounding structures restrict shape changes during cytokinesis. We analyze a minimal model of a dividing cell as an incompressible volume bounded by an interface with effective isotropic tension, and show that confinement imposes a minimum attainable furrow radius. Beyond this limit, the cytokinetic ring can continue to contract within a septum without further furrow ingression, leaving the cell shape, mitotic pressure, and transmitted force unchanged. We determine this confinement-limited state for rigid parallel walls, soft axial confinement, and three-dimensional enclosures. Between parallel walls, the limiting force and minimum furrow radius collapse onto universal curves when scaled by surface tension and cell size. In soft confinement, environmental stiffness and spindle forces select the operating point. In three-dimensional confinement, the free volume between the cell and the confinement controls force transmission and cell shape. This yields a method for inferring the mechanical state of a cell dividing in deformable three-dimensional confinement from its shape. The radius and tangent angle of the cell surface at the stalled furrow and at the point where the cell detaches from its surroundings determine the mitotic pressure and the axial force carried by the cell surface, relative to the cortical tension.

### Paper [247]
Title: peapods: A Rust-Accelerated Monte Carlo Package for Ising and XY Spin Systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.19045
Authors: Yan Ru Pei
Abstract: We present peapods, an open-source Python package for Monte Carlo simulation of classical spin models with arbitrary couplings, whose sampling loop runs in Rust. It samples Ising models on periodic Bravais lattices given by user-specified neighbor offsets, optionally with open or antiperiodic boundaries, and XY models with signed couplings and site dilution on hypercubic lattices. Besides Metropolis and heat-bath sweeps, Swendsen-Wang and Wolff cluster updates and parallel tempering, it provides a family of replica cluster moves for spin glasses: the Houdayer, J\"org and Chayes-Machta-Redner moves, multi-pair variants of the first two, replica-permutation moves and a gauged form of them, and Swendsen-Wang replica Monte Carlo across temperatures. The same-temperature moves share one construction, a symmetry of the replicas' joint energy applied on clusters, and one-step stationarity tests from exact Boltzmann samples on small lattices check each family of moves that satisfies detailed balance. The package measures overlap distributions, spin-glass correlation lengths, autocorrelation times, equilibration and tempering-flow diagnostics, and jackknife errors over disorder samples. It reproduces the exact critical temperatures of the square and triangular Ising lattices, and its Binder curves for simple-cubic, BCC and FCC lattices agree at the literature critical temperatures. For the three-dimensional $\pm J$ spin glass, the crossings of $\xi_{\mathrm{SG}}/L$ for $L \le 12$ extrapolate, with the Janus correction exponent held fixed, to $T_c = 1.085(42)$, consistent with the Janus value $1.1019(29)$. For the two-dimensional XY model, the helicity modulus, correlation length and structure factor agree with high-precision reference data within $1.9$ standard errors. This article describes version 0.3.0.

### Paper [248]
Title: Boundary-Sculpted Arctic Surface in the Cubic Ice Model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.24051
Authors: Benjamin Canals
Abstract: In square ice, domain-wall boundaries freeze the corners behind an arctic curve; strings of reversed arrows, unable to pass each other, give it Tracy-Widom fluctuations. In 3D, they pass around one another. Monte Carlo simulations calibrated on exact 2D results show that boundaries still sculpt an arctic surface in cubic ice, enclosing, by a lower bound, 3% of the box at the largest size. If local equilibrium holds up to it, it lies where the local field reaches the bulk Kasteleyn threshold. Its fluctuations are not Tracy-Widom along lines, and much closer than in 2D to independent strings.

### Paper [249]
Title: Digitally Optimized Initializations for Fast Thermodynamic Computing
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24183
Authors: Mattia Moroder, Felix C. Binder, John Goold
Abstract: Thermodynamic computing harnesses the relaxation dynamics of physical systems to perform matrix operations. A key limitation of such approaches is the often long thermalization time required for the system to approach equilibrium with sufficient accuracy. Here, we introduce a hybrid digital-thermodynamic algorithm that substantially accelerates relaxation through optimized initializations inspired by the Mpemba effect. In the proposed scheme, a classical digital processor efficiently computes an initialization that suppresses slow relaxation modes, after which the physical system performs the remaining computation through its intrinsic relaxation dynamics. We focus on overdamped Langevin dynamics for quadratic energy landscapes, analyzing the spectral structure of the associated Fokker-Planck operator and identifying the corresponding optimal initial covariances. This yields a predictable reduction in thermalization time, determined by the spectrum of the encoded matrix. We derive analytic expressions for the resulting speedups and numerically analyze thermodynamic implementations of matrix inversion and determinant computation as concrete examples. Our results show that optimized initialization protocols provide a simple and broadly applicable route to accelerating thermodynamic computations.

### Paper [250]
Title: Interfacial charge-transfer in 3d/5d complex oxide heterostructures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25514
Authors: Arun Kumar Jaiswal, Di Wang, Ji Soo Lim, Shruti Roy, Fabrice Wilhelm, Vanessa Wollersen, Andrei Rogalev, Matthieu Le Tacon, Dirk Fuchs
Abstract: Interfacial charge transfer (ICT) provides a powerful route to engineer electronic phases in correlated oxide heterostructures, yet predictive design principles remain elusive. Here, we systematically investigate superlattices composed of the 5d spin orbit coupled semimetal SrIrO3 and a series of correlated 3d perovskites (LaMnO3, LaFeO3, and LaCoO3), thereby establishing a quantitative framework for ICT across 3d/5d interfaces. Combining Ir L2,3 edge X ray absorption spectroscopy with spatially resolved electron energy loss spectroscopy, we estimate the electron depletion in the SrIrO3 layers and the corresponding electron accumulation in the 3d layers. The magnitude of ICT follows the trend expected from electronegativity mismatch, supporting electronegativity driven band alignment as the dominant contribution in this series. Beyond interfacial doping, we find that strong 3d,5d hybridization induces a low spin to high spin conversion in the cobaltate layers, hinting to interface controlled spin state engineering without chemical substitution. These results establish electronegativity mismatch as a predictive design parameter for correlated oxide interfaces and provide a materials platform for tailoring band filling, orbital hierarchy, and spin configurations in quantum oxide heterostructures, paving the way towards advanced oxide electronics and next-generation information technologies.

### Paper [251]
Title: The Mpemba effect likes to hit a wall
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01543
Authors: Yue Liu, Tan Van Vu, Rapha\"el Ch\'etrite, Fr\'ed\'eric van Wijland, Hisao Hayakawa
Abstract: The original Mpemba effect refers to the counterintuitive possibility that a system prepared at a higher temperature relaxes to a colder equilibrium faster than one prepared at a lower temperature. In microscopic classical realizations, this phenomenon has been studied for overdamped particles in external potentials and observed experimentally. We show that, for a particle in an asymmetric polynomial double-well potential, the one-dimensional Mpemba effect is controlled not by the double-well structure or metastability itself, but by the high-energy confinement: a hard boundary, or more generally a sufficiently steep confining tail, on the shallow side can induce the required population inversion. For the polynomial potentials considered here, the effect disappears when both hard boundaries are taken to infinity, whereas sufficiently asymmetric soft tails can play the same role as walls. We identify the underlying mechanism as a redistribution of the initial equilibrium population when it begins to probe the high-energy tails of the potential, a picture that also extends to single-well and symmetric double-well potentials.

### Paper [252]
Title: Quasi-1D Planar Magnetic Topological Heterostructure
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01823
Authors: Z. Z. Alisultanov
Abstract: We theoretically introduce a quasi one dimensional magnetic heterostructure of alternating 2D topological and normal insulator strips. Such a structure can be realized by special fabrication techniques or may emerge naturally in twisted bilayers of certain transition metal dichalcogenides. Its low-energy physics is governed by a hybrid Hamiltonian intertwining the Su-Schrieffer-Heeger and Shockley models, with spin-momentum locking and local Zeeman splitting. Symmetry analysis places it in class AIII, characterized by chiral symmetry and a $\mathbb{Z}$ topological invariant. Computing the winding number from the block-off-diagonal structure of the Hamiltonian reveals topological phases characterized by invariants $\nu = 0$, $1$, and $2$. Furthermore, a single magnetic defect acts as a sensitive local probe, whose in-gap spectrum provides a spectroscopic fingerprint to distinguish global topological phases. We show that the frequency-dependent optical absorption induced by such a defect exhibits distinct features in the topological and trivial phases, offering an independent, experimentally accessible local indicator of the global topological order. Extending the platform to a multilayer geometry uncovers a nonsymmorphic projective symmetry that gives rise to M\"obius band topology.

### Paper [253]
Title: Neural and Tensor-Network Representations of $\mathbb{Z}_N$ Cluster and Dipolar-cluster SPT States
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06741
Authors: Seungho Lee, Daesik Kim, Hyun-Yong Lee, Jung Hoon Han
Abstract: The $\mathbb Z_N$ cluster state is a paradigmatic example of symmetry-protected topological (SPT) order with $\mathbb Z_N\times\mathbb Z_N$ symmetry. We express its wavefunction and those of dipolar cluster states as a projector-based realization of correlator product states, which we dub the $P$-representation. The local projector $P$, also known as the copy tensor, can have multiple legs connecting a site to neighboring sites depending on the model and its deformations. We construct explicit wavefunctions of cluster and dipolar cluster states under deformation using the $P$-representation. The transition to the neural quantum state representations of the same state occurs by splitting the interaction matrices between $\mathbb{Z}_N$ spins into a pair of weight matrices. We derive these weight matrices explicitly for the cluster and dipolar cluster states, thus generalizing previous works on $\mathbb{Z}_2$ cluster states. The projector scheme then leads to the tensor product state (TPS) representation of the cluster-state wavefunctions. We benchmark the resulting TPS construction against conventional MPS representation using density-matrix renormalization group simulations and argue that the TPS could offer efficient representations for some modulated and deformed SPT states.

### Paper [254]
Title: Effect of subcritical fluid shear flow on granular bed strength
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14936
Authors: Dong Wang, Sophie Bodek, Nicholas T. Ouellette, Mark D. Shattuck, Corey S. O'Hern
Abstract: Interactions between fluids and granular materials are prevalent on the Earth's surface. In the case of fluid flow over a sediment bed, the fluid imparts a shear stress to the granular materials. When the applied shear stress is above a critical value, the grains become entrained in the fluid flow. Prior experimental studies have shown that granular beds subjected to a subcritical fluid flow can strengthen in the same direction as the subcritical flow. In contrast, granular beds can become weaker in the direction opposite to the subcritical fluid flow. To investigate the grain-scale mechanisms that control directional strengthening and weakening, we perform discrete element method (DEM) simulations of granular beds subjected to model fluid flows in two (2D) and three (3D) dimensions with varied inter-particle static friction coefficients and conditioning flow speeds. In these studies, the subcritical grain motion does not cause significant bed compaction. Instead, we find that the strength of a granular bed in a particular direction is highly correlated with the fraction of {\it surface} grains that can be dislodged by a fluid force applied in that direction. Further, the anisotropic bed strength only persists over a finite time scale that is set by the Shields number. We also show that inter-particle static friction is not required for bed strength anisotropy, but varying the friction affects the magnitude of the anisotropy. This research enhances the grain-scale understanding of erosion of granular beds caused by fluid flows and underscores the importance of tracking the history of the fabric of the bed surface since it couples strongly to bed strength.

### Paper [255]
Title: Giant spontaneous Kerr effect and its self-doping dependence in altermagnetic MnTe
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21021
Authors: Weitung Yang, Choongjae Won, Cory Cress, Weihang Lu, Xiaochen Fang, Shelby Fields, Nicholas Combs, Shaofeng Han, Marshall Zachary Franklin, Steven P. Bennett, Sang-Wook Cheong, Jing Xia
Abstract: Altermagnetism, a third class of collinear magnetism with spin-split bands and vanishing net magnetization, has emerged in hexagonal {\alpha}-MnTe, a promising platform for ultrafast, stray-field-free spintronics. Whether MnTe's macroscopic symmetry-breaking signatures reflect ideal altermagnetic order or are activated by defects remains open. Here we report giant spontaneous Kerr rotations of up to 1500 rad in {\alpha}-MnTe single crystals at the telecommunication wavelength of 1550 nm, onsetting precisely at the N\'eel temperature TN = 307 K. The signal appears in disjoint macroscopic patches whose amplitude tracks the sample conductivity, and a hole-conducting {\alpha}-MnTe film reproduces this response together with an anomalous Hall effect. Within a single crystal, Kerr-active regions are optically distinct from their Kerr-silent surroundings in co-registered reflection maps. These observations indicate that carrier self-doping, rather than ideal altermagnetic order alone, governs the giant magneto-optical response, and establish telecom-wavelength Kerr imaging as a practical readout for altermagnetic spintronics.

### Paper [256]
Title: Topological antiqued mechanical toy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27554
Authors: Hirofumi Wada, Hayato Mizobata, Shuto Ueno, Taiju Yoneda
Abstract: {\it Jacob's ladder} -- a classic children's toy -- is a simple mechanical frame comprising rigid blocks connected by strings that shows curious unidirectional flipping waves. Nonetheless, its physical origin remains elusive. By combining experiment, numeral simulation, and theory, we show that understanding the underlying design principle of this toy requires diverse physical ideas. First, we conduct a water-tank experiment that excludes the collision-based mechanism, thus defying widespread expectations. Subsequently, we analytically demonstrate that the toy is bistable under gravity, thus implying its kink wave as a class of topological solitons. The waves are surprisingly reminiscent -- both experimentally and theoretically -- to those in the Kane--Lubensky topological chain, owing to the stiffening of zero modes by the pretension under gravity. However, a close examination based on the index theorem reveals that the similarity remains superficial and that the floppiness of the toy underlies the kink and antikink coexistence -- a forbidden mode in the topological chain. By analyzing a generalized asymmetric toy, we reveal that its symmetric connection renders it topologically singular, thus resulting in amusing motions. We demonstrate these ideas by experimentally observing a dramatic pair annihilation of kink and antikink waves.

### Paper [257]
Title: Optimization of current-based sensing of phonon temperature using a finite reservoir
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.28155
Authors: Sindre Brattegard, Stephanie Matern, Mark T. Mitchison, Saulo V. Moreira
Abstract: In realistic nanoscale transport set-ups, electron-phonon coupling leads to the exchange of heat between phonon baths and electronic reservoirs with finite heat capacities. Such exchange affects the finite reservoir's temperature. However, this sensitivity of the finite reservoir temperature to the exchange of heat with the finite reservoir has remained unexplored for thermometry. Here, we fill this gap by combining current metrology techniques with a thermodynamic framework encompassing finite reservoirs. We focus on an experimentally realizable set-up with a quantum dot coupled to a finite reservoir and consider two distinct current-based strategies in the long time limit, namely monitoring quanta exchanged between the quantum dot and finite reservoir and the measurement of the total current flowing from the quantum dot into an infinite reservoir. A third strategy involves measurements of the quantum dot occupation. For a large but finite reservoir, we show that the Fisher information for all three strategies captures the finite reservoir's contribution to sensitivity through common factors. We also demonstrate that monitoring quanta exchanged between the system and finite reservoir in the long time limit achieves optimal precision. Finally, we provide an optimization analysis that explores how maximal precision can be achieved within each of the current-based strategies by tuning the gate voltage.

### Paper [258]
Title: Hidden Quantum-Geometric Connection between Injection and Shift Currents
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08643
Authors: Mohammad Yahyavi, Tay-Rong Chang, Md Shafayat Hossain, Arun Bansil, Naoto Nagaosa, Guoqing Chang
Abstract: Injection current (IC) and shift current (SC) are generally regarded as distinct nonlinear optical responses with different microscopic origins. Here, within the clean limit, based on an independent-particle interband description, we uncover a general hidden connection between the IC and SC responses. The generalized-derivative sum rule is used to show that the SC response separates into an injection-determined contribution associated with velocity asymmetry and a genuine shift-current (GSC) contribution associated with real-space wave-packet displacement. In systems with approximately linear electronic dispersion near the Fermi level, the corresponding tensor combinations of SC and IC can become effectively equivalent at low photon energies, governed by the same interband quantum-geometric dipole. Crystal symmetry can also eliminate the injection-determined contribution, enabling direct access to the GSC component. Extending our approach to second-harmonic generation reveals analogous response relations, suggesting a route toward a broader network of quantitative connections among the various nonlinear optical responses.

### Paper [259]
Title: Microscopic origin of Boson peak in two-dimensional scalar spring network
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09436
Authors: Cunyuan Jiang
Abstract: We proposed a non-analytic model to explain the microscopic origin of the anomalous vibrational density of states (DOS), the Boson peak (BP), in amorphous solids based on the scalar dynamical matrix of a network with springs and nodes. We argue that disorder can be classified into two factors: fluctuation of spring strength and fluctuation of coordination numbers (the number of springs connected to a node). The results suggest that BP originates from fluctuation of coordination numbers, while the fluctuation of spring strength only contributes to the effect of damping and has very limited effect on low frequency DOS. This work converts complexity into simplicity and provides a direct answer to the puzzle of the microscopic origin of BP in amorphous solids.

### Paper [260]
Title: Exact Fixed-Point Constraints in Neural-ODEs: Injecting Prior Knowledge into the Network Parameters
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10613
Authors: Feliciano Giuseppe Pacifico, Duccio Fanelli, Lorenzo Buffoni, Lorenzo Chicchi, Diego Febbe, Raffaele Marino
Abstract: Neural ordinary differential equations (Neural-ODEs) learn a velocity field from data. Often, however, additional information on the target field is available, such as the location of some of its equilibria or, more generally, its values at given points. We introduce a method to inject this information directly into the parameters of a Neural-ODE, so that a finite set of prescribed fixed points holds exactly throughout training, with no penalty terms and no post-hoc projection. The output matrix is parametrized through the Moore-Penrose pseudoinverse, implemented by a thin QR factorization of the hidden features at the prescribed points. This splits the output matrix into a component determined by the constraints and a free one, which shapes the field away from the prescribed points without altering its values there. As a result, the constrained Neural-ODE remains a universal approximator of the continuous vector fields compatible with the constraints. We test the method on a two-dimensional Lotka-Volterra competition model with four equilibria. In vector field regression, the constrained model keeps the fixed-point residual at single-precision round-off, and its test error is up to about five times lower than that of an unconstrained network of the same architecture. A soft penalty on the equilibria lowers the residual only at the cost of accuracy, and still leaves it at least an order of magnitude above that of the constrained model. Trained to convergence on datasets of increasing size, the unconstrained and penalized networks saturate already on small datasets, while the constrained model keeps improving over a wider range. Close to the equilibria and over long time horizons, the constrained model is the most accurate at every dataset size considered. This also holds when the models are trained on observed trajectories, by backpropagation through the ODE solver.

### Paper [261]
Title: Spontaneous duality breaking and the invertibility of non-invertible symmetries
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13363
Authors: Jos\'e Dupont, Jasper van Wezel
Abstract: Alongside symmetry and topology, duality is used as an organizing principle throughout modern physics. Dualities are, however, often not exact and, consequently, implemented by a non-invertible operator, giving rise to a non-invertible symmetry at self-dual quantum critical points. Considering the specific example of the transverse-field Ising model, an archetype for both (self-)duality and non-invertible symmetry, we show that its duality can be made exact by a local adjustment of the boundary conditions. In the modified model with exact duality, the symmetry emerging at the quantum critical point is invertible. Moreover, we find that the exact duality necessitates the presence of an anomalous edge degree of freedom, thus realizing a duality-based rather than topology-based bulk-boundary correspondence. The exactness of the duality furthermore seems to suggest that the spontaneous breaking of a global symmetry can also be described as the spontaneous breakdown of a local dual symmetry. We show that this apparent contradiction of Elitzur's theorem can be resolved by considering the required symmetry-breaking fields. This moreover leads to the concept of spontaneous duality breaking, in which arbitrarily small interactions with an embedding environment distinguish between mathematically equivalent dual partners, in direct analogy to the spontaneous breakdown of ordinary symmetries. We argue that both duality-based edge modes and spontaneous duality breaking emerge generally across systems in which a local-global duality can be made exact. Together, these phenomena introduce a conceptually novel interplay between duality, symmetry and topology, that is applicable across diverse areas of physics.

### Paper [262]
Title: Evaluation of External Magnetic Flux Density in Piezo-Flexomagnetic Nanobeams Using a Hybrid 1D-2D Finite Element Framework
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19422
Authors: Lala Samprit Ray, Bishweshwar Babu
Abstract: Most theoretical models of piezo-flexomagnetic nanostructures forcibly impose magnetic isolation at the structure-air interface, neglecting the external magnetic domain entirely. This mathematical compromise limits the model's capability to account for stray-field behavior in non-contact applications. To overcome this gap, our study proposes a fully coupled hybrid finite element formulation combining a 1D Timoshenko beam model with a 2D magneto-static problem, encompassing both the beam body and the surrounding air domain. By continuously mapping 1D structural kinematics into the 2D space to define the mechanically induced magnetic flux, and subsequently integrating the resulting 2D scalar potential back into 1D static generalized magnetic loads, this framework enhances the capability of the simple 1D beam model. The results present the distribution of an external magnetic flux in piezo-flexomagnetic structures and identify the material parameters driving this. Ultimately, the proposed computational method extends the boundary of a 1D beam model to an infinite-domain multiphysics environment.

### Paper [263]
Title: Finite-temperature micromagnetic model bridging atomic- and macro-scale magnetism
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29617
Authors: R. Kiefe, J. S. Amaral
Abstract: A multi-scale finite-temperature micromagnetic model is presented, based on the Landau-Lifshitz equation and the Bernoulli differential equation. This model accurately reproduces classic Maxwell magnetostatics of paramagnets for high temperatures and accurately reproduces standard micromagnetics described by the conventional Landau-Lifshitz model in ferromagnets. The Landau-Lifshitz-Bernoulli (LLBe) model can, by design, directly couple atomic-scale simulations with micromagnetics and output consistent predictions of bulk magnetic properties at finite temperatures, from below to above the material's Curie temperature. The LLBe model is validated against established solvers: MUMAX3 for zero-temperature micromagnetics, and FEMCE for high-temperature classic magnetostatics. We present an application of the LLBe model by simulating Heat-Assisted magnetic recording on a thin magnetic track with local heating, demonstrating the multi-scale finite-temperature capabilities of the LLBe.

### Paper [264]
Title: Predicting the conditions for observing the Mpemba effect
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03445
Authors: Yue Liu, Tan Van Vu, Rapha\"el Ch\'etrite, Fr\'ed\'eric van Wijland, Hisao Hayakawa
Abstract: The Mpemba effect, a counterintuitive phenomenon where a hotter system relaxes faster than a colder one, has been widely observed in various nonequilibrium systems. Despite this progress, the fundamental structural features of the energy landscape required for its emergence remain a subject of debate. In this study, we investigate the conditions for the Mpemba effect within one-dimensional overdamped Langevin dynamics. We classify the potential landscapes based on the presence of single or double wells, their symmetry properties, and the existence of walls. We establish that the existence of the effect is primarily driven by the presence of boundaries, either hard or soft, rather than the specific internal structure of the potential landscape, such as metastability or the number of minima. By employing a spectral decomposition of the Fokker-Planck operator, we analyze the behavior of the first nontrivial eigenmode and demonstrate that its derivative acts as a Dirac delta peak in the low-temperature regime. This helps us elucidate the mechanism underlying the Mpemba effect: it appears as the interplay between this behavior and the initial population dynamics in a non-trivial way induced by the presence of the wall. Our analysis provides a unified classification across single- and double-well potentials, highlighting the crucial role of boundary conditions and asymmetry. Furthermore, we demonstrate that this framework allows for the engineering of potential landscapes capable of producing multistage Mpemba transitions.

### Paper [265]
Title: Atomic-scale visualization of the toroidal order in a trimeric Dy(III) single-molecule toroic
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10077
Authors: Michael J. Jenkins, Madalynn G. Marshall, Jie Xing, Muthu Satheeshkumar, Brandon D. Watson-Sanders, Xiaoping Wang, Christina M. Hoffmann, Gopalan Rajaraman, Huibo Cao, Rongying Jin, Zi-Ling Xue
Abstract: Single-molecule toroics (SMTs) offer a unique platform for next-generation quantum devices utilizing head-to-tail spin alignments in the compounds. Presence of toroidal moments in SMTs has been essentially based on magnetometry and ab initio calculations. Here, we report observation and probe of the toroidal moment in [Dy3(OH)(teaH2)3(paa)3]Cl(OMe) [teaH3: triethanolamine; paaH: N-(2-pyridyl)-acetoacetamide] from mapping of Dy3+ magnetic susceptibility tensors by polarized neutron diffraction (PND). Neutron diffraction under variable magnetic fields demonstrates field-induced magnetization along the c-axis with toroidal moments anti-parallelly stacked, providing definite proof of the toroidal moment. Magnetometry studies confirm the toroidal ground state. For the first time, the combined use of PND, variable-field neutron diffraction, ab initio calculations, and magnetometry is introduced as a robust and quantitative methodology to probe molecular-scale toroidal magnetism. This integrated approach overcomes limitations of earlier indirect methods, establishes a benchmark framework for investigating SMTs, and provides valuable insights for the design of molecular quantum materials.

### Paper [266]
Title: Dynamics of repeated BEC formation and extraction in dimple traps
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11428
Authors: Kyrylo Kovalchuk, Dominik Pfeiffer, Ludwig Lind, Mark Edwards, Alexander Yakimenko, Gerhard Birkl
Abstract: We investigate repeated Bose-Einstein-condensate (BEC) formation and extraction in a dimple trap embedded in a reservoir of thermal atoms using a kinetic model. The model includes pulsed extraction, evaporation, three-body losses, and thermal-atom replenishment. Three extraction protocols are compared: extraction of all atoms from the dimple (BEC and thermal atoms), full and partial extractions of the BEC, but not of the thermal atoms. Residual atoms in the dimple after extraction seed subsequent Bose-stimulated growth and reduce the recovery time between extractions, but also enhance density-dependent losses. For all protocols, repeated extraction of BECs can be achieved without replenishment, but the number of BEC formations is limited by reservoir depletion and heating. With continuous replenishment, the system can reach a periodic steady-state regime, after an initial transient period, controlled by the externally imposed rates of extraction pulses and thermal-atom input. Within the explored parameter range, partial BEC extraction gives the highest efficiency, particularly for short extraction periods and high input rates. These results identify seeding by residual populations of BECs and thermal atoms as a kinetic mechanism for improving repeated condensate production in dimple traps.

### Paper [267]
Title: Nonlocal fractional Kardar-Parisi-Zhang dynamics of grain boundaries
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21903
Authors: Kai Zhao
