Abstract: The kinetic roughening of driven grain boundaries (GBs) is mediated by the collective behavior of disconnections, whose long-range 1/r elastic interactions distinguish them from conventional growing surfaces. Large-scale molecular dynamics simulations show that the roughness of driven GBs undergoes a sharp crossover as the applied strain increases, with the Hurst exponent (H) increasing from approximately 0.33 to 0.8. We develop a nonlocal fractional Kardar-Parisi-Zhang (fKPZ) theory to describe this low-temperature behavior, where the 1/r nonlocal elasticity behaves as the fractional relaxation of the GB morphology. The results show that the spatially-correlated noise associated with disconnection avalanches interacts with the KPZ nonlinearity to produce the post-crossover roughening observed in atomistic simulations. The post-crossover roughness exponent (H ~ 0.8) is comparable to values reported for dynamic fracture, suggesting the underlying physics shared by the collective behavior of defects (e.g., disconnections, dislocations) and interfacial roughening.

### Paper [268]
Title: Nonlinear Static Screening of Positive Charges in an Electron Gas: Contact Hartree Potential
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23065
Authors: M. Sherafati, G. Rodway-Gant, A. X. Chen
Abstract: We investigate nonlinear static screening of a proton in a homogeneous electron gas, focusing on the induced density and contact Hartree potential $V_{\rm H}(0)$, an $r$-weighted moment of the entire screening cloud rather than a strictly local quantity. Thomas--Fermi, RPA, and local-field-corrected linear response are compared with nonlinear DFT-LDA and DFT-PBE calculations. The Estreicher--Meier LDA density fit reproduces the contact Hartree potential closely and resolves its core and Friedel contributions, despite an approximate cusp. Kaplan--Kukkonen and Corradini--Del Sole--Onida--Palummo local-field factors give nearly identical contact predictions for the states examined, without implying general insensitivity to local-field corrections. We extend the variable-phase approach with a companion amplitude equation to reconstruct the quantal induced density from bound and continuum states of constrained screened potentials. Friedel-constrained Yukawa, hydrogenic, and Hulth\'en potentials satisfy the same total-screening condition but yield different contact densities and Hartree potentials. In the two-parameter Apagyi--Nagy construction, the cusp constrains an auxiliary Poisson density, whereas the reconstructed quantum density obeys the Coulomb cusp automatically; neither the Friedel sum rule nor the cusp uniquely fixes the screening cloud. Model-potential contact results approach nonlinear DFT toward high density but deviate increasingly as $r_s$ grows. By contrast, density-informed Popovi\'c--Stott potentials reproduce historical Hartree-only and exchange--correlation-modified contact values within $0.05\%$ without refitting. These findings distinguish global scattering constraints, local cusp behavior, and full-density information, and establish a one-center baseline for future two-center screening calculations.

### Paper [269]
Title: Scaling limit of the Random Language Model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28105
Authors: Eric De Giuli
Abstract: We develop a quantitative theory of the Random Language Model (RLM), an ensemble of stochastic context-free grammars, in a scaling limit where the number of hidden symbols $N \to \infty$ while the grammar temperature $\tilde\epsilon_d \to 0$ at fixed $x = \tilde\epsilon_d \log N$. In this limit, the model admits a controlled description based on a large-deviation principle over rule-usage patterns. A semi-annealed approximation maps the problem to a class of Random Energy Models with nontrivial combinatorics.
  We show that the RLM exhibits a freezing transition at a critical value $x_c=1/8$, below which rule usage condenses on a subset of preferred rules and language statistics acquire a nontrivial dependence on corpus length. A second characteristic scale at $x=1/2$, associated with the breakdown of Gaussian fluctuations, separates transition and babbling regimes. Across regimes, we derive explicit scaling laws for the number of distinct rules, entropy, and energy, identifying distinct frozen and critical regimes controlled by the interplay of grammar size, corpus length, and temperature.
  The theory resolves previous ambiguities regarding the existence of a thermodynamic transition and explains the slow approach to the large-$N$ limit as a consequence of the dependence on $\log N$. It further provides a unified framework in which universal statistical properties of language emerge from typical realizations of generative grammars, with implications for both natural language statistics and the behavior of large language models. Heaps' law and Zipf's law are both derived, and the dependence of predictive entropy on context length is rationalized. Comparison with empirical measurements of Heaps' and Zipf's laws yields parameter estimates for real languages.

### Paper [270]
Title: Near itinerancy and slow singlet formation in the triangular lattice NaRuO2
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05287
Authors: Charles C. Tam, Alon Hendler Avidor, Pritam Bhattacharyya, Yongseong Choi, Daniel Haskel, Sven Luther, Hlynur Gretarsson, Liviu Hozoi, Stephen D. Wilson
Abstract: NaRuO$_2$ forms a delafossite-like structure that contains triangular sublattices of edge-sharing RuO$_6$ octahedra. It shows no evidence of magnetic order down to 100~mK and persistent spin fluctuations, suggestive of a quantum disordered magnetic ground state. In order to characterize the physical regime from which this disordered state arises, we use resonant inelastic X-ray scattering (RIXS) and X-ray absorption spectroscopy (XAS) at the Ru-$L_{2,3}$-edge, along with pulsed high-field magnetization to characterize both the local electronic structure and the magnetic interactions. We infer significant spin-orbit coupling from XAS measurements. With RIXS, we observe intraorbital excitations, which are damped due to the near itinerancy of the band and suggestive of strong charge fluctuations. Corroborated by models of the high-field magnetization to a random singlet model, we propose a picture of a nearly itinerant system with strong magnetic and charge fluctuations that destabilize long-range magnetic order.

### Paper [271]
Title: Fractionalized Fermi liquids with the ghost-Gutzwiller Ansatz
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12410
Authors: Ivan Pasqua, Carlos Mejuto-Zaera, Michele Fabrizio
Abstract: Fractionalized Fermi liquids ($\mathrm{FL}^{\!*}$), elusive metallic states characterized by fractionalized quasiparticles alongside conventional ones and defying Luttinger's theorem, are prime candidates for the pseudogap regime of underdoped cuprates. We show that a $\mathrm{FL}^{\!*}$ phase emerges in the single-band $t$-$J$ model through a simple ghost-Gutzwiller Ansatz, optimized at the cost of a mean-field calculation. The resulting temperature-doping phase diagram encompasses a low-doping $\mathrm{FL}^{\!*}$, a $d$-wave superconducting dome, and an overdoped conventional Fermi liquid, thereby reproducing key qualitative features of cuprate phenomenology.

### Paper [272]
Title: Thermo-elastic properties of hydrated epoxy-graphene nanocomposites from ensemble-based molecular dynamics simulations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15301
Authors: Maxime Vassaux, Werner A. M\"uller, James L. Suter, Alexandros Anastasiou, Martin Simmons, David Tilbrook, Peter V. Coveney
Abstract: Epoxy-based materials are inherently hygroscopic, absorbing moisture from the environment, which can significantly alter their short and long-term performance. The presence of graphene is often considered as a potential candidate to act as a microscopic barrier, mitigating the adverse effects of hydration on the matrix. This study investigates the impact of hydration on the glass transition and elastic mechanical properties of epoxy resins and their graphene nanocomposites, focusing on water content up to 5 %wt. Using large-ensemble molecular dynamics simulations, we analyze the temperature-driven glass transition and mechanical response of both neat epoxy and epoxy-graphene systems under varying hydration levels. Our results reveal a distinct threshold at 3 %wt water content: below this, hydration primarily reduces the glass transition temperature, while mechanical properties remain unaffected. Beyond 2 %wt, however, the mechanical properties deteriorate, highlighting a non-linear sensitivity to water uptake. Furthermore, we emphasize the critical role of ensemble size in ensuring the reliability of molecular dynamics predictions for such heterogeneous systems. Our simulations demonstrate that ensembles substantially larger than current state-of-the-art standards are necessary to achieve converged distributions of the predicted mechanical properties, particularly in highly heterogeneous hydrated epoxy-graphene nanocomposites. These findings provide novel insights into the hydration behavior of epoxy-based materials and underscore the limited potential of graphene to enhance their environmental resistance. This work also advances the understanding of structure-property relationships in polymer nanocomposites, offering guidance for the design of more robust materials in humid environments.

### Paper [273]
Title: Statistical mechanics of a three-body Hopfield model with finite connectivity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17234
Authors: Yushi Sugawara, Koji Hukushima
Abstract: A three-body Hopfield model defined on sparse random graphs with finite mean connectivity is studied using a replica-symmetric (RS) analysis. By extending the functional replica framework for conventional two-body finite-connectivity Hopfield models, self-consistent equations for the local-field distribution are derived and solved numerically by population dynamics. At low memory load, the RS analysis shows a discontinuous transition between paramagnetic and retrieval states, accompanied by coexistence of distinct fixed points and an upper spinodal of the retrieval solution. Starting the RS population dynamics from an uninformative finite-amplitude field distribution also leads to a spin-glass-like (SG-like) fixed point at low temperatures, even though the paramagnetic fixed point remains locally stable. As the number of embedded patterns increases, the retrieval solution disappears, while the SG-like RS solutions are found at high load. However, the local instability and thermodynamic inconsistencies of the low-temperature RS solutions indicate the limitations of the RS description of the glassy regime. Finite-size Monte Carlo simulations using population annealing agree well with the RS description of the retrieval branch during heating but exhibit glassy trapping behavior under cooling. Such trapping can occur even for a single embedded pattern, where conventional cross-talk noise among stored patterns is absent, indicating the importance of sparse connectivity and three-body interaction. The characteristic number of embedded patterns separating different solution regimes grows approximately linearly with the mean connectivity, corresponding to finite characteristic load ratios. These results clarify how sparse connectivity and many-body interactions influence the stability and accessibility of retrieval states in associative memory.

### Paper [274]
Title: Statistical properties of quadrangular surfaces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19221
Authors: Hrant Topchyan, Rudik Badalyan, Ara Sedrakyan
Abstract: We investigate the statistical properties of random quadrangular surfaces generated by different randomization procedures: the Gruzberg-Kl\"umper-Nuding-Sedrakyan (GKNS) construction, and two newly introduced generalizations of dynamical triangulations (DT), dynamical (DQ) and general quadrangulations (GQ). We formulate these surfaces within a unified graph-theoretic framework and establish the relationships between the elementary operations defining the different ensembles. For GKNS surfaces, we demonstrate that the construction is equivalent to two mutually constrained percolation processes and determine the associated critical point and critical exponents, revealing deviations from ordinary percolation. For DQ and GQ, we analyze the underlying Markov chains and determine the scaling of mixing and relaxation times. We further analyze all three ensembles through their degree distributions, degree correlations, distance statistics, and Hausdorff dimensions. While DQ exhibits exponentially decaying degree distributions and geometric properties similar to DT, GKNS and GQ display broad, scale-free degree distributions. Moreover, GKNS surfaces possess an asymptotic Hausdorff dimension $\Delta_H\approx 2$, whereas DQ and GQ approach $\Delta_H\approx 4$, similarly to DT. This indicates that DQ is compatible with the universal behavior of DT, while GKNS and GQ define distinct classes of random geometry, implying a different underlying measure in the space of random surfaces and a possible geometric framework for a new class of noncritical string theories.

### Paper [275]
Title: Magnetic Modulation of Ionic Transport in a Multistate Channel Model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23239
Authors: Tina Arabi, Ehsan Noruzifar
Abstract: We study the magnetic modulation of ionic transport in an idealized multistate channel model. The channel can occupy different structural states, each with its own ionic current and magnetic response. We assume quasi-equilibrium Boltzmann populations for the structural states, while ionic transport is driven by an externally imposed electrochemical potential difference. Using a statistical-mechanical linear-response approach, we relate changes in the average current to magnetic perturbations of the structural states. The response is determined by the covariance between the state-dependent ionic currents and their magnetic-response coefficients, which controls both its sign and magnitude. We also examine the magnetic and thermal sensitivities, illustrate the results with a three-state model. Order-of-magnitude estimates suggest that the predicted effect for a single channel is extremely small at millitesla magnetic fields. Overall, the results provide a quantitative criterion for assessing magnetic modulation of transport within this idealized model and may help in considering possible effects in real ion channels and other multistate transport systems.

### Paper [276]
Title: Hidden symmetry at the diabolical points of a biaxial spin
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26308
Authors: Shadan Ghassemi Tabrizi
Abstract: In a rotated frame the biaxial spin Hamiltonian $k_1S_x^2+k_2S_y^2-\mathbf{h}\cdot\mathbf{S}$ is a finite tight-binding chain whose hopping amplitudes are tuned by the applied field. A chain with no vanishing hopping has a nondegenerate spectrum, so a degeneracy can occur only where the field severs the chain. At every point of the diabolical-point lattice proven exact by Ke\c{c}ecio\u{g}lu and Garg, the chain is severed in two different rotated frames, at two bonds that are labeled independently. We write the two severings as two spectral-cut projectors. These are explicit polynomials in two field-independent spin components; they commute with the Hamiltonian at that point but not with each other. This gives a concrete form to the hidden symmetry anticipated by Garg: on the degenerate subspace the two projectors generate a pseudospin SU(2) that commutes with the Hamiltonian. The rank of a pairing operator built from them gives the multiplicity of every lattice point, without the continuity and topological argument used previously. Because the two partners of a doublet occupy disjoint stretches of the chain, the splitting determinant factorizes exactly and has a fixed negative sign. Every degeneracy of the model is therefore a linear cone, all cones have the same orientation, and the lower level carries Chern charge $-1$ for the Berry connection $i\langle\psi|\nabla\psi\rangle$.

### Paper [277]
Title: Non-Hermitian Random Matrix Theory of Jamming in Active Disordered Media
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26406
Authors: Hisao Hayakawa
Abstract: We develop a non-Hermitian random-matrix description of active jammed systems in which a passive Wishart operator is perturbed by non-reciprocal Ginibre couplings. Within a Hermitized self-consistent Born approximation (SCBA), we derive a scalar susceptibility equation predicting \(J\sim\alpha_0^{-6/7}\) at the marginal point. Direct random-matrix calculations confirm that the mechanically relevant response is governed by singular values rather than complex eigenvalues. They further reveal strong finite-size fluctuations: near marginality, a large fraction of the total susceptibility is typically carried by the smallest singular value. These results distinguish the smooth large-\(N_c\) SCBA response from the extreme singular-value statistics of finite non-Hermitian systems.

### Paper [278]
Title: Local B-site chemistry controls oxygen-vacancy energetics in Ca-Ce-Ti-Mn perovskites for thermochemical hydrogen production
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28752
Authors: Manish Kumar (Washington University in St. Louis), Natalia Ali (Arizona State University), Matthew D. Witman (Sandia National Laboratories), Shang Zhai (The Ohio State University), James E. Miller (Arizona State University), Ivan Ermanoski (Arizona State University), Ellen B. Stechel (Arizona State University), Robert B. Wexler (Washington University in St. Louis)
Abstract: Two-step thermochemical water splitting driven by concentrated solar heat is a scalable route to renewable hydrogen, but it requires oxides whose oxygen-vacancy formation energies balance facile reduction with favorable reoxidation. Perovskite solid solutions can tune this balance, but the relationship between bulk stoichiometry and local defect energetics remains poorly understood. Here we map oxygen-vacancy formation energetics across Ca-Ce-Ti-Mn (CCTM) perovskites by combining first-principles calculations with a coverage-constrained special quasirandom structure approach that realizes all fifteen symmetry-distinct oxygen nearest-neighbor environments, an interpretable crystal-feature model whose fitted coefficients directly encode the underlying Born-Haber thermochemistry, and a fine-tuned defect graph neural network. Local B-site chemistry dominates the oxygen-vacancy formation energy $E_\mathrm{v}$: varying the nearest-neighbor Mn fraction shifts $E_\mathrm{v}$ by 1.0-1.5 eV depending on local Ce content, whereas A-site Ce variation contributes a smaller, Mn-dependent shift of 0.2-0.6 eV. Short-range B-site cation order, if it can be established and kinetically retained through processing, is therefore a candidate means of tuning redox performance without changing bulk composition. Composition-space maps identify a Ce/Mn-balanced region (~29-33% Ce on the A site and 58-67% Mn on the B site) combining a high fraction of vacancy sites within the targeted $E_\mathrm{v}$ window with phase stability and solubility, whose predicted redox cycle capacity matches or exceeds the ceria benchmark at 1350 $^\circ$C rather than the roughly 1600 $^\circ$C ceria requires. Measurements on three CCTM compositions show cycle capacity increasing monotonically with Ce content under protocols close to the model conditions. The design rules are expected to transfer to related perovskite families.

### Paper [279]
Title: Emergence of Bogoliubov Fermi Surfaces in hybrid Al/InAs heterostructures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06553
Authors: S. Feyrer, V. Dimic, I. Lobato, A. Kirchner, P. Drexler, L. Rupp, D. Bougeard, T. Lindemann, S. Gronin, G. Gardner, M. J. Manfra, G. F. R. Ruiz, C. A. Balseiro, L. Arrachea, M. Aprili, N. Paradiso, C. Strunk, L. Tosi
Abstract: We investigate the microwave electrodynamics of a proximitized two-dimensional electron gas in hybrid superconductor/semiconductor heterostructures. Using lumped-element resonators with inductor wires oriented relative to an in-plane magnetic field, we directly probe the superfluid stiffness via the kinetic inductance. As the field increases, the resonance frequency exhibits a non-monotonic and strongly anisotropic evolution that cannot be explained by orbital pair breaking alone. We show that this behavior is consistent with the emergence of Bogoliubov Fermi surfaces, which selectively suppress the supercurrent response depending on the direction of the magnetic field. Microscopic calculations of the stiffness tensor capture the observed anisotropy driven by the interplay of Zeeman and orbital Fulde-Ferrell effects. Our results establish microwave stiffness measurements as a sensitive probe of anisotropic gapless superconductivity in hybrid systems.

### Paper [280]
Title: Magneto-optical signal from $\mathrm{Co_2Mn}$-based Heusler thin films in MOKE and BLS
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07052
Authors: Anna Maria Friedel, Nicolas Fermon, Tobias B\"ottcher, S\'ebastien Petit-Watelot, St\'ephane Andrieu, Philipp Pirro
Abstract: $\mathrm{Co_2Mn}$-based Heusler compounds offer a versatile, composition-tunable platform for magnonics and spintronics. Among them, the half-metallic $\mathrm{Co_2MnSi}$ is of particular interest for magnonics owing to its ultralow Gilbert damping, yet its weak magneto-optical response in the visible challenges optical probing such as Brillouin light scattering (BLS). We study the magneto-optical response of epitaxial $\mathrm{Co_2Mn}X$ films ($X = \{\mathrm{Al}_x\mathrm{Si}_{1-x}, \mathrm{Ga}_x\mathrm{Ge}_{1-x}, \mathrm{Sn}\}$) by magneto-optical Kerr effect (MOKE) spectroscopy and BLS. Angle-resolved MOKE resolves a significant, wavelength-dependent quadratic MOKE (QMOKE) only for $\mathrm{Co_2MnSi}$, whereas $\mathrm{Co_2MnAl}$, $\mathrm{Co_2MnGa}$ and $\mathrm{Co_2MnSn}$ respond dominantly linearly. Comparing BLS intensities of thermal magnons at two wavelengths, $\mathrm{Co_2MnSi}$ gives the weakest signal at 532 nm yet among the strongest at 457 nm, tracking the spectral dependence of its Kerr angle. These results emphasise the relation between the two magneto-optical techniques, guiding the choice of probing wavelength for $\mathrm{Co_2Mn}$-based Heusler compounds, and provide a transferable approach for other magnetic materials.

### Paper [281]
Title: Fubini-Study Speed and Short-Time Identification of Bosonic Instability
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12406
Authors: A. M. Tishin
Abstract: We ask what short-time quantum distinguishability reveals about instability in quadratic bosonic systems. For a fixed autonomous quadratic Hamiltonian, the infimum of the Fubini-Study speed over centered pure Gaussian inputs equals the Euclidean norm of the positive real parts of the flow eigenvalues divided by the square root of 2. The infimum is attained exactly when the flow generator is diagonalizable. We also derive the minimum speed under a finite squeezing budget for a detuned parametric amplifier. For a lossy single-mode amplifier with a common phase-insensitive Markovian bath, two return-fidelity traces, from the bare vacuum and a calibrated squeezed vacuum, determine the growth margin, damping, and diffusion from their initial slopes and curvatures. Finite-window simulations illustrate how the traces distinguish cases on opposite sides of the instability threshold.

### Paper [282]
Title: Local molecular motions encode time-resolved infrared spectra of proteins
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12914
Authors: Emanuel Dorbath, Peter Hamm, Gerhard Stock
Abstract: Time-resolved infrared spectroscopy probes protein dynamics over timescales spanning more than ten orders of magnitude, yet the molecular motions underlying the observed kinetic signatures have remained elusive. Here we combine transient infrared spectroscopy with nonequilibrium molecular dynamics simulations to establish a direct connection between experimental relaxation times and local structural motions. Studying single-domain allosteric proteins, we find that inter-residue contact distances provide the structural representation that most faithfully reproduces the experimental dynamics. Correlation analysis identifies localized networks of coordinated contacts that mediate long-range coupling between secondary-structure elements. The characteristic timescales of these contact networks quantitatively match the experimentally observed relaxation processes, enabling each kinetic step to be assigned to a specific molecular motion. Applied to allosteric signal propagation in PDZ3 and photoinduced perturbation of the binding pocket of PDZ2 or a ligand binding to PDZ2, this framework provides an atomistic picture of hierarchical protein relaxation and establishes a general framework for connecting transient infrared spectroscopy with the molecular mechanisms of protein dynamics.

### Paper [283]
Title: Unconventional Pressure Evolution of Spin-Density-Wave State in La$_{3}$Ni$_{2}$O$_{7}$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17505
Authors: Xiaoxiang Zhou, Shiyu Xie, Liangxin Qiao, Hengyuan Zhang, Jun Shu, Rui Liu, Mengwu Huo, Deyuan Hu, Hengjie Liu, Chuansheng Hu, Ge He, Zeming Qi, Meng Wang, Dong-Lai Feng, Zengyi Du
Abstract: The discovery of pressure-induced high temperature superconductivity in the bilayer nickelate La$_{3}$Ni$_{2}$O$_{7}$ has raised the question of how its spin-density-wave (SDW) state evolves toward the superconducting regime. Here, we report a systematic electronic Raman study of La$_{3}$Ni$_{2}$O$_{7}$ single crystals under hydrostatic pressures up to 16.51~GPa. Both the SDW gap energy and the transition temperature $T_{\mathrm{SDW}}$ show an overall increase with pressure, while the dimensionless coupling ratio $2\Delta_{\mathrm{SDW}}/(k_{\mathrm B}T_{\mathrm{SDW}})$ remains approximately pressure independent, indicating a robust strong-coupling character of SDW state. At the same time, the SDW-related Raman peak broadens as pressure is applied, indicating the enhanced damping of the associated electronic excitation. These results reveal an unusual pressure evolution in which the SDW energy scale is enhanced while the SDW state becomes progressively less coherent, providing spectroscopic constraints on the magnetic correlations relevant to superconductivity in bilayer nickelates.

### Paper [284]
Title: Room-Temperature Polarity Control of the Anomalous Nernst Effect in a High-Magnetic-Anisotropy Topological Nodal-Line MnAlGe
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18462
Authors: Nanhe Kumar Gupta, Keisuke Masuda, Masaaki Kakoki, Benugopal Bairagya, Satoki Tazawa, Weinan Zhou, Hirofumi Suto, Ryo Toyama, Akio Kimura, Yuya Sakuraba
Abstract: Controlling the polarity of anomalous Nernst thermopower is a promising strategy for enhancing the performances of thermoelectric applications. However, realizing such control at room temperature (RT) in topological ferromagnets with high magnetic anisotropy (Ku) remains challenging. Here, we report RT polarity control of the anomalous Nernst effect (ANE) in quasi-two-dimensional nodal-line MnAlGe epitaxial thin films through Al/Ge compositional tuning while preserving robust high Ku. This polarity reversal originates from intrinsic Berry curvature contributions modulated by sublattice-selective carrier doping, as supported by spin-resolved electronic band structure analysis and hard X-ray photoemission spectroscopy. To demonstrate the practical feasibility, we also fabricated a meander-structured device combining MnAlGe with positive and negative polarity enhanced the thermoelectric output. Our results demonstrate that tuning the Fermi level relative to the nodal-line electronic structure while preserving high Ku enables controllable ANE polarity reversal within a single material, providing a route toward RT transverse thermoelectric devices.

### Paper [285]
Title: Exact expressions of correlation functions between two spins in the boundary row of the two-dimensional rectangular Ising model with periodic-free boundary conditions and finite size
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19274
Authors: Mei Tao
Abstract: We present exact expressions of correlation functions between two spins in the boundary row of the two-dimensional rectangular Ising model with periodic-free boundary conditions and finite size. Some properties of exact expressions obtained are discussed. The fundamental property of the Ising model that the long range order emerge as the temperature decrease is shown clearly; expressions of the correlation functions in the thermodynamic limit varies depending on the order of taking limits of two parameters L and N; the impact of different sizes on correlation functions is illustrated with the aid of diagrams. Expressions of the correlation function discussed in this paper in the thermodynamic limit has been presented by previous researchers, and we prove that expressions obtained in this article is identical in form to expressions provided by previous researchers in the thermodynamic limit.

### Paper [286]
Title: Topologically protected zero-reflection nodes for perfect crossed Andreev reflection
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26702
Authors: Abhiram Soori
Abstract: Generating non-locally entangled electron pairs via Cooper-pair splitting is vital for solid-state quantum information processing. However, isolating the underlying crossed Andreev reflection (CAR) is challenging due to competing transport processes like electron tunneling (ET) and local Andreev reflection (AR). Here, we propose a flux-tunable four-terminal normal metal-superconductor junction that achieves deterministic, 100\% efficient CAR. We demonstrate that at exactly half a magnetic flux quantum ($\phi=\pi$), exact destructive Aharonov-Bohm and Peierls interferences structurally forbid ET and AR, respectively. By tuning the central junction hopping, electron reflection is also suppressed to zero. Using the Cauchy argument principle, we prove that this zero-reflection node is topologically protected by a quantized winding number in parameter space. When combined with the precise spatial symmetries of the junction, this exact suppression guarantees a unit CAR probability. We establish that this regime is characterized by a strictly positive cross-correlation shot noise, providing an unambiguous experimental signature of Cooper-pair splitting. Furthermore, this perfect CAR is nearly broadband within the superconducting gap. While generic structural disorder breaks the spatial symmetries required for the exact cancellation of ET and AR, the crossed Andreev reflection--though no longer perfectly quantized --remains highly dominant under weak disorder, offering a resilient architecture for deterministic nonlocal entanglement generation.

### Paper [287]
Title: Field-free precessional magnetization dynamics in a multidomain magnet driven by light-induced strain
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29245
Authors: H\'elo\"ise Damas, Carl S. Davies, Tomasz Zalewski, Petr M. Vetoshko, Vladimir I. Belotelov, Andrzej Stupakiewicz, Andrei Kirilyuk
Abstract: Ultrafast lattice excitation offers a route to controlling magnetization through magnetoelastic coupling, yet its ability to drive large-amplitude dynamics within pre-existing multidomain states remains less explored. Here, we show that a single 5-ps mid-infrared pulse induces a large-amplitude, reversible magnetic response in cobalt-doped yttrium iron garnet at zero applied magnetic field. Time-resolved magneto-optical microscopy reveals a transient reversal of the out-of-plane magnetic contrast within approximately 2 ns, followed by recovery of the initial state, while the labyrinthine domain morphology remains largely preserved. Concurrent polarization microscopy reveals a localized, long-lived strain-sensitive response and a propagating acoustic wave. The correlated structural and magnetic responses, together with micromagnetic simulations, support a mechanism in which transient strain modifies the magnetic anisotropy and drives precessional reorientation within existing domains. These findings establish single-pulse magnetoelastic excitation as a route to reversible, large-amplitude magnetization dynamics without substantial domain reorganization or an externally applied magnetic field.

### Paper [288]
Title: FrOGS: Discrete Neural Sampler for Independent Alloy Configurations Across Chemical Conditions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02948
Authors: Kyucheol Min, Elyssa Hofgard, Tess Smidt
Abstract: Predicting the thermodynamic properties of an alloy requires sampling its configurations across many chemical conditions and recovering free energies on a common absolute scale. Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) is the standard tool, but it requires separate simulations at different conditions, and auxiliary free-energy methods such as thermodynamic integration are used to place results on a common absolute scale. Modern discrete neural samplers typically use reverse KL divergence as the objective and can be mode-seeking or biased. We present Free energy Offering Generative Sampler (FrOGS), a hybrid discrete neural sampler that couples an autoregressive model to a continuous-time Markov chain (CTMC) to be trained jointly under a single shared loss. FrOGS draws i.i.d. configurations, returns an unbiased estimate of the partition function, and gives consistent estimates of thermodynamic observables. We train a single model across a wide range of chemical conditions to produce estimates on a common absolute free-energy scale. FrOGS matches exact finite-size results on the 2D Ising model and reference phase diagrams for AgPd and CuAu, without mode collapse. We additionally compare to SEGAL, a published autoregressive baseline, and find that only FrOGS recovers the stability range of the CuAu$_3$ phase.

### Paper [289]
Title: Simultaneous measurements of enzyme propagation and biopolymer mobility elucidate the directional degradation of a dense biopolymer matrix
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03792
Authors: Vincenzo Ruzzi, Antoine Bouchoux, Carole Antoine-Assor, Donna-Joe Bigot, Salma Menzeh, Maike Petermann, Laurent Leclercq, Herv\'e Cottet, C\'edric Montanier, Claire Dumon, Luca Cipelletti, Laurence Ramos
Abstract: Enzymatic degradation of dense biopolymer matrices is central to natural processes like digestion and cancer progression, as well as applications in biomedicine, biomass valorization, and nanotechnology. Yet, it remains unclear how enzyme propagation and substrate degradation interact, and especially whether and how enzymatic activity shapes the propagation process. Here, we bridge this gap by employing Fluorescence-Photon Correlation Imaging to simultaneously track directional enzyme propagation and biopolymer degradation in a model system. We use viscoelastic solutions of arabinoxylan, a hemicellulosic polysaccharide with a xylan backbone substituted by arabinose and ferulic acid, and a fluorescently labeled arabinoxylanase, an enzyme that randomly cleaves the xylan chain. To resolve enzyme propagation and biopolymer degradation with spatiotemporal precision, we combine fluorescence imaging of the enzyme with speckle-based photon correlation imaging, a technique to quantify biopolymer mobility, which we show is a proxy for biopolymer degradation. Our experiments reveal that enzyme diffusion is decoupled from catalytic activity, with no evidence of activity-enhanced diffusion. Instead, the progression of the degradation front is governed by a combination of enzyme diffusion, reaction kinetics, and slow enzyme deactivation, the latter being characterized by a double-exponential decay. By extending the Michaelis-Menten framework to space- and time-dependent conditions, we quantify how cumulative enzyme exposure and deactivation shape the degradation dynamics. This work establishes a quantitative framework to understand enzymatic processes in concentrated, beyond-dilute-regime substrates, with implications for the rationalization of biocatalytic systems in industrial and biomedical applications.

### Paper [290]
Title: de Almeida-Thouless Stability and Replica Symmetry Breaking in a Hybrid Watermarking Model with Image and Message Decoding
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06405
Authors: Tatsuya Uezu, Kao Hayashi, Masaki Kawamura
Abstract: We investigate the de Almeida-Thouless (AT) stability of the replica symmetric (RS) solution for a spread-spectrum
  watermarking model incorporating Bayes-estimation-based image restoration. In our previously proposed
  model [1], which simultaneously performs message decoding and image restoration under Gaussian
  channel noise, a discrepancy between the theoretical predictions of the RS solution and Markov chain Monte Carlo
  (MCMC) simulations was observed in specific parameter regimes. In this paper, by analyzing the blind case where both the original
  message and image are unknown, we explicitly derive the conditions for the AT stability of the RS solution
  by clarifying the structure of the replicon eigenspace for the hybrid system. Our
  results
  reveal that AT stability is broken precisely in the parameter region where the RS solution deviates from
  MCMC simulations, thereby successfully explaining the mechanism underlying this discrepancy
  in terms of replica symmetry breaking (RSB). [1] M. Kawamura, T. Uezu, K. Hayashi, and M. Okada, ``Statistical mechanical evaluation of a spread-spectrum watermarking model with image restoration,'' Phys. Rev. E \textbf{99}, 062132 (2019).

### Paper [291]
Title: Curvature-Enhanced Polarization in Transition-Metal Dichalcogenide Janus Nanowrinkles
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08295
Authors: Stefan Velja, Surender Kumar, Domenico Corona, Caterina Cocchi
Abstract: Strain-gradient engineering via out-of-plane wrinkling offers a powerful route to tune electronic and electromechanical properties in two-dimensional (2D) materials. Here, we systematically investigate the electronic and polarization response of wrinkled Janus MoSSe/MoSeS monolayers using density functional theory (DFT) calculations coupled with continuum elastica modeling. Exploring varying wrinkle sizes and compressive strain levels ($5\%$ -- $20\%$), we show that the global out-of-plane polarization follows a linear behavior when parameterized by the projected aspect ratio of the nanowrinkles. On this basis, we develop a physically grounded geometric model incorporating an effective 2D electromechanical coupling coefficient, which accurately predicts DFT polarizations without requiring higher-order nonlinear parameters. Atom- and orbital-resolved charge density analyses reveal the microscopic origin of this behavior: while the central Mo $4d$ projected weight remains approximately constant, local curvature drives continuous, chemically asymmetric charge transfer between the S $3p$ and Se $4p$ sublayer manifolds. Our findings establish clear geometric design rules for harnessing polarization in Janus monolayers for flexible nanoelectronics and optoelectronics.

### Paper [292]
Title: Trajectory Statistics Govern Mechanical Power Transfer in Active Baths
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17047
Authors: Chul-Ung Woo, Jiwon Choi, Heiko Rieger
Abstract: In active media, a moving passive object can experience negative drag and receive positive mechanical power from the bath, in contrast to the dissipative response in equilibrium. For a probe moving at velocity $\mathbf{V}$ and interacting weakly with an ideal active bath, we show that free-particle trajectory statistics determine the density response and the resulting bath force. The probe--particle interaction sets the wave-vector weighting, while the imposed motion selects frequencies $\omega_{\mathbf q}=\mathbf q\cdot \mathbf V$, separating bath dynamics from probe properties. The sign of the response spectrum yields probe-independent constraints for mechanical power transfer. Applying this framework to representative active-particle models reveals distinct power-transfer behavior despite identical displacement variances. For prescribed probe motion, the relation predicts drag reversal, motion-induced density distortions, and velocity-dependent friction, in quantitative agreement with simulations. Beyond the mean force, the free-particle trajectory statistics also determine the force autocorrelation. These results provide a route to predict the mechanical response of probes from single-particle trajectory statistics measured in the absence of the probe.

### Paper [293]
Title: Lyapunov-controlled thermalization: an exact real-time example
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20597
Authors: Jonas Loy, Jan C. Louw
Abstract: A verified Kubo-Martin-Schwinger (KMS) relation, after a drive has ceased, is not sufficient evidence to prove equilibrium. We demonstrate this in a large-$N$ large-$q$ Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev (SYK) quench protocol. Although all post-quench fermion correlators are exactly thermal, a second quench back to the initial Hamiltonian reveals hidden memory of the initial state. It is encoded in correlators connecting to times before the first quench. This memory is an extensive nonequilibrium (NEQ) witness with its decay rate being the Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_L$. Thus $\lambda_L$ sets the rate at which the state becomes effectively indistinguishable from a Gibbs state. Despite being a unitary interacting many-body system, the complete NEQ real-time evolution is obtained exactly in the large-$N$, large-$q$ limit, making the setup ideal for analytically studying thermalization.

### Paper [294]
Title: Magnetic Quadrupole Lens and Current-Induced Quadrupolar Dynamics of Antiskyrmion
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23129
Authors: Linfeng Du, Yang Liu, Peng Yan, Ying Su
Abstract: We demonstrate that antiskyrmions can serve as magnetic quadrupole lenses that focus or defocus electric currents traversing them. We showcase the antiskyrmion core as a focal point where electron beams converge for specific injection directions, leading to anisotropic and nonuniform current distributions. Thus the current-induced antiskyrmion dynamics is profoundly modified by the magnetic quadrupole lensing via the spin-transfer torque. By incorporating quantum transport and micromagnetic simulations iteratively, we self-consistently capture the coupled dynamics of conduction electrons and antiskyrmions. Our results unveil a quadrupolar antiskyrmion Hall effect that is highly anisotropic with respect to the current direction and can be effectively tuned by the spin-orbit coupling. In particular, the current focusing (defocusing) dramatically enhances (suppresses) the antiskyrmion speed while simultaneously reducing its Hall angle, thereby overcoming a key challenge for antiskyrmion racetrack memories. Our work establishes the intrinsic current lensing as a powerful mechanism for manipulating topological magnetic textures and opens new avenues for antiskyrmion-based spintronics.

### Paper [295]
Title: Coupled Electronic Structure and Phonon Transport Driven by Bonding Reconstruction in Ba-B-C Clathrates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32894
Authors: Ghulam Hussain, Dario Massa, Muhammad Usman, Rajibul Islam, Magdalena Birowska, Carmine Autieri, Xiaoguang Li
Abstract: Composition-driven changes in electron count, structural symmetry and bonding environment can couple the electronic and thermal responses in a material. Here, with density-functional theory and machine-learned interatomic potentials (MLIPs), we compare cubic BaB3C3 with its B-C substituted tetragonal counterpart, BaB2C4. The additional valence electron and accompanying change in crystal symmetry drive a transition from metallic BaB3C3 to semiconducting BaB2C4 and the formation of short/strong C-C bonds. The reconstructed bonding network hardens the phonon spectrum, with the maximum frequency increasing from 800 to 900. At room temperature, the in-plane lattice thermal conductivity increases from 7.6 to 18.7 W/mK, while the thermal transport alters from isotropic to anisotropic for BaB2C4. This enhancement is associated with the strengthened bonding network, higher phonon frequencies/group velocities, longer lifetimes and larger mean free paths. Machine learning potentials accurately reproduced the DFT-level energies and forces with negligible errors, and the harmonic and anharmonic features are benchmarked against explicit first-principles calculations. Our findings demonstrate that minimal B-C substitution provides a viable strategy to link composition- and structure-associated bonding reconstruction with correlated electronic and phononic alterations.

### Paper [296]
Title: Intrinsic spin-orbit coupling in graphene stacks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05905
Authors: Guillermo Parra-Martinez, Alejandro Jimeno-Pozo, Ady Stern, Jose Angel Silva-Guill\'en, Francisco Guinea
Abstract: The effect of spin-orbit coupling (SOC) in monolayer graphene has been thoroughly studied in the past. Its impact is commonly disregarded since the electronic structure of graphene is almost unaffected. Consequently, SOC has also been ignored in other graphitic systems. In this work, we calculate the intrinsic SOC in stacks of graphene layers from a model that includes all the valence orbitals of carbon as well as the intra-atomic SOC.The leading spin-orbit term in a graphene multilayer combines a Rashba-like spin-flip process with interlayer hopping. We find that this intrinsic term modifies the phase diagram and Fermi surfaces of finite rhombohedral stacks, and it leads to a distinct density-dependence of the magnetization in weak magnetic fields. This Letter shows that, unlike in the single-layer case, SOC has important consequences in multilayer graphitic systems and it should not be neglected when studying their physical properties.

### Paper [297]
Title: Nodal magnon spin current at an antiferromagnet/altermagnet junction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39206
Authors: Koki Mizuno
Abstract: We investigate the thermally driven magnon spin current across a junction between a $d$-wave altermagnet (AM) and a ferromagnet (FM) or an antiferromagnet (AFM). The current consists of a component independent of the interface angle and an angularly dependent component with the $d$-wave form. For a sublattice-symmetric interface, the two junctions behave oppositely under reversal of the altermagnetic domain: in the FM/AM junction the angularly dependent component is odd and cancels on averaging over the domains, whereas in the AFM/AM junction the angularly independent component vanishes by symmetry and the angularly dependent component survives the domain average at a fixed N\'eel vector of the AFM. Both follow from a selective tunneling: a pair of spins bonded across the interface transmits magnons when the spins are parallel and not when antiparallel, which makes the AFM/AM junction a magnon valve: the configuration in which the bonded spins are antiparallel blocks the current up to the sublattice admixture of the magnons. We reveal the uniqueness of the altermagnet by comparing with the anisotropic antiferromagnet. The anisotropic tunneling is detectable as the sign reversal of the current between the interfaces normal to $[310]$ and $[130]$, and the valve reads the N\'eel vector of the altermagnet relative to that of the antiferromagnet through the magnitude of the current at a fixed interface.

### Paper [298]
Title: Influence of pairing and correlations on transport properties in three-terminal superconducting nanostructure
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40059
Authors: G. G\'orski, V. Pokorn\'y, T. Doma\'nski
Abstract: We study the local and nonlocal charge transport properties of three-terminal nanostructure, consisting of the correlated quantum dot embedded between two metallic electrodes and superconductor. Using the linear response theory, we analyze interplay of the induced on-dot pairing with the correlation effects, inspecting their influence on the conductance and thermopower. Our calculations predict negative values of the nonlocal conductance and reversal of the Seebeck coefficient when the quantum dot ground state is represented by the BCS-type singlet configuration. These phenomena are realized when the subgap charge transport is predominantly contributed by the crossed Andreev scattering. In contrast, on the doublet side, we obtain signatures for the Kondo effect manifested by the low-temperature enhancement of the charge conductance and clearly evident in the thermopower. Such temperature-dependent local and nonlocal transport properties can unambiguously identify the specific configuration of the correlated quantum dot and reveal the rich physics emerging in the doublet-to-singlet crossover region.

### Paper [299]
Title: 10 kV $\beta$-Ga$_2$O$_3$ Sub-Bandgap Triggered Impurity Photoconductive Semiconductor Switches (i-PCSSs): Lessons for Optimal Design
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41049
Authors: Imteaz Rahaman, Hunter Ellis, Afrouz Taherian, Seyedeh Shadi Mirmohammadi, Botong Li, Berardi Sensale Rodriguez, Michael A. Scarpulla, Kai Fu
Abstract: $\beta$-Ga$_2$O$_3$ photoconductive semiconductor switches (PCSSs) are attractive for high-voltage operation, but above-bandgap triggering requires deep-ultraviolet excitation. Here, we investigate a 650-um-thick, 0.01% Fe-doped $\beta$-Ga$_2$O$_3$ impurity PCSS with a 5-mm lateral gap under continuous-wave (CW) 808-nm sub-bandgap excitation at biases up to 10 kV ( 20 kV cm^-1). The device exhibited clear sub-bandgap photoconductive switching. As the optical power density increased from 0.64 to 3.18 W cm^-2, the ON-state current increased progressively while the ON-state resistance decreased, consistent with enhanced defect-assisted photocarrier generation. A higher applied bias further strengthened the switching response through enhanced field-assisted carrier transport. The ON/OFF ratio increases with optical power density but exhibits a nonmonotonic bias dependence, reaching approximately 15-16 near 5-7 kV. At 10 kV, increased OFF-state conduction and non-ideal transient behavior reduce the switching contrast. These results demonstrate near-infrared photoconductive switching but also highlight critical trade-offs in photoconductive thresholds for impurity states.

### Paper [300]
Title: Universal model of the growth of III-V tetrahedral semiconductors on polar surfaces: pyramids and glue atoms
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41559
Authors: Pawel Strak, Stanis{\l}aw Krukowski
Abstract: Ab initio extensive calculations were used to simulate the atomic stages of the vapor growth of III-V tetrahedral semiconductors (GaN, GaP, GaAs and GaSb) from their components. The structural and electronic properties of the stoichiometric polar surfaces of III-V tetrahedral semiconductors (GaN, GaP, GaAs and GaSb), i.e. metal- and nonmetal-terminated were determined. Adsorption of the components was simulated, shown to be atomic. At low coverages adatoms occupy H3 sites, considerably stabilizing the surfaces, at high coverage, the adatoms are located in the skewed on-top positions creating the first layer necessary for growth (with the exception of nitrogen admolecules). The second adlayer is adsorbed differently: on a Ga-terminated surface it does not create pyramid structures due to strong interaction within the first NM adlayer, thus preventing effective growth. On NM terminated Ga covered surface the second NM adlayer atoms create a stable pyramid structure which is an effective vehicle for surface diffusion. Growth of III-V semiconductors occurs via the attachment of the pyramids to steps by glue atoms, i.e. additionally attached NM adatoms. The NM glue atom connection process requires additional electrons, which leads to strong dependence of the process and the entire growth on the Fermi level at the NM terminated surface

### Paper [301]
Title: Universal model of the growth of III-V tetrahedral semiconductors on polar surfaces: pyramids and glue atoms. II. Adsorption of nonmetal atoms (N, P, As, and Sb) on Ga-terminated surfaces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41571
Authors: Pawel Strak, Stanis{\l}aw Krukowski
Abstract: Ab initio calculations were used to simulate the adsorption processes on Ga-terminated surfaces of tetrahedral semiconductors: GaN, GaP, GaAs and GaSb. The considered adsorbates are nonmetal species, i.e., nitrogen, phosphorus, arsenic, and antimony on wurtzite gallium nitride, i.e., GaN(0001) surfaces, zinc blende gallium phosphide, i.e., GaP(111), gallium arsenide, GaAs(111), and gallium antimonide, GaSb (111) surfaces. The cases investigated include single adsorbed nonmetal atoms and full coverage, i.e. theta=1 of these surfaces. The calculated results include the stable sites and the energies of the single adsorbed atoms: located at H3 sites at low coverage and in on-top positions at high coverage for GaP, GaAs and GaSb. At high nitrogen coverage, the N2 admolecules are formed, weakly attached to the surface. The calculated electronic properties of the single-adatom systems include the band diagrams of the slabs in real and momentum space, the energies of the quantum states. In addition, the overlaps of the quantum states of the nonmetal adatoms and the nearest topmost surface Ga atoms are given. The full coverage data include the atomic configurations and the bond energies of the adsorbed layers. The electronic properties include the above-mentioned data for single adatoms, together with additional properties related to interactions between adsorbed atoms. The evaporation stability analysis confirms that the adatoms stabilize the topmost Ga atoms against detachment because of the high energy barrier of DE > 3 eV which is further increased to DE > 4 eV by coverage located at H3 sites. Thus, growth simulations should employ unreconstructed Ga-terminated surfaces covered by nonmetal atoms located at on-top positions.

### Paper [302]
Title: Thermal conductivity of Lennard-Jones fluids from equilibrium thermodynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41952
Authors: Miguel Hoyuelos, Alejandro Al\'es
Abstract: We derive a general expression for the thermal conductivity of simple fluids within a thermodynamic transport theory (TTT) in which the Onsager coefficients of an interacting system are related to those of an ideal reference system through the ratio of the determinants of the Hessians of the relevant thermodynamic potential. The transported quantity is identified with an effective energy density proportional to the rms energy fluctuations of a small fluid cell. The result expresses the thermal conductivity as the product of its dilute-gas value and a function of equilibrium thermodynamic properties, namely the specific heats at constant density and at constant $\rho T$ and the temperature derivative of the latter, with no adjustable parameters. For hard spheres, it reduces to a previously verified expression in terms of the thermodynamic factor. The theory is tested against molecular dynamics results for the Lennard-Jones (LJ) and the truncated and shifted Lennard-Jones (LJTS) fluids in the supercritical region, for densities up to $\rho^*=0.8$, using equations of state based on perturbation theory. For the LJTS fluid, the mean absolute percentage error is below 8\% for all the temperatures considered. For the LJ fluid, the predictions are compared with those of the revised Enskog theory for Mie fluids, using the same equation of state: TTT gives smaller errors at almost all temperatures, with a significant improvement at low temperatures, where the errors are reduced approximately by a factor between two and three.

### Paper [303]
Title: Stable AgBiS2 near-infrared absorbers for thin film tandem photovoltaics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01797
Authors: Shiling Dong, Yongjie Wang, Hae-Jun Seok, Mengyuan Huang, Edward Newton, Der Ying Chen, Yixin Wang, Xiaoyu Guo, Keisuke Takemoto, Dusan Ivanovic, Avari Roy, Yongge Yang, Grey Christoforo, Rebekah Fraser, Seongrok Seo, John O'Sullivan, Ha Kyung Park, Jack M. Woolley, Ji-Seon Kim, Ruy S. Bonilla, Henry J. Snaith, Qing Shen, Aditya Sadhanala, Lydia H. Wong, Robert L. Z. Hoye
Abstract: AgBiS2 has rapidly risen in efficiency as single-junction photovoltaics (PVs), but its potential for tandem PVs is unexplored. Herein, we perform optical modeling based on the experimental absorption spectrum of AgBiS2, revealing that, as the NIR sub-cell, efficiencies exceeding 47.9% are achievable in triple-junction tandems, superseding tandems using Pb/Sn perovskite NIR cells (44.2%). However, fulfilling this potential requires overcoming the limited transport properties of AgBiS2 nanocrystal films, which constrains film thicknesses to only 30-50 nm, severely restricting NIR response. A solution-phase ligand exchange process is herein developed, allowing one-step deposition of passivated AgBiS2 nanocrystals that maintain efficient charge-carrier extraction despite an order of magnitude increase in film thickness. These films enable 24.0%-efficient four-terminal tandems with visible-light-absorbing lead-halide perovskite sub-cells, and monolithic tandems successfully demonstrating voltage addition. AgBiS2 PVs have high stability, retaining >90% of the original efficiency after 3 months of storage in dry air without encapsulation. This work establishes the significant potential of AgBiS2 as a stable and scalable NIR absorber for tandem PVs.

### Paper [304]
Title: Universal model of the growth of III-V tetrahedral semiconductors on polar surfaces: pyramids and glue atoms. VI. Surface diffusion on a nonmetal-terminated surface
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02723
Authors: Pawel Strak, Stanis{\l}aw Krukowski
Abstract: Ab initio calculations were used to simulate surface diffusion of adsorbates on the nonmetal-terminated surface of tetrahedral semiconductors: GaN, GaP, GaAs and GaSb. These surfaces are wurtzite gallium nitride, i.e. GaN(000-1) surface, zinc blende gallium phosphide, i.e. GaP(-1-1-1) gallium arsenide, GaAs (-1-1-1) and gallium antimonide GaSb (-1-1-1) surfaces. The mixed coverage consists of Ga adatoms in the first layer with a nonmetal atom attached above them forming a triangular-based pyramid. The pyramidal structures are stable during surface jumps involving rotations around one of the basal Ga adatoms. Thus, the diffusion proceeds via rotation (pivot) of the pair of the other basal Ga adatoms around an immobile Ga adatom moving them into new, originally empty sites. Using an energy-path determination procedure, the diffusion barriers were obtained as the difference between the initial site energy and the activated complex state energy. The detachment energy of the NM adatom was obtained assuming a final state in atomic and molecular form. The energy barrier for detachment to the atomic state is high for all atoms, on the order of 5 eV. For the molecular state these barriers are different: smaller for nitrogen, on the order of 1 eV, which is related to the high dissociation energy of the N2 molecule. For P and As the barrier is above 2 eV. For Sb it was not determined. Accordingly, the surface diffusion length is macroscopically large, indicating that all NM adatoms arrive at the steps.

### Paper [305]
Title: Universal model of the growth of III-V tetrahedral semiconductors on polar surfaces: pyramids and glue atoms. VII. Step kinetics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02735
Authors: Pawel Strak, Stanis{\l}aw Krukowski
Abstract: Ab initio calculations were used to simulate the kinetics of attachment of new segments of the crystalline lattice (pyramids) at steps on the nonmetal-terminated surface of tetrahedral semiconductors: GaN, GaP, GaAs and GaSb. These surfaces are wurtzite gallium nitride, i.e. GaN(000-1) surface, zinc blende gallium phosphide, i.e. GaP(-1-1-1) gallium arsenide, GaAs(-1-1-1) and gallium antimonide GaSb(-1-1-1) surfaces. Diffusion species are gallium adatoms at high coverage or gallium adatoms connected with nonmetal atom creating stable pyramids. These pyramids arrive at the steps, and cannot be attached be directly because the Ga adatom at the pyramid base cannot be attached directly. Therefore they are incorporated into the step by additional nonmetal atoms glue atoms arriving from the vapor or detached from other segments of the surface terraces.

### Paper [306]
Title: The maximum packing fraction of disordered bidisperse discs: theory and predictions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02805
Authors: Raphael Blumenfeld
Abstract: Predicting the highest packing fraction that disordered packing of bidisperse discs can achieve, $\phi_{max}$, is an important unresolved problem. Such a predictive theory is hindered by the density dependence on the packing protocol and by ensuring disorder. In bidisperse packings, this is more complicated because $\phi_{max}$ is not a number but a function of the disc species size ratio, $D$, and one of the disc species concentration, $p$. These difficulties have been overcome in monodisperse disc packings by using the cell order distribution (COD)~\cite{Bl21}. Focusing on size ratios that prohibit rattlers, a COD-based theory is constructed here. It introduces bidisperse statistical closure equations, which combine an explicit disorder criterion with consistent cell and edge statistics, and yields $\phi_{max}(p,D)$ and structural predictions. The a-priori disorder-guaranteeing criterion proposed here ensures fast exponential a decay of the occurrence probability of ordered clusters with size. Using a constrained maximum-entropy analysis to ensure maximum disorder, the occurrence probabilities of all the possible cell and edge configurations are derived in terms of $p$ and $D$ and they are found to be binomial. All the results are assembled to yield an exact prediction of $\phi_{max}(p,D)$.

### Paper [307]
Title: Thermodynamic Bethe Ansatz for the Toda chain from inverse scattering transform
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03857
Authors: Alberto Brollo
Abstract: The Thermodynamic Bethe Ansatz (TBA) procedure developed by Yang-Yang provides the free energy functional to be minimized via a variational problem. Their result not only gives the correct thermodynamics, but it also sets the framework for Generalized Hydrodynamics. Originally developed for quantum systems, this procedure has been recently successfully applied to (integrable) classical particle systems. However, this has never been done for classical field theories: no classical derivation has consistently accounted for solitons, breathers, radiation (dispersive modes), and their mutual interactions. We here derive the TBA equations for the Toda chain directly from its inverse scattering transform. This results in solitonic modes interacting with radiation, extending the soliton gas picture. We provide numerical verification of our equations against well established TBA equations in terms of particle modes. We expect the same procedure should apply to KdV, and pure radiative models as defocusing NLS and Sinh-Gordon, while we discuss current limitations in other models such as focusing NLS (and Ablowitz-Ladik) and Sine-Gordon.

### Paper [308]
Title: Prediction of antimagnon-polarons with intrinsic momentum-space level attraction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07434
Authors: Chongzhou Wang, Zhengda Jing, Weichao Yu
Abstract: Propagating hybrid quasiparticles formed by magnons and phonons (magnon-polarons) or photons (magnon-polaritons) typically exhibit level repulsion in momentum space. Level attraction, by contrast, requires an anti-phase channel and has so far been restricted to zero-dimensional systems mediated by dissipative baths. Here we predict the antimagnon-polaron in a continuous magnetoelastic medium, which inherently exhibits momentum-space level attraction. The spin-orbit-torque (SOT)-stabilized antimagnon precesses with opposite handedness, directly reversing the magnetoelastic coupling without requiring an external environment. This intrinsic attraction yields a distinctive 0/{\pi} phase-locked simultaneous growth. Micromagnetic simulations confirm this picture, revealing that the SOT-controlled balance between gain and damping drives a collective instability. This ultimately enables a continuously tunable, dc-pumped, single-frequency phonon laser, establishing a novel route for coherent acoustic emission.

### Paper [309]
Title: Devitrification: crystal nucleation and growth mechanisms in glasses
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08667
Authors: Max Brown, Peter Wolynes
Abstract: If a liquid is cooled rapidly, it may fall out of equilibrium before it can crystallize. It may, however, crystallize later from the nonequilibrium glassy state. Using the Random First-Order Transition (RFOT) theory of glasses, we discuss a variety of mechanisms of crystallization as a route to devitrification. Ordinary nucleation is sped up by the frozen in energy of the glass relative to the liquid. At sufficiently low temperatures, a mechanism of nanonucleation appears, producing nanometer scale crystal inclusions in the glass. Nanocrystallization depends strongly on the local fluctuations in stability, and the transition from classical nucleation and growth to nanocrystallization occurs dominantly through rare, less stable glassy regions that percolate through space, producing an additional dramatic speedup in the crystal nucleation and growth rates. Similar fluctuations in local stability also explain anomalies of crystal growth in deeply supercooled systems.

### Paper [310]
Title: Discovery of Kondo-Kramers Nodal Line Topological State in strongly correlated material CeAuAl$_3$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10368
Authors: Hari Bhandari, Kevin Allen, Kuan-Sen Lin, Yuan Fang,  Arushi, Karthik Rao, Mounica Mahankali, Sanu Mishra, Jian-Xin Zhu, Christopher Lane, Qimiao Si, Emilia Morosan
Abstract: Kramers nodal lines (KNLs) are predicted in all achiral, non-centrosymmetric crystal structures, and yet there are very few experimental realizations of KNLs, only in weakly correlated systems. Especially, their realization in strongly correlated systems remains unexplored. Here we investigate KNL topology in the non-centrosymmetric, achiral Kondo lattice CeAuAl$_3$, using magnetotransport, thermodynamic measurements, density functional theory (DFT), and tight-binding toy model calculations. We find that CeAuAl$_3$ hosts KNLs in the $\mathit{\Gamma}\text{-}Z$ and $X\text{-}P$ directions, protected by time-reversal and mirror symmetries. Breaking of these crystalline symmetries, along with time-reversal symmetry via spin polarization, lifts the KNL degeneracy, which, in turn, generates Berry curvature resulting in an intrinsic anomalous Hall effect (AHE). More importantly, CeAuAl$_3$ shows large AHE not only below the magnetic ordering temperature $T_N$ but rather below the Kondo temperature. This underscores the role of the Kondo correlations in bringing the KNLs at the Fermi surface rather than at accidental energies, as would be expected without correlations. Our work identifies a new topological state, Kondo-Kramers Nodal Line, with CeAuAl$_3$ being the first compound explicitly identified to host this new correlated topological state, and demonstrating the expected AHE associated with it. More generally, our work outlines the pathway for finding Kondo-KNL systems in all compounds that combine achiral, non-centrosymmetric structure with Kondo correlations.

### Paper [311]
Title: Enhancing valley splitting in Si/SiGe Wiggle Well quantum dots through integrated stressor-gate architectures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12850
Authors: E. S. Joseph, Talise Oh, D. E. Savage, D. S. Stone, DeAnna Campbell, Benjamin D. Woods, Mark Friesen, M. A. Eriksson
Abstract: Engineering large and reproducible valley splittings is essential for the scalable operation of silicon spin qubits. In Si/SiGe quantum dot devices, valley splitting arises predominantly from atomic-scale disorder and is therefore inherently random, motivating approaches that introduce a large deterministic contribution. Strain-assisted Wiggle Wells provide a route to deterministic valley splitting by combining an oscillatory Ge concentration in the Si well with shear strain; however, generating the required strain in a manner compatible with large-scale architectures remains a challenge. Here, we propose patterned stressed thin films as a scalable approach for generating shear strain in the quantum well of Si/SiGe quantum dot devices. Finite-element simulations show that compressively stressed TiN films can generate shear strains exceeding 0.1% at the quantum well, sufficient for deterministic valley splittings of hundreds of $\mu \text{eV}$ in strain-assisted Wiggle Wells. We show that this strain can extend across multiple quantum dots and is maintained in realistic gate geometries, providing a fabrication-compatible approach for scaling deterministic valley splitting to quantum dot arrays.

### Paper [312]
Title: Geometry-Selective Higher-order topological insulators induced by $d_{xy}$ altermagnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13438
Authors: Siyuan Cheng, Yuee Xie, Xiaosen Yang, Yuanping Chen
Abstract: We show that proximity to a $d_{xy}$-wave altermagnet provides an effective route to engineering boundary-geometry-dependent higher-order topological phases. The boundary geometry plays a crucial role in determining the topological properties: axis-aligned edge states remain gapless, whereas diagonal edges acquire effective masses with alternating signs, giving rise to four corner modes localized at the mass domain walls. By projecting onto the edge-state subspace, we derive the effective edge Hamiltonians for different boundary geometries. Furthermore, $\pi$-flux defects introduced under periodic boundary conditions yield zero-energy bound states localized at the flux cores, providing complementary evidence for nontrivial topology without physical boundaries. The interplay between the Zeeman field and the altermagnetic order drives a topological transition through the closing and reopening of the edge gap, while the bulk gap remains open. These findings highlight the crucial role of boundary geometry in higher-order topology and establish proximity-induced $d_{xy}$-wave altermagnetism as a route to engineering higher-order topological phases.

### Paper [313]
Title: Edge quantum weight of finite Chern flakes and its topological floor
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14271
Authors: Rohit Narula
Abstract: The zero-point energy of an $LC$ circuit measures the polarization fluctuation of an insulator coupled to it. In a crystal this fluctuation is the bulk quantum weight $K_{\rm bulk}$, which the Chern number $C$ bounds below by $|C|$. We show that a finite Chern flake adds an edge term that survives as the flake grows. The low-frequency transitions of its chiral edge have squared dipole matrix elements that sum to an edge weight $K_{\rm edge}\ge |C|$ per unit area, a floor that follows from the positive absorption of both circular polarizations and the known cancellation of circular dichroism between edge and bulk. At weak coupling, which a growing flake eventually leaves, a circuit slower than the edge modes reads $K_{\rm bulk}+K_{\rm edge}$, averaged over two field directions, and a faster one approaches $K_{\rm bulk}$, so the reading depends on whether the flake is made large or the circuit slow first. In exact free-fermion calculations on lattice flakes with $|C|=1$ and 2 the summed circular dichroism of the edge sector extrapolates to $|C|$ in magnitude, so that the floor is $|C|$. Above the floor, each edge channel adds a number fixed by the boundary shape and its edge velocities, which square and rectangular lattice flakes with one channel follow to within 0.003, while the circular dichroism counts only the net chirality. The avoided crossing of the circuit with the edge mode gives the edge weight in units of the Hall conductance quantum, up to a geometric factor of the capacitor.

### Paper [314]
Title: Thermality from a Rindler quench
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.04584
Authors: Jorma Louko
Abstract: Ultracold fermionic atoms in an optical lattice, with a sudden position-dependent change (a quench) in the effective dispersion relation, have been proposed by Rodr\'iguez-Laguna et al. as an analogue spacetime test of the Unruh effect. We provide new support for this analogue by analysing a massless scalar field on a $(1+1)$-dimensional continuum spacetime with a similar quench: an early time Minkowski region is joined at a constant time surface, representing the quench, to a late time static region in which left and right asymptotically Rindler domains are connected by a smooth negative curvature bridge. We show that the quench is energetically mild, and late time static observers, modelled as a derivative-coupling Unruh-DeWitt detector, see thermality, in a temperature that equals the Unruh temperature for observers in the asymptotic Rindler domains. The Unruh effect hence prevails, despite the energy injected into the field by the quench and despite the absence of a late time Killing horizon. These results strengthen the motivation to realise the experimental proposal.

### Paper [315]
Title: Macroscopic diffusive fluctuations for generalized hard rods dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.13037
Authors: Pablo A. Ferrari, Stefano Olla
Abstract: We study the fluctuations in equilibrium for a dynamics of rods with random length. This includes the classical hard rod elastic collisions, when rod lengths are constant and equal to a positive value. We prove that in the diffusive space-time scaling, an initial fluctuation of density of particles of velocity $v$, after recentering on its Euler evolution, evolve randomly shifted by a Brownian motion of variance $\mathcal D(v)$.

### Paper [316]
Title: Symmetries, correlation functions, and entanglement of general quantum Motzkin spin-chains
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16070
Authors: Varun Menon, Andi Gu, Ramis Movassagh
Abstract: Motzkin spin-chains, which include `colorless' (integer spin $s=1$) and `colorful' ($s \geq 2$) variants, are one-dimensional (1D) local integer spin models notable for their lack of a conformal field theory (CFT) description of their low-energy physics, despite being gapless. The colorful variants are particularly unusual, as they exhibit power-law violation of the area-law of entanglement entropy (as $\sqrt{n}$ in system size $n$), rather than a logarithmic violation as seen in a CFT. In this work, we analytically discover several unique properties of these models, potentially suggesting a new universality class for their low-energy physics. We identify a complex structure of symmetries and unexpected scaling behavior in spin-spin correlations, which deviate from known 1D universality classes. Specifically, the $s=1$ chain exhibits $U(1)$ spontaneous symmetry breaking and ferromagnetic order. Meanwhile, the $s \geq 2$ chains do not appear to spontaneously break any symmetries, but display quasi-long-range algebraic order with power-law decaying correlations, inconsistent with standard Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless (BKT) critical exponents. We also derive asymptotic scaling expressions, with explicit additive constants, for entanglement measures in both colorless and colorful chains, generalizing previous results of Movassagh [J. Math Phys. (2017)], while providing benchmarks for potential quantum simulation experiments. The $\sqrt{n}$ entanglement scaling implies that any matrix-product-state description of these ground states requires bond dimension $e^{\Omega(\sqrt{n})}$; this superpolynomial cost of the standard 1D tensor-network toolkit, along with the analytical tractability of their ground state properties, positions Motzkin spin chains as intriguing testbeds for quantum simulation of many-body physics.

### Paper [317]
Title: Interface Energy and Phase Transformations: A Comparative Analysis of Cahn-Hilliard and CALPHAD-based Models in Ternary Substitutional Alloys
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16430
Authors: Wolfgang Flachberger, Thomas Antretter, Swaroop Gaddikere-Nagaraja, Silvia Leitner, Manuel Petersmann, Jiri Svoboda
Abstract: Diffusional phase transformations in alloys are modeled either by phase-field methods of Cahn-Hilliard type, in which a double-well free energy and a gradient term impose an interface energy, or by reactive diffusion models, in which the driving forces follow from the CALPHAD methodology, the convex hull of the molar free energy, and the interface carries no energy. Their numerical schemes are tied to their free energies: phase-field schemes break down when the interface energy is set to zero, and reactive diffusion codes rely on finite differences. We derive, from the thermodynamic extremal principle, a single finite element scheme that covers both limits: the stationarity of the Rayleighian functional (free energy rate plus one half of the dissipation, with mass conservation imposed by multipliers) with respect to the rates, fluxes and multipliers at a frozen state. Fluxes and chemical affinities are primary unknowns, so no derivative of the affinity is required. The time-discrete scheme is the corresponding incremental minimization; it conserves mass exactly and satisfies a discrete energy-dissipation inequality for every convex molar free energy, including the convex hull without regularization. We make explicit that this last case is a degenerate parabolic problem of Stefan type, whose degeneracy can be removed, without altering the free energy, only by a moving mesh; since our aim is the coupling to continuum mechanics and damage on a fixed mesh, we operate at the boundary of degeneracy and show that mass conservation, the energy balance and the second law remain intact there. Thermodynamic consistency is proved for multi-component systems, a scheme relating Onsager and diffusion coefficients is proposed, and the role of interface energy is studied in binary and ternary examples, the latter with vacancies as a non-conserved component.

### Paper [318]
Title: High-frequency dispersive readout of a superconducting qubit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.09161
Authors: Pavel D. Kurilovich, Thomas Connolly, Charlotte G. L. B{\o}ttcher, Daniel K. Weiss, Sumeru Hazra, Vidul R. Joshi, Andy Z. Ding, Heekun Nho, Spencer Diamond, Vladislav D. Kurilovich, Mykola Kishmar, Wei Dai, Valla Fatemi, Luigi Frunzio, Leonid I. Glazman, Michel H. Devoret
Abstract: Driving a low-dissipation, non-linear system periodically can destabilize it by exciting indirect resonances, which occur whenever an integer multiple of the drive frequency matches an integer combination of the system's mode frequencies. Avoiding such resonances is a central design constraint across physics and engineering, from radio-frequency ion traps to particle accelerators. Remarkably, the measurement of quantum bits based on superconducting circuits has so far remained limited by non-linear resonances. Measurements of qubit states are customarily performed in a regime where the frequency of the microwave readout tone is comparable to that of the circuit. In that case, the drive excites resonances that eject the qubit from its computational subspace. This limits the readout power and hence the achievable measurement fidelity. We show that a readout tone with frequency much larger than that of the circuit circumvents this problem by exponentially suppressing the resonances. Using a readout tone with frequency 12 times larger than that of our transmon qubit, we achieve a quantum non-demolition measurement fidelity of $99.931\pm0.003\%$, with an error an order of magnitude smaller than the previous record.
